# Source expansion register — 2026-10-03

Produced for Oscar at his instruction, under his rule: **verify the document exists and contains what we
think before valuing it; never speculate about cost; tag the real observed access requirement and the worth.**

Eleven scouts searched: nine endpoints plus a regulatory/CMC sweep and an open-licence sweep, each reading
its own branch first so leads are aimed at measured gaps. Every lead is tagged `VERIFIED_EXISTS` or
`LEAD_UNVERIFIED`; access is recorded only as actually observed. **Nothing here is ingested, promoted or
scientifically admitted** — these are acquisition candidates for Rocksteady, gated as every other round is.

## Headline: no purchase decision is pending

**Zero leads, across eleven scouts, were `PAYWALLED_VERIFIED`.** Not one price, purchase page or payment
requirement was observed anywhere. Every access failure was a bot wall, a rate limit, an egress block, a 403
or a transport error. What genuinely needs Oscar's decision is **redistribution terms**, not money — see §3.

## 1. Honest assessment

**Short answer: this expansion genuinely moves two of the three things that matter, does not move the third, and the scouting process wasted roughly half its own effort on duplication. Volume is not the story and I am not going to present it as one.**

**1. Molecule count — yes, it really does move, but not where you might assume.** There is genuine H1 here, not padding: Lidberg's human PT-MPS (up to 10 PMO/PSO oligomers with printed sequences, including a base-sequence-matched PMO/PSO pair that isolates backbone chemistry from sequence — a contrast the dataset contains nowhere); Wave's HD and C9-ALS patient-derived motor neuron panels (~14 and a further series, sequences in supplements); Kim 2023's 89 ASOs in human fibroblasts and whole blood under CC BY; Soule's ~26 aptamers and Yu's ~12 in human plasma; Idera's ~97 in human PBMC with per-position chemistry; CpG 1018 as a single clean new molecule dosed to >9,000 humans with complete positional backbone chemistry by construction. That is hundreds of constructs in human systems with sequences attached, which is exactly the priority class the brief names.

But be clear-eyed about what kind of molecules they are. Almost all are **research-grade tool compounds and aptamers in plasma or iPSC systems**, not clinical candidates. They raise construct count and chemistry diversity; they do not raise the count of molecules meeting the full qualifying bar, and Beebop is right that the ~25-molecule figure is endpoint-specific until a deduplicated project-wide union exists. If the November narrative implies hundreds of *qualifying* molecules on the strength of this register, that will not survive review.

**2. Mandatory fields — H2 moves decisively, H3 does not move on the number that matters.** H2 is the real win and it is well verified: GSRS for ~30 molecules, Kim 2023's per-position legend, three patents with printed notation legends, and a free re-parse of INN PDFs already on disk. That takes the top-ranked mandatory field from near-zero to substantial coverage on the held roster.

H3 is where I have to be blunt, and where seven scouts independently arrived at the same wall. **No source retrieved by anyone, including me, yields a drug-substance release purity value for any approved oligonucleotide.** The three numeric findings each fail for a documented reason, and all three would be errors if ingested naively:
- Imetelstat 71.2–71.6% (US 11,332,489 B2): I read the patent — these are **crude product composition before preparative RP-HPLC purification**. In-process, not release.
- Nusinersen 4.1–6.7% impurities (PMDA): deliberately **impurity-enriched toxicology batches**, not product.
- Givlaari NMT 0.5% / NMT 1.0% / NMT 20.0 area % / 100 EU/ml (I verified these myself): genuine published numbers, and they refine the consensus that EMA prints nothing — but they are **acceptance limits and an identification threshold, not a measured purity**.

So `purity_pct` will stay largely empty, and the honest Phase 2 move is the one the thrombocytopenia branch already models: populate `purity_method` and `identity_confirmation` fully and source-cited (this register can take them from ~0 to roughly 12–15 approved compounds), and record `purity_pct` as **withheld-with-evidence** — quoting the (b)(4) redaction counts, the EMA confidentiality note, the PMDA asterisk masking. That satisfies the Challenge's explicit "record missingness" requirement far better than an unexplained TBD, and it is a materially stronger disclosure than a silent blank. It is not the same as having the data, and the narrative must not blur the two.

**3. Rows on compounds already held — yes, a substantial share, and here is which.** The registry material is the clearest case: the AE and outcome tables for inotersen, nusinersen, mipomersen, volanesorsen, eplontersen, viltolarsen and patisiran all attach to molecules the project already holds. They are still worth taking, but for three specific reasons that are *not* volume: they supply **denominators** the existing rows lack, they supply **raw continuous values** where the dataset holds curator binaries, and they are **public domain** where the incumbent sources are paywalled NEJM and Circulation papers. That is H4, H5 and H6 — a change in evidence *shape*, which is what the register's critics have been asking for. If these land as "+900 rows" in a status report rather than "denominators and measured zeros on compounds we already model", they will have been mis-sold.

**4. The process finding, which I think is the most actionable thing in this report.** Eleven scouts produced heavy duplication: seven chased the same EMA EPAR family, seven independently rediscovered the purity ceiling, five independently rediscovered the accessdata user-agent fix (while a sixth reached the opposite conclusion and left a false "blocked" assertion in the kidney register), four chased GSRS, eight mined ClinicalTrials.gov with at least six trial-level collisions, and two CNS lineages are now duplicating acquisition as well as curation. Against the measured backdrop — **197 sequences already in two or more endpoint datasets with no crosswalk, and 10 rows inflating to 28 across files** — this is the same failure mode moving upstream from the data into the sourcing. The fix is cheap and structural: acquire each of the eleven duplicate families **once**, centrally, keyed on UNII and NCT, and publish the extraction to the endpoints rather than letting nine sessions each fetch the same PDF. Exactly one scout stood down on noticing another agent's downloads in the shared scratchpad; that should be the default, not an accident.

**5. One correction you need to see before anything ships.** A scout proposed NCT03358030 as *"the only candidate that satisfies every clause of H4"* and read its 200 mg arm as the project's first clean clinical negative. The banded platelet data is real and the scout's numbers were exact — but I pulled the same trial's adverse-event module and it reports `frequencyThreshold: '5'` with `Thrombocytopenia` at **4/53, 6/54 and 7/50 against 0/53 placebo**, plus a serious immune thrombocytopenic purpura and a cerebral haemorrhage. That molecule is a dose-graded **positive**. Had it shipped as the first clean negative it would have been a third citation-grade error on a project already damaged twice. It remains valuable — as excellent H5 and as the positive control to sit opposite DEVOTE — but it is not what it was offered as. That is precisely why this register separates what was *retrieved* from what was *concluded*.

**6. What you are not being asked for: money.** Zero leads across eleven scouts and my own verification are PAYWALLED_VERIFIED. Not one price, purchase page or payment requirement was observed anywhere. Every failure was a bot wall, a rate limit, an egress block or a transport error. The decisions genuinely waiting on you are about **redistribution terms** — PMDA's All Rights Reserved, TGA's no-redistribution clause, the time-limited Elsevier grant, and the ND-licensed material carrying real payload — plus one schema decision (a stereochemistry column) and one free housekeeping win (dropping the 687 `verbatim_quote` cells that put 30,885 words of publisher prose inside a file meant to ship openly).

## 2. Highest-value single find

**The NCATS/FDA GSRS substance API (register row 1)** — and I rank it first on the project's own criteria rather than on novelty.

Why it most changes the submission: Phase 2 is judged on mandatory PER-OLIGO fields, and H2 (modification positions) is joint-highest-ranked and almost entirely empty — 0 of 27 clinical compounds in coagulopathy, 0 of 12 in complement, 0 of 4 of the unresolved intrathecal CNS compounds, 0 of 65 position-resolved in kidney. GSRS closes it for roughly 24–30 held molecules in one scripted pass, in machine-readable form, from the agency running the Challenge. I verified three records myself and the per-position data is real and precise: eplontersen resolves to `MOE 1_1-1_5;1_16-1_20`, `dR 1_6-1_15`, `P 1_3-1_6;1_17-1_18`, `sP 1_2;1_7-1_16;1_19-1_20` — a mixed phosphorothioate/phosphodiester backbone located linkage by linkage, which is precisely the distinction the project's central scientific question turns on and which no paper in the corpus supplies.

It is also the only lead that is simultaneously highest-ranked, zero-cost, zero-access-risk, public-domain, redistributable, machine-readable, and cross-endpoint (six endpoints from one script). Nothing else on the register has that profile.

And it delivered something no scout anticipated, which I confirmed directly: GSRS carries **per-linkage phosphorothioate stereochemistry**. Rovanersen returns `'Phosphorothioate R-isomer' -> 1_13` and `'Phosphorothioate S-isomer' -> 1_1;1_5-1_12;1_14-1_15`. These are stereopure molecules, the project's schema has no stereochemistry column, and recording them as plain `full_PS` would be silently, unrecoverably wrong. That is a schema finding worth more than most of the rows on this register, because it is the kind of error that survives review and poisons a model.

**Runner-up, with a distinct role: NCT04089566 (DEVOTE), register row 4.** It is the strongest H4 I could verify, and H4 is the one absence with a named consequence — the lead scientist is blocking a classifier on it and the project has zero. I confirmed every element: AE `frequencyThreshold: '0'` so absences are measured zeros, prespecified **PRIMARY** platelet and coagulation endpoints rather than an incidental safety table, traceable per-arm denominators (6/40 and 25/50/8/16), and nusinersen's sequence and per-position chemistry already held so no new chemistry work is needed. GSRS changes the most cells; DEVOTE unblocks the most work. Take both in week one — they do not compete for the same hands.

## 3. Decisions for Oscar

- NO PURCHASE DECISION IS PENDING. Across all eleven scouts and my own verification, ZERO leads were tagged PAYWALLED_VERIFIED, because not one price, purchase page, or payment requirement was observed anywhere. Every access failure was a bot wall, a rate limit (EMA HTTP 429), an egress-policy block, a 403, a 503, or a transport error. The existing RESEARCH_ACCESS_REGISTER already reached the same conclusion: 'No new request is classified as confirmed in this update. The existing kidney list reports paywalls, but spot checks found free alternatives.' Oscar should not be asked to fund anything on this register. The three items below are observed REUSE/REDISTRIBUTION requirements, which are genuine decisions but are not costs.

- REDISTRIBUTION DECISION 1 — PMDA review reports (register row 13). Observed, quoted: the PMDA English site states 'Copyright (c) Pharmaceuticals and Medical Devices Agency, All Rights Reserved.' The documents themselves are free to retrieve (viltolarsen 1,634,124 bytes; tofersen 1,567,366 bytes, both HTTP 200, no login) and each carries the disclaimer 'This English translation ... is intended to serve as reference material ... In the event of any inconsistency between the Japanese original and this English translation, the Japanese original shall take precedence.' DECISION NEEDED: confirm that extracted facts may be ingested and cited while the documents are not redistributed, and that the Japanese original is cited as the authority. WORTH: this is the only route to a per-position regulator convention for a PMO, plus a second independent specification for nusinersen. Note the PMDA Site Policy page (/english/0013.html) was NOT fetched by any scout, so a more permissive clause may exist that nobody has read — worth one fetch before the decision.

- REDISTRIBUTION DECISION 2 — TGA Australian Public Assessment Reports (six oligonucleotide AusPARs located by exact filename: nusinersen, patisiran, vutrisiran, givosiran, inclisiran, donidalorsen). Observed, quoted from the TGA copyright policy: 'You are not permitted to re-transmit, distribute or commercialise the material without prior written approval', with requests to tga.copyright@tga.gov.au; reproduction is permitted only as fair dealing under the Copyright Act 1968. No price was observed. The document bodies could NOT be retrieved by any scout (HTTP 503 from WebFetch; curl 'HTTP/2 stream 1 was not closed cleanly: INTERNAL_ERROR' then 'Empty reply from server'), so their Quality-section content is UNVERIFIED. DECISION NEEDED: whether to spend a session retrying from different egress for a fourth regulator's purity specification on nusinersen, knowing the output cannot be redistributed. WORTH: MEDIUM at best, and strictly as a cross-check against the EMA and PMDA nusinersen specifications. THE REAL VALUE HERE IS A POLICY CORRECTION, and it is free: the project is generalising 'regulator document = government work = public domain'. That rule reaches US federal agencies ONLY. EMA permits commercial reuse with attribution, TGA forbids redistribution, PMDA is All Rights Reserved. Write a per-regulator licence table with each term quoted; stop using one basis string for all of them.

- REDISTRIBUTION DECISION 3 — the CC BY-NC-ND and conditional-grant material carrying genuine H1/H2 payload (register rows 6, 14, 15, 20). Observed: PMC11255113 'cc by-nc-nd'; PMC13571576 'cc by-nc-nd'; Soule 2022 carries a TIME-LIMITED Elsevier grant, quoted verbatim: 'These permissions are granted for free by Elsevier for as long as the COVID-19 resource centre remains active'; the Soule 2016 Duke thesis has dc.rights EMPTY, i.e. freely downloadable (9,470,413 bytes, HTTP 200) with no re-use licence at all. No price in any case. DECISION NEEDED: Phase 2 commits to an openly licensed dataset. Oscar must rule on whether extracted facts from ND-licensed sources may ship as derived rows (the thrombocytopenia branch already reasons this way: 'extracted values are facts, not expression'), and whether a time-limited publisher grant may be relied on at all. RECOMMENDATION: adopt the thrombocytopenia basis project-wide, record the Elsevier grant verbatim in sources.csv as a new 'conditional_publisher_grant' value rather than relabelling it CC_BY, and prefer the CC BY siblings where they exist (PMC7870851 for Wave chemistry; Kim 2023 for per-position notation). A free, immediate win sits inside this decision: coagulopathy ships 30,885 words of verbatim publisher prose in the `verbatim_quote` column across 687 restricted rows. The numbers beside it are facts; the quote is the authors' expression. Dropping or hashing that one column on those 687 rows removes the exposure and loses no measurement.

## 4. Free wins — acquire now, no decision needed

- ROW 1 — NCATS/FDA GSRS API (drugs.ncats.io/api/v1/substances(<UNII>)?view=full): public-domain US federal substance registry, HTTP 200, no key, no login. I verified three records myself. ~30 UNIIs = ~30 HTTP GETs for the project's worst-covered mandatory field (H2). Acquire centrally and distribute; do NOT let four endpoints each query it. Two verified limits to carry: imetelstat is registered substanceClass 'chemical' and pegnivacogin 'polymer', so neither carries a nucleicAcid record; and the nusinersen subunit field is internally inconsistent with its own systematic name and with both the EPAR and the repo, so treat that one cell as known-bad and take nusinersen from the EPAR instead.

- ROW 3 — EMA Legal notice (ema.europa.eu/en/about-us/legal-notice): I retrieved and quoted it. Costs nothing, requires no acquisition, and is a pure file edit: it converts 64 coagulopathy rows currently tagged publisher_restricted/unresolved, and repairs the legal basis under 216 thrombocytopenia rows that are justified as 'US Government work' for documents the EMA published. Do this first — it is what makes rows 2, 9 and 18 redistributable rather than merely readable.

- ROW 5 — The threshold-0 ClinicalTrials.gov filter and its verified member trials (NCT00770146, NCT04089566, NCT00707746, NCT01703988, NCT01839656, NCT04306510, NCT01836549, NCT05583552, NCT03728634): public domain, public API v2, no key. I verified frequencyThreshold == '0' on two of them with full denominators. This is a method as much as a source and it is the project's only credible route to H4. Run it once, centrally, over every posted-results trial of every held compound.

- ROW 11 — WHO INN re-extraction: the PDFs are ALREADY ON DISK and already validated base-for-base by scripts/fill_inn_sequences.py. Acquisition cost is literally zero; the only work is a second parse for an unpopulated field. Add rl94 (diranersen/BIIB080) and the unparsed entries in the held rl75/77/81/83/85. State in the write-up that this is a re-extraction of a held source, not a new acquisition — presenting it otherwise is exactly the overstatement that has damaged this project before.

- ROW 17 — Fucini 2012 Supp_Data.pdf (PMC4047996, 251.7 KB): one HTTP GET from pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/instance/4047996/bin/Supp_Data.pdf. The repository currently classes this 'no open route found' and calls it the single highest-value remaining file; that classification is wrong (the per-file REST endpoint 403s, the article page serves it). It is the ONLY outstanding retrieval that releases already-held records. Free to read but publisher-copyright, so it does NOT count toward H6.

- ROW 22 — accessdata.fda.gov, now unblocked: I confirmed the fix with byte counts (default UA -> HTTP 302 / 420 bytes / 'FDA Apology'; browser UA -> HTTP 200 / 2,181,318 bytes / 711-page PDF). Correcting toxicity/kidney/SOURCES.md:12 and SOURCE_REGISTER.md section 2 costs one line each and converts kidney's largest stated dead end into ordinary retrieval work. Free, and it unblocks several endpoints at once — but budget for methods and documented withholding, not purity numbers.

- ROW 7 — Kim 2023 Nature S Table 13 (41586_2023_6277_MOESM4_ESM.xlsx, CC BY, 1,188,914 bytes from static-content.springer.com): openly licensed, bulk-redistributable, and the only true per-position modification legend any scout found. One download. Use the Springer host, not the PMC mirror (which serves a 21,319-byte wall page).

- ROW 8 — US 11,332,489 B2 (Geron, imetelstat): US patent text, public domain, free full text. I read it myself. Closes the only approved compound in the catalogue with neither sequence nor modification map. Free — provided the crude-vs-release purity caveat travels with every row.

- ROW 12 — US 7,884,197 B2 (Idera, TLR9 agonists) and ROW 16 — US 12,421,516 B2 (Roche, PCSK9): both US patents, public domain, free. Together ~143 sequences with per-position notation and printed legends. Row 16 needs the Google Patents HTML because the USPTO PDF has no text layer.

- ROW 18 — EU Clinical Trials Register posted results (EudraCT 2007-004819-54, 2015-001955-54) and the 11-row sponsor-protocol crosswalk: public register, no login, free. Supplies the only numeric human complement values with denominators found anywhere, for an endpoint that holds zero, and removes complement's named registry-identifier blocker.

## 5. Acquire once, centrally — cross-endpoint duplicate families

The duplication the audit found in the *data* has reappeared in the *sourcing*. Acquire each family once,
keyed on UNII and NCT, and publish the extraction to the endpoints rather than letting nine sessions fetch
the same PDF.

- EMA EPAR ASSESSMENT REPORTS — the worst duplication in the set: proposed independently by SEVEN of eleven scouts (kidney, hepatic, coagulopathy, complement, CNS-alternate, XCUT-CMC, XCUT-open-licence). It is ONE document family of ~17 products behind ONE predictable URL pattern. Direct collisions: Givlaari proposed by kidney, coagulopathy(adjacent), XCUT-CMC and XCUT-licence; Onpattro by XCUT-CMC and XCUT-licence; Qalsody by kidney, complement, XCUT-CMC and XCUT-licence; Tegsedi by kidney, hepatic and XCUT-CMC; Spinraza by CNS-alternate, XCUT-CMC and hydrocephalus(adjacent); Leqvio and Oxlumo by coagulopathy and XCUT-CMC. ACQUIRE ONCE: one owner pulls the family, paced against HTTP 429, extracts the Quality sections into a shared characterization table keyed on UNII, and publishes it to all seven endpoints. Four scouts also independently hit the same 429 rate limit and three left identically-sized 40,642-byte 429 error pages in scratchpads mistaken for documents.

- NCATS GSRS / INXIGHT — proposed independently by FOUR scouts (coagulopathy with 24 UNIIs, complement with 8, CNS-original with 7, thrombocytopenia as the recommended route for nedosiran). The UNII sets overlap heavily: eplontersen, inotersen, mipomersen, volanesorsen, olezarsen, alicaforsen, patisiran, givosiran, lumasiran, vutrisiran, nedosiran, eteplirsen, viltolarsen, golodirsen, casimersen, pegaptanib and avacincaptad pegol appear in two or more scouts' lists. ACQUIRE ONCE: one owner pulls every UNII into a single shared modifications table. This is the cleanest acquire-once-share item on the register and the only one that serves six endpoints from one script.

- CLINICALTRIALS.GOV POSTED RESULTS — eight of eleven scouts mined the registry, with specific trial-level collisions: NCT04089566 (DEVOTE) proposed by BOTH thrombocytopenia and coagulopathy, and also relevant to CNS; NCT02211209 (APPROACH) by thrombocytopenia, coagulopathy(held) and XCUT-open-licence; NCT02386553 (NURTURE) by thrombocytopenia and coagulopathy; NCT01737398 / NCT02175004 (NEURO-TTR and its extension) by kidney and thrombocytopenia; NCT04136184 (NEURO-TTRansform) by coagulopathy and CNS-alternate, having been FETCHED AND REJECTED by coagulopathy for a different purpose; NCT04534114 (RE-THINC ESRD, fesomersen) by coagulopathy while thrombocytopenia independently mined NCT03358030 on the same molecule. ACQUIRE ONCE: a single central pull of the posted-results modules for every NCT touching any held compound, with frequencyThreshold stored as a column, keyed to a shared study register. Note that NCT02175004 is an EXTENSION of NCT01737398 and NCT02658175 of NCT02211209 — parent/extension pairs must be declared as nesting edges, never summed.

- THE PURITY CEILING — not a lead but the costliest duplication of all: SEVEN scouts independently burned part of a session rediscovering that numeric oligonucleotide purity is systematically withheld by every regulator (kidney, thrombocytopenia, hepatic, complement, immunotoxicity, CNS-original, XCUT-CMC all reached it separately, each by a different document). FDA redacts as (b)(4) — 56 redactions in the inotersen review, 84 pages withheld in the tofersen review; EMA deletes 'information of a commercially confidential nature'; PMDA masks numeric cells with asterisk runs. PUBLISH THIS AS A SETTLED PROJECT FINDING so no eighth session repeats it, and re-point every recovery_ledger entry that currently aims at FDA CMC for purity.

- IMETELSTAT CHEMISTRY — two different routes to the same gap, from three scouts: thrombocytopenia proposed US 11,332,489 B2 (Geron process patent) while complement and XCUT-CMC independently proposed the Rytelo EMA EPAR (EMA/13310/2025). Both close the sequence AND per-position linkage chemistry for the same molecule, held as TOLG070 in thrombocytopenia and COG-OLG120 in coagulopathy, which is the only approved compound with neither sequence nor map. ACQUIRE BOTH TOGETHER, ONCE, and cross-check them — they are mutually corroborating and I verified the patent side myself.

- PMDA REVIEW REPORTS — exact file-level collisions: file 000237467.pdf (viltolarsen) proposed by BOTH thrombocytopenia and XCUT-CMC; file 000228334.pdf (nusinersen) by BOTH XCUT-CMC and CNS-alternate; file 000277928.pdf (tofersen) by BOTH CNS-original and CNS-alternate. Three documents, five proposals. Kidney additionally named PMDA as its single most promising unreached seam. ACQUIRE ONCE, facts only, subject to the All-Rights-Reserved constraint.

- WAVE LIFE SCIENCES SUPPLEMENTS — PMC11255113 (Iwamoto, stereopure HTT) proposed independently by CNS-alternate and hydrocephalus; hydrocephalus additionally proposed PMC7870851 (C9orf72, CC BY) and PMC9092894 (WVE-004) from the same programme, and CNS-alternate separately proposed the same group's PMC9177980 notation paper. These are one research programme with one shared notation convention and one shared supplementary-file route. ACQUIRE AS A BUNDLE, once, and take the CC BY member (PMC7870851) as the redistributable anchor.

- DE BRUYCKER / CONTROL-ASO PAPER 10.1016/j.omtn.2026.103051 (PMC13571576) — proposed by BOTH CNS-original and CNS-alternate, the two rival nervous-system lineages that oversight has already flagged for consolidation (P4: 'Two parallel nervous-system lineages are duplication, not breadth'). Likewise the review 10.1016/j.omtn.2026.103062 (PMC13560553) was proposed by CNS-original and separately surfaced by CNS-alternate and hydrocephalus. This pair is direct evidence that the duplicate CNS lineage is now duplicating ACQUISITION as well as curation — decide the authoritative lineage before either spends a session.

- KIM 2023 NATURE / MILASEN — CNS-alternate proposed the Nature supplement S Table 13 (CC BY, 89 ASO sequences incl. AT008/atipeksen) while CNS-original independently proposed the milasen clinical report plus patent US 11,999,953 B2 from the same individualised-ASO programme and the same Boston Children's group. The CC BY Nature supplement already supplies the sequence table CNS-original was chasing through the patent, and it also retires CNS-alternate's 'treat as unverified' note on CNS001. One acquisition answers both.

- WHO INN LISTS — kidney proposed re-extracting per-position chemistry from the twenty lists the project ALREADY HOLDS; hydrocephalus independently proposed rl94 plus the unparsed entries inside the five lists it already cites; thrombocytopenia and immunotoxicity both named the INN route as their top unreached next step. Same document family, four endpoints, and the files are already on disk. ACQUIRE ONCE as a single parse pass — and note that GSRS (duplicate 2) supersedes it wherever a UNII exists, so run GSRS first and use INN only for the remainder.

- ACCESSDATA.FDA.GOV USER-AGENT FINDING — independently rediscovered by FIVE scouts (kidney, hepatic, complement, immunotoxicity, XCUT-CMC) and independently reached as the OPPOSITE conclusion by coagulopathy, which logged the host as Akamai-blocked. I resolved it definitively this session. One corrected register line serves every endpoint; instead it consumed six scouts' attention and left one branch asserting a false block.

- SEWING 2017 S1 RAW FILE — already identified in oversight P3 as shared between complement and thrombocytopenia ('the Sewing raw-file acquisition is shared with thrombocytopenia so the effort is paid once'), and the thrombocytopenia scout independently observed another agent's EMA downloads appearing in the shared scratchpad minutes before its own session and deliberately stood down. That is the correct behaviour and the only instance of it in eleven reports — it should be the default, not an accident.

## 6. Leads flagged unverified — never cite these as sources

- InvivoGen vendor documentation for ODN 2395 / ODN 2216 (complement H3). Direct fetch of invivogen.com/odn2395 returned HTTP 403 with no body; existence is indicated only by a search-result link set. No purity, endotoxin or quality content has been seen by anyone. The related patent US 8,834,900 WAS verified and lists TCGTCGTTTTCGGCGCGCGCCG as SEQ ID NO:1 identified as ODN 2395, but carries no per-position notation, so it does not close H2.
- Buller 2015 NEJM FXI-ASO supplementary appendix (PMC4367537, coagulopathy H5/H6). Europe PMC core metadata verified (authMan Y, hasSuppl Y) but the supplementaryFiles endpoint returned HTTP 200 with only a 296-byte XML body and no payload. The open-registry fallback is verified NOT to exist: NCT01713361 and NCT02553889 both have no posted results. Access UNKNOWN, no price seen. Largely superseded by NCT04534114 (307 participants, placebo-controlled FXI-ASO bleeding, public domain).
- TGA AusPAR document bodies — all six. Filenames and sponsor metadata retrieved; content never seen (HTTP 503 / HTTP-2 INTERNAL_ERROR / empty reply). Any statement about what their Quality findings sections contain is a hypothesis, not a finding.
- Sewing / Minz / Boess 2019, 'In Vitro Assessment of the Hepatotoxicity Potential of Therapeutic Oligonucleotides', 10.1007/978-1-4939-9670-4_15. Crossref record verified (Methods in Molecular Biology, pp.249-259). Not retrieved. The only access signal is a Crossref licence URL (springer.com/tdm) which is a text-and-data-mining link and is NOT evidence of a paywall. This is the document that would let Sewing 2016 Figure 8A's x-axis be interpreted; check Europe PMC before escalating.
- Creyon Bio ChemRxiv preprint 10.26434/chemrxiv-2025-d2bhr (hepatic H2). Crossref record verified incl. CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 and the full abstract. Both the landing page and the direct PDF returned HTTP 403 — a technical block, not a paywall. Species and compound count are UNVERIFIED; the abstract names no human system, so the expected H2 payload is unconfirmed.
- Toxicology Letters P18-33, 10.1016/j.toxlet.2025.07.486 (hepatic/CNS H1). Crossref-verified to volume 411 page S203 with six named authors. ScienceDirect returned HTTP 403. The identities of its fourteen ASOs are UNVERIFIED. Its real value is as a pointer: two of its authors also authored the NCATS Zenodo deposit, so it is the fastest route to that deposit's missing companion article.
- Milasen: the sequence-to-exposure link. Both documents are VERIFIED to exist (NEJM PMID 31597037 / PMC6961983; patent US 11,999,953 B2, Boston Children's, granted 2024-06-04). But NOBODY has seen the milasen base sequence in either, and the patent may claim a genus without identifying the dosed molecule. If that link cannot be made, this is a clinical narrative with no molecule and must NOT be recorded as an H4.
- US 10,955,407 B2 (Roche, in vitro hepatotoxicity assay): existence, inventors, dates and figure captions verified from Google Patents. Whether it contains a sequence listing at all is UNVERIFIED and the Google Patents read suggested it does NOT, which contradicts the pattern of its two held kidney siblings. This materially changes its H2 value. The repo has already shown patent OCR text layers here are unreliable and produced at least three spurious chemistry conflicts — render the pages, do not grep.
- US 12,421,516 B2 sequence listing contents (register row 16): the document and its kidney-relevance statements are verified, but the 72-page USPTO PDF has NO text layer (pdftotext returned zero characters). The notation legend and SEQ ID count come from the Google Patents HTML, not the PDF. No base has been extracted or validated by anyone.
- EP 1 957 647 A2 / US 2007/0142315 A1 (Coley, immunostimulatory oligoribonucleotides): identity, applicants, inventors and priority dates VERIFIED from the EPO publication server. But the EPO HTML rendered only the cover/bibliographic page — the sequence tables, SEQ ID numbering and cytokine tables were NOT seen. The claim that it carries per-position chemistry and human PBMC data comes from the US family member's description, not from inspecting the tables. Verify before costing.
- EP 4 500 170 A2 (Ionis, 'Methods for Separating Certain Oligonucleotide Compounds'): existence verified from the EPO publication server (published 05.02.2025). Only the bibliographic header was retrieved. NO purity figure, NO named compound and NO sequence listing from this document has been seen by anyone on this project. Existence-verified and content-unverified; ranked deliberately low so nobody mistakes the two.
- Hornung 2005 (10.1038/nm1191) green-OA copy at EPFL Infoscience record 198831. Unpaywall reports a free repository location with licence cc-by-nc-nd, but retrieval FAILED: the PDF URL returned HTTP 200 with 685,900 bytes of HTML app shell, and the record page returned HTTP 429. Version is submittedVersion, so figure/table numbering may differ from the published article. This is the source the lead scientist named by name, and the file is still not in hand.
- Forsbach 2008 (10.4049/jimmunol.180.6.3729): two registries DISAGREE. Unpaywall says is_oa True, bronze, publisher publishedVersion, license None; Europe PMC says 'Subscription required'. The AAI PDF URL returned HTTP 200 but only 1,482 bytes of HTML stub. Access honestly UNKNOWN; no price seen. Note that even if it opens, bronze means no reuse licence, so it cannot serve H6.
- Riera-Tur 2024 (10.1089/nat.2024.0013): CC BY confirmed independently by Europe PMC and Unpaywall, which is a useful licence FACT about the largest approved human block in immunotoxicity. But the article was never opened (liebertpub 301 -> journals.sagepub 403 Cloudflare), so its claimed H3 content is UNVERIFIED, and Europe PMC reports hasSuppl=N, which is evidence AGAINST a characterization table existing.
- Both bioRxiv supplement file lists (10.1101/2021.10.30.466173 Burel; 10.1101/2021.12.12.472280 Pollak). Records and abstracts verified via Europe PMC and the bioRxiv API, including licence 'cc_no' (no reuse licence) and the published-link field. The supplementary-material pages returned HTTP 429 and Europe PMC reports hasSuppl=N for both, so NOBODY has confirmed a sequence table is attached. The Burel supplement is the repo's top access request; try once more before escalating to Oscar.
- CPG 7909 mass per dose in AV7909 (immunotoxicity H4, NCT01263691/NCT03877926). The 0.25 mg per 0.5 mL figure appeared only in a search summary. Neither the posted protocol nor the CYFENDUS label was opened. The dose element of H4 is therefore UNSATISFIED for both AV7909 trials — do not log them as complete H4s until it is read from one of those two documents.
- Mangsbo doctoral thesis (Uppsala). Only an avhandlingar.se index record was retrieved (HTTP 200, 31,722 bytes) with a keyword list including complement, Compstatin, properdin, C3 and 'whole blood loop system'. No DiVA URN, no full text, no licence. uu.diva-portal.org reset the connection (curl exit 35). That it CONTAINS the whole-blood-loop complement data, and that its licence helps, are both UNVERIFIED.
- AON-D21 is NOT verified to be chemically identical to NOX-D21. The held Immunobiology 2025 review states the lineage NOX-D19 -> NOX-D20 -> NOX-D21/AON-D21 in prose only; the verified crystal-structure paper (10.1038/ncomms7481, CC BY) gives sequence and chemistry for D19 and D20 ONLY. Any row keying the clinical molecule to that sequence is an inference and must not be made.
- Nedosiran duplex sequence. UNVERIFIED from any source. H1 credit for PHYOX2 (NCT03847909) and NCT05001269 depends entirely on it; until it resolves from WHO INN, a Dicerna patent or FDA GSRS, those two trials are H5 only against a sequence-TBD compound and must not be entered as H1.
- The companion article behind Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.18776233. I VERIFIED the deposit myself: CC BY 4.0, ten .R files, and NO .xlsx or .csv data file present. The six workbooks named inside the scripts (insphero_raw_data_alt_v4.xlsx, _atp_v3.xlsx, _ldh_v3.xlsx and three processed_*.xlsx) are NOT in the deposit, and the deposit carries no isSupplementTo/isPublishedIn relation. The human/mouse/rat IC50 tables are confirmed only as code inputs; not one numeric value has been seen. This was ranked as the best human-liver lead available and it is currently a pointer with no payload — do not present it as an acquirable dataset.
- Health Canada Summary Basis of Decision / Regulatory Decision Summary (SBD00361 Spinraza, SBD00422 and RDS00509 Tegsedi, SBD00446 and RDS00567 Onpattro). One scout retrieved the SBD00361 page itself (HTTP 200, 92,039 bytes) including a narrative '7.3 Quality Basis for Decision' section; the others appeared only in search listings and were never opened. Whether any numeric purity or impurity limit appears is UNVERIFIED — the wording seen is narrative ('meet the approved specifications') and may carry no number. A fifth regulator the project has entirely missed.
- WO 2017/015555 A1 (Wave) sequence tables. The patent is VERIFIED to exist and VERIFIED to name WVE120101 alongside references to 'Tables N1A, N2A, N3A, N4A and 8'. Those tables were NOT in the retrieved excerpt, so the per-linkage Rp/Sp stereochemistry inside them is EXPECTED, not confirmed. This is the realistic route to WVE-120101/120102 sequences, but it is unproven.
- Nagano 2023 Table S1 (PMC10445101). The heading is present on page 13 of mmc1.pdf but the table body has no text layer. It is the one table that would close chemistry for all 32 COG-S074 compounds, and it is an OCR job nobody has done. mmc2.pdf (11,658,451 bytes) was downloaded and never opened.
- BIIB105 / ION-541 and WVE-003 sequences remain UNFOUND after the patent route (US 12,188,020 B2 carries ATXN2 sequences but no compound-code-to-SEQ-ID mapping), the WHO INN route (Recommended Lists 70-95 and Proposed Lists 108-135 already exhausted by the repo), and GSRS (no INN, no UNII for either). Escalate as named unresolved items rather than letting a fourth session re-run the same three routes. Note the repo's own warning on CNS005: 'any sequence assignment would be a guess.'
- Kidney OLG014/OLG015 (Sandelius 2020 cEt tool and control ASOs). journals.sagepub.com returned HTTP 403 on the /doi/xml/ route under a browser user-agent, matching a prior round's 403 on three routes; the record is marked isOpenAccess=N. No patent, preprint or thesis disclosing these two AstraZeneca proprietary tool sequences was found. Treat as genuinely undisclosed rather than merely unretrieved until someone tests an institutional route. No price was observed.

**Tegsedi (inotersen) EPAR public assessment report — active substance Specification section**  
- identifier: `EMA/411876/2018 (printed in the PDF footer, "Assessment report EMA/411876/2018 Page 15/142")`
- gap closed: Closes purity_method for OLG001 inotersen, currently the literal string TBD. Names the exact analytical procedure (IP-HPLC-UV-MS for content/purity and product-related impurities) and the orthogonal identity panel (accurate-mass MS + MS-MS sequencing), which independently corroborates identity_confirmation for OLG001 — presently sourced only from a regulatory label. This is the first source on the project that reports a purity METHOD for any oligo; the dataset has 0/65.
- extract: Section 2.2.2 'Active Substance' → the 'Specification' sub-section on p.15/142, and the adjacent 'Manufacture, characterisation and process controls' paragraph on p.14-15 for the impurity-grouping and ICH M7 mutagenic-impurity statements. Also pull the finished-product specification paragraph (text line ~1014 of the extracted text: "inotersen content and product related impurities (IP-HPLC-UV-MS), deliverable volume ..."). Extract into purity_method; leave purity_pct TBD — see worth_reasoning.
- access: OPEN — Free retrieval, HTTP 200, no login, no payment, no bot wall. No licence statement observed on the PDF itself, so I am not asserting one; EMA documents are published for public access and the project already treats regulatory documents as redistributable at summary level.
- worth: HIGH — Purity is required twice by the Phase 2 brief and the project has 3 real values in 3,034 oligo rows. This is the single mandatory field with the worst coverage, and STATUS.md §3a shows the team concluded 'not reported in any source reviewed' after reviewing only patents and labels — a class of document they never opened reports it in full. CRITICAL HONESTY CAVEAT, so Rocksteady does not over-promise: these reports give the METHOD and the fact that limits were set and tightened, but they do NOT print the numeric acceptance criteria. purity_method can move from 0/65 toward ~12/65; purity_pct will stay TBD from this route. Report it that way or the gain is overstated.

**WHO INN Recommended List residue-by-residue nomenclature — re-extract per-position modification and phosphorothioate LINKAGE positions (documents already held)**  
- identifier: `WHO Drug Information Vol. 30 No. 3, Recommended INN List 76, pp. 500-502 (givosiran entry); file rl76.pdf`
- gap closed: Directly closes the two position gaps the endpoint has zero coverage of: (a) the missing modification-position column, and (b) phosphorothioate LINKAGE positions, recorded for 0/65. Derivable for the 20 INN-sourced oligos, which are precisely the rows where chemistry is least resolved: OLG003 givosiran, OLG007 inclisiran, OLG019-OLG024, OLG028, OLG029, OLG032-OLG040 all carry ps_count=TBD today. The S-count in the molecular formula (S6 for givosiran) gives an independent arithmetic check on the PS count parsed from the name, so each row can be validated rather than trusted.
- extract: For each of the 20 lists in SOURCE_REGISTER.md §5, the English nomenclature paragraph of the named compound (NOT the French/Spanish repeats, which are byte-identical in content and will otherwise triple-count). Parse the '-(3'→5')' / '-(5'→3')' delimited residue chain into one row per residue: position index, sugar modification, base, and linkage type to the next residue (P-thio = phosphorothioate, unmarked = phosphodiester). Cross-check the resulting PS count against the sulfur subscript in the molecular formula on the same page. Output a new long-format modifications table keyed on oligo_id + position, which is also the schema change the endpoint needs.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200, free, no login. These exact PDFs are already held and already parsed by scripts/fill_inn_sequences.py, so acquisition cost is zero.
- worth: HIGH — Ranked top with the EPARs because H2 and H3 are the mandatory fields. Unique profile among every lead here: zero acquisition cost and zero access risk, because the project already has the files on disk — the only work is a second parse of a source it has already validated base-for-base. It converts the brief's requirement for 'the location of every chemical modification' from unmet to largely met for a third of the roster in one pass. BE ACCURATE IN THE WRITE-UP: this is a re-extraction of a held document for an unpopulated field, NOT a newly discovered source. Presenting it as a new acquisition would be exactly the kind of overstatement that has already damaged this project twice.

**US 12,421,516 B2 — Antisense oligomers targeting PCSK9 (Roche Innovation Center Copenhagen)**  
- identifier: `US 12,421,516 B2; granted 2025-09-23; priority 2013-06-27; USPTO PDF retrieved from image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/12421516`
- gap closed: H2: this is the only source I verified that notates BOTH modification positions AND phosphorothioate-versus-phosphodiester linkage per position in machine-readable form, with the legend printed in the document. It covers the exact chemical series the endpoint cannot resolve — the Roche PCSK9 LNA gapmers OLG002/OLG047 (SPC5001, the project's known human nephrotoxin, and its duplicate), OLG042, OLG043, OLG046, OLG048. H1: 46 SEQ ID NOs against the roster's 65 total, from an assignee and series already central to the dataset, with explicit nephrotoxicity-reduction design intent — so new molecules arrive with a kidney rationale attached rather than as unrelated sequences.
- extract: The SEQUENCE LISTING (SEQ ID NOs 1-46) and the compound/motif tables in the description that apply the superscript-L / subscript-s / superscript-Me-C notation, plus the notation legend paragraph itself so the parse is auditable. Then Figure 15 ('Kim-1 expression from rat safety study') with its figure legend and the corresponding Example section giving species, dose and schedule. Join on sequence to confirm which listed SEQ IDs are the already-held SPC5001 series before adding any new oligo_id.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200 from the USPTO public image service, no login, no payment. US patent text is public domain; the project already classifies US patent material as redistribution=public_domain for N3/N4, so this is consistent with what it ships today.
- worth: HIGH — Patents are already the proven-productive class on this project and this one is squarely on-target rather than adjacent: same assignee lineage, same PCSK9 LNA chemistry, and written explicitly around reducing the kidney toxicity of SPC5001 — the single molecule the dataset leans on hardest. PRACTICAL WARNING: the USPTO PDF I pulled has no text layer (pdftotext returned zero characters from 72 pages), so budget for OCR or use the Google Patents HTML full text, which does carry the text and which is where I read the notation legend. Verify any OCR'd sequence against the Google Patents text before writing a single base, given this project's history with transcription.

**RACER53 posted results — viltolarsen Phase 3, renal adverse events with placebo denominators**  
- identifier: `NCT04060199 (ClinicalTrials.gov, hasResults = true)`
- gap closed: This is a candidate for the project's FIRST clean clinical negative, the absence the lead scientist is blocking a classifier on. It satisfies every element of the H4 definition independently: exact sequence held (OLG013, and independently re-confirmed today against FDA NDA 212154 — see the viltolarsen quality-review lead); human exposure at the approved dose (80 mg/kg weekly IV); adequate duration (48 weeks); explicit organ monitoring evidenced by protocol-driven renal lab terms in the AE table itself (UPCR, urine protein, urine beta-2-microglobulin — these are scheduled-laboratory terms, not spontaneous reports, and beta-2-microglobulin is a proximal-tubule marker); explicit outcome, quantified and negative — every renal term is at or below placebo except 'Urine abnormality' (3 vs 0); and a fully traceable denominator WITH a randomised comparator arm, 38 vs 39. It also directly answers SOURCE_REGISTER §6's problem that 13 grade-0 clinical rows are 'not supported as measured negatives': this one is measured, monitored and placebo-controlled.
- extract: From the API record (or the 'Adverse Events' tab): the full otherEvents and seriousEvents tables with organSystem, term, numAffected and numAtRisk per eventGroup, plus adverseEventsModule.frequencyThreshold (5) and timeFrame, and the armGroups descriptions for dose and duration. Record the 5% reporting threshold on the row — below-threshold renal terms are not listed, so absence is not zero and must not be curated as such. Note there are NO posted continuous renal outcome measures in this record (I checked outcomeMeasuresModule for renal/cystatin/creatinine/protein and found none), so this is an H4 source, not an H5 one.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200 from the public ClinicalTrials.gov API v2, no key, no login, no payment. US federal government work, public domain — the same basis on which the project already ships US federal label material as redistribution=public_domain.
- worth: HIGH — The brief calls a clean clinical negative the highest-value find available and the project has zero. This one is verified end to end, in public-domain data, on an oligo whose sequence the project already holds and which I independently re-confirmed today from a second regulatory document. It also fixes the shape problem rather than just adding a row: the dataset's negatives have been criticised for lacking traceable denominators and measured monitoring, and this supplies both plus a randomised comparator. Qualifier to carry onto the row: DMD renal interpretation is confounded by low muscle mass, which SOURCE_REGISTER §4 already flags for every DMD row — hence the value of the non-creatinine markers (UPCR, urinary beta-2-microglobulin) being present here.

**NEURO-TTR posted results — inotersen renal AND platelet adverse events with placebo denominators**  
- identifier: `NCT01737398 (ClinicalTrials.gov, hasResults = true)`
- gap closed: H6: an openly licensed, public-domain substitute for the paywalled NEJM NEURO-TTR paper (10.1056/NEJMoa1716793), which is anchor A1 and currently supports 3 rows. SOURCES_TO_ACQUIRE §B and SOURCE_REGISTER §6 both route inotersen clinical safety through NEJM; this carries the same safety table without the entitlement, which matters because the PADP commits to CC BY 4.0 and half of one endpoint reportedly cannot legally ship. H4/H5: supplies the denominators the inotersen rows lack — the dataset records inotersen renal findings but not 2/112 glomerulonephritis against 0/60 placebo — and it is the positive-control counterpart to the viltolarsen negative, with BOTH platelet and renal monitoring in one table, which is the exact monitoring shape the H4 definition names.
- extract: The full seriousEvents and otherEvents tables with numAffected/numAtRisk per group (serious and non-serious are separate tables — do not merge them, and do not sum the two Thrombocytopenia rows), the frequencyThreshold, the timeFrame, and the armGroups text for dose and 64-week duration. Promote this as the primary citation for the inotersen renal rows and demote the paywalled NEJM reference to corroboration.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200 from the public ClinicalTrials.gov API v2, no key or login. US federal government work, public domain.
- worth: HIGH — Does three jobs at once: removes a paywall dependency on the endpoint's best-known anchor, adds the denominators that make the inotersen rows auditable, and gives the classifier a quantified positive to sit opposite the viltolarsen negative. Worth noting for the modelling discussion: the register's §6 analysis found search-derived rows systematically under-report severity (zero reach grade >=2 against 11/22 anchor rows, Fisher p = 4.5e-5). Registry posted results are structured, thresholded and denominator-bearing, so routing clinical rows through them instead of through prose summaries attacks the cause of that bias, not just the symptom.

**Lidberg 2021 doctoral dissertation — Application of a Renal Proximal Tubule Microphysiological System for Drug Safety Assessment and Disease Modeling**  
- identifier: `University of Washington ResearchWorks, item 15f84c49-4d99-42e1-8bcd-c72c138d4408; PDF bitstream a390ead3-f81b-4f26-a774-6668a939d0d1`
- gap closed: Attacks the endpoint's named structural weakness head-on: human in-vitro breadth, where 48 of 67 rows (72%) come from one patent table spanning 3 oligos. This supplies a second, fully independent human in-vitro system with (a) up to 10 oligomers whose sequences are printed, (b) a base-sequence-matched PMO/PSO pair — HPV PMO and HPV PSO share the identical base sequence CCTTTAGGGTAACAAGTCTTC and differ ONLY in backbone chemistry, which is a controlled chemistry contrast the dataset does not currently contain anywhere, and (c) new HUMAN in-vitro data on drisapersen, which the project already holds as OLG008 with a confirmed sequence but whose human evidence rests on Janssen 2019 alone. H5: continuous readouts (KIM-1, HO-1, miRNA, RNAseq) rather than curator-derived binaries.
- extract: Table 2.1 'PMO oligomer sequences' (page ~57) for the 10 sequences, targets and chemistry; Supplemental Table 2.1 for donor demographics so the human system is documented per the brief's metadata requirement; Table 2.3 for the 18-gene DSP PSO transcriptional response at 10 µM/44 h; and the Chapter 2 figures/sections carrying KIM-1 secretion, HO-1 immunocytochemistry and miR-30e-5p with their doses and exposure durations (1 µM for 5 days, and 10 µM for 44 h — the two regimens are distinct and must not be pooled). Treat the HPV PMO/HPV PSO pair as two oligo records sharing one base sequence, exactly as the source presents them.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200 free direct download of the full 181-page PDF from the University of Washington institutional repository, no login, no payment, no bot wall. I did NOT verify a licence statement on the item record, so I am not claiming a specific licence — the H6 value is the open institutional-repository route, and the licence must be checked before any verbatim redistribution.
- worth: HIGH — Highest-value H1 I found, and it lands exactly where the endpoint is thinnest. The brief makes human in-vitro the explicit priority and 55.7% of rows are animal; this is a human MPS with named donors and printed sequences, in a document class the project has never used. The base-sequence-matched PMO/PSO pair is the standout scientific asset: it isolates backbone chemistry from sequence, which is precisely the comparison the dataset's chemistry-versus-toxicity modelling cannot currently make. Caveats to carry: it is a dissertation, so Chapter 2 should be checked against any peer-reviewed version before the data are treated as published, and the oligomers are CYP3A5/HPV research tools rather than clinical candidates — they raise molecule count and chemistry contrast, not clinical relevance.

**FDA NDA 212154 Product Quality Review(s) (viltolarsen) — and the access finding that accessdata.fda.gov is a user-agent bot wall, not a block**  
- identifier: `NDA 212154Orig1s000, 'PRODUCT QUALITY REVIEW(S)', Reference ID 4624922, review dated 6/15/2020`
- gap closed: H3: names the purity/characterisation panel for OLG013 viltolarsen (orthogonal identity tests, assay, related substances, plus SEC for aggregates) where purity_method is TBD today. H2: independently re-confirms the OLG013 sequence 5'-CCTCCGGTTCTGAAGGTGTTC-3' base-for-base from a regulatory quality review, and the formula C244H381N113O88P20 corroborates the P20/21-nt check already noted in the OLG013 row. ACCESS: overturns the stated position in SOURCES.md line 12 and SOURCE_REGISTER.md §2 that accessdata.fda.gov is 'NO — blocked'. It is a user-agent bot wall. That single change unblocks SOURCES_TO_ACQUIRE §C — the Pharmacology/Toxicology and Multi-disciplinary reviews for 210922, 215515, 215887, 217388, 219019, 212154, 211970, 213026, 211172 — which §C calls 'the only route to 39 missing doses' and 'the only public source for animal NOAELs and renal lab tables'.
- extract: From this document: the 'Drug Substance' and 'Drug Product' Specification paragraphs (pp.4-6 of the extracted text) into purity_method, and the Section 11 DESCRIPTION block carrying the nucleotide sequence, molecular formula and MW into identity_confirmation. Then, as the larger follow-on, re-run SOURCES_TO_ACQUIRE §C against the drugsatfda_docs/nda/<year>/<app>Orig1s000<TYPE>.pdf pattern with a browser user-agent, pulling PharmR, ChemR and Multi-disciplinary review PDFs for the nine listed applications.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200, application/pdf, 2,181,318 bytes, free, no login and no payment — retrieved with a standard browser user-agent. The 302-to-apology-page response is explicitly an 'abuse-detection' bot wall; no price, purchase page or access requirement was shown at any point. FDA review documents are US federal government work, public domain.
- worth: HIGH — Rated HIGH for the access finding more than for the single document. One corrected register line converts the endpoint's largest acknowledged dead end — 39 missing doses, nine applications, described in the repository as unreachable — into ordinary retrieval work, and it applies to every other endpoint on the project too. The document itself is a genuine but partial H3: it names the methods and confirms identity, while the numeric acceptance criteria are redacted as (b)(4) trade secret, 31 such redactions in 25 pages. So FDA quality reviews yield purity_method, not purity_pct — the same ceiling the EPARs hit. Rocksteady should state that limit plainly rather than opening nine review packages expecting numbers that are not in them.

**Onpattro, Givlaari, Waylivra and Qalsody EPAR assessment reports — active substance Specification sections (purity method for four more roster oligos)**  
- identifier: `Onpattro EMA/554262/2018 (p.21/188); Givlaari, Waylivra and Qalsody EPAR public assessment reports, retrieved 2026-10-03`
- gap closed: Extends the Tegsedi lead from one oligo to five: closes purity_method for OLG019 patisiran, OLG003 givosiran, OLG005 volanesorsen and OLG017 tofersen alongside OLG001 inotersen. Each also supplies an orthogonal identity panel including explicit sequence confirmation by MS-MS (Givlaari) or sequencing by IP-HPLC-TOF-MS (Waylivra) or Failure Sequence Analysis (Qalsody), corroborating identity_confirmation for rows whose identity currently rests on a label or an INN parse alone. The Givlaari report additionally states the impurity-threshold basis — that oligonucleotides fall outside ICH Q3A(R2) and the Ph. Eur. 'Substance for pharmaceutical use' synthetic-peptide thresholds were applied instead — which is the methodological justification the Phase 2 methodology document needs for how oligo purity is specified at all.
- extract: In each report, Section 2.2 (Quality aspects) → Active Substance → the 'Specification' sub-section, plus the finished-product Specification sub-section. Pull the verbatim test list into purity_method per oligo. From Givlaari additionally extract the ICH Q3A(R2)-exclusion / Ph. Eur. synthetic-peptide-threshold paragraph for METHODOLOGY_PHASE2.md §5. Then repeat the same URL pattern for the roster oligos I did not reach: oxlumo (OLG020), leqvio (OLG007), amvuttra (OLG022), spinraza (OLG004), rivfloza (OLG021) and kynamro (OLG006) — all returned HTTP 429 rate-limiting, not refusal. Note wainua returned 404 on this pattern, so eplontersen (OLG018) needs a different document path.
- access: OPEN — Four HTTP 200 free retrievals, no login, no payment. The failures I saw were HTTP 429 rate-limiting (pace the requests) and one HTTP 404 (wrong path for wainua) — neither is a paywall, and no price or access requirement was displayed. No licence statement observed on the PDFs themselves, so none is asserted.
- worth: HIGH — Same reasoning as the Tegsedi lead, with the decisive addition that the route is now demonstrated to REPLICATE across products from one predictable URL pattern — five of five attempted products that returned a document carried a usable specification paragraph. That turns purity_method from a one-off rescue into systematic coverage of the approved-oligo slice of the roster, and the pattern transfers to the project's other endpoints unchanged. The same ceiling applies and must be stated: method yes, numeric purity_pct no.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- OLG014 and OLG015 (Sandelius 2020 cEt tool and control ASOs) remain with no sequence candidate and I did not change that. journals.sagepub.com returned HTTP 403 on the /doi/xml/ route even with a browser user-agent, which matches the prior round's finding of 403 on three routes; the record is marked isOpenAccess=N. I found no patent, preprint or thesis disclosing these two AstraZeneca proprietary tool sequences. Treat them as genuinely undisclosed rather than merely unretrieved until someone tests an institutional route.
- I did not verify a licence statement for the Lidberg 2021 dissertation. Retrieval was free and unauthenticated, but the H6 claim for it is 'open institutional-repository route', not a named licence. Check the item record before any verbatim redistribution.
- EMA EPAR assessment reports for oxlumo (OLG020 lumasiran), leqvio (OLG007 inclisiran), amvuttra (OLG022 vutrisiran), spinraza (OLG004 nusinersen) and rivfloza (OLG021 nedosiran) all returned HTTP 429 rate-limiting. This is pacing, NOT refusal and NOT a paywall - I simply ran the batch too fast and did not come back to them. The wainua (OLG018 eplontersen) URL returned 404, so that product needs a different document path. Kynamro (OLG006 mipomersen) was not attempted at all.
- No licence statement was observed on any of the five EMA PDFs I did retrieve. I am asserting 'freely retrievable', not a specific licence. The PADP's CC BY 4.0 commitment means redistribution terms for EMA text need checking separately before any verbatim quotation ships.
- The PMDA Japanese-language review documents surfaced in search (pmda.go.jp/files/000282162.pdf and /000237467.pdf, the latter a 'Report on the Deliberation Results' for viltolarsen) were NOT retrieved or read. Japanese regulatory CMC remains an open and plausibly productive seam for purity specifications, and it is the one under-searched class named in my brief that I did not actually open.
- I did not search pharmacopoeial monographs (USP, Ph. Eur.) at all. The Givlaari EPAR points at the Ph. Eur. 'Substance for pharmaceutical use' synthetic-peptide thresholds as the applied basis for oligo impurity limits, so a Ph. Eur. general-chapter search is an obvious and unexplored follow-on that might yield numeric limits where the product-specific reports redact them.
- US 12,421,516 B2 was retrieved from USPTO as a 72-page PDF with NO text layer (pdftotext returned zero characters). Everything I report about its notation legend, SEQ ID count and kidney statements comes from the Google Patents HTML record, not from the PDF I hold. The sequence listing itself has not been extracted or validated by me, and OCR output must be checked against the Google Patents text before any base is written to oligos.csv.
- I did not re-examine whether the US11105794 / US11479818 patent family contains further continuations with additional HUMAN in-vitro tables. Two sibling PCSK9 patents surfaced (US 11,739,332 and US 12,421,516); I verified only the latter. US 11,739,332 is unexamined, as is the published application US 2019/0265230.
- No repository deposit search was run this round (Zenodo, Figshare, Dryad, OSF, GEO, ArrayExpress). The prior round logged per-compound hit counts in research_staging/logs/repository_targeted_hits.json; I did not open that log or extend it, so raw continuous data (H5) is the hit type I covered most weakly - only incidentally, via the Lidberg RNAseq and biomarker readouts.
- No numeric purity_pct value was found in ANY source I retrieved. Both regulatory classes hit the same ceiling: FDA redacts acceptance criteria as (b)(4) trade secret (31 redactions in 25 pages), and the EMA reports describe limits as set and tightened without printing them. My leads can move purity_method from 0/65 toward roughly 12/65; they do not move purity_pct off TBD. STATUS.md §3a's recommendation to present purity as a stated limitation should stand for the numeric field, now on firmer evidence - the team can say which methods are specified and that the numbers are withheld as commercially confidential, which is a materially stronger disclosure than a silent blank.

### thrombocytopenia — 11 leads

**Gaps targeted:** Read from origin/claude/oligo-challenge-data-4um5mi: thrombocytopenia/STATUS.md, SOURCES.md, ROCKSTEADY_RESEARCH_REPORT_2026-10-02.md, data/oligos.csv (259 rows), data/measurements.csv (1,959 rows), curation/rights/rights_audit.csv, curation/research_staging/search_coverage_log.csv, curation/scientist_v09/Oligo_Characterization.csv.  Gaps confirmed in-repo, not taken on trust: (1) purity_pct = TBD for all 17 approved compounds I enumerated from oligos.csv (avacincaptad pegol, casimersen, eplontersen, eteplirsen, givosiran, golodirsen, imetelstat, inclisiran, inotersen, lumasiran, mipomersen, nusinersen, olezarsen, patisiran, tofersen, viltolarsen, vutrisiran); purity value 0/34 human-clinical and 0/48 human-lab per the Rocksteady report. Scientist package records NOT REPORTED IN CURRENT CORPUS for all 45 records. (2) modification_map absent for 14 of the 17 approved compounds (map present only for inotersen, mipomersen, nusinersen, olezarsen); imetelstat (TOLG070) has NEITHER sequence NOR map; viltolarsen (TOLG257) has sequence but no map. Rocksteady's own audit: 0/34 clinical position-chemistry maps are source-verbatim. (3) Zero qualified clinical negatives; 21 audited candidates

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, NCT03358030 — ISIS 416858 (IONIS-FXIRx) Phase 2 in end-stage renal disease on haemodialysis, n=213**  
- identifier: `NCT03358030`
- gap closed: The single highest-value gap in the project: ZERO clean sequence-linked clinical negatives, which is why the clinical sequence classifier is BLOCKED. This is the only candidate I found that satisfies every clause of H4 at arm grain without relying on reporting silence. Exact sequence: held in-repo as TOLG108, ACGGCATTGGTGCACAGTT(..), full_PS 2'-MOE 5-10-5 gapmer, ps 19. Human exposure: yes. Adequate dose and duration: three dose levels (200/250/300 mg) to Day 260. EXPLICIT platelet monitoring: a protocol-pre-specified, platelet-count-specific endpoint with five pre-defined severity bands — this is affirmative measurement, not absence of an AE report, which is precisely the objection that disqualified all 21 of German's audited candidates. Explicit outcome: counts in every band. Traceable denominator: 53/53/54/50. The 200 mg arm is a clean NEGATIVE at the severe thresholds — 0 of 53 below 75 K/mm^3, identical to placebo (0 of 53) — while the 250 and 300 mg arms are positive in the same trial under the same definition. So it yields a negative, a placebo control and a dose gradient on one sequence. Also H5 (five banded counts replace a curator binary) and H6 (public domain, where the project's comparable platelet-band content is CC BY-NC).
- extract: The outcome-measures module for NCT03358030, specifically the OTHER_PRE_SPECIFIED measure "Number of Participants With Laboratory Abnormalities Related to Platelet Count" — all 5 classes x 4 groups = 20 cells plus the 4 denominators. Pull as JSON from https://clinicaltrials.gov/api/v2/studies/NCT03358030?fields=resultsSection.outcomeMeasuresModule.outcomeMeasures so the locus is machine-citable. Take the adverseEventsModule in the same call for the thrombocytopenia/platelet-count-decreased MedDRA terms with their numAtRisk, and the two FXI-activity measures as the intended-pharmacology flag German's registry requires.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved without credentials over the public REST API v2, HTTP 200, no paywall or registration encountered. ClinicalTrials.gov is an NIH/NLM US Government work. I did not separately fetch a licence page, so I am recording the retrieval, not quoting a licence statement.
- worth: HIGH — It is the only find that breaks the specific logjam blocking the classifier, and it breaks it with a sequence the project already holds, so it raises no new chemistry gap. A negative arm, a placebo arm and a positive arm from one molecule under one prospectively-defined platelet endpoint is a better object than any three separate sources. Public domain on top.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, NCT04089566 — DEVOTE, nusinersen higher-dose study (Parts A, B and C)**  
- identifier: `NCT04089566`
- gap closed: Second clean clinical negative, and the one with the strongest endpoint definition anywhere in this endpoint: a confirmed-on-two-consecutive-measurements platelet threshold, carried as a PRIMARY outcome, at a deliberately escalated loading dose (50 mg). Nusinersen is TOLG240 with sequence AND modification map already held, so the sequence linkage needs no new acquisition. 0% of 6 and 2.5% of 40 at the high dose, 0% in three of four Part B arms. Grep against measurements.csv: NCT04089566 carries ZERO measurement rows today despite being named in 10 repo files — pure anchor, so this is new content.
- extract: Both "Percentage of Participants With a Postbaseline Platelet Count Below the Lower Limit of Normal on at Least 2 Consecutive Measurements" measures (Parts A/C primary and Part B secondary) with all six arm denominators, and the three "Shifts From Baseline in Clinical Laboratory Parameters (Hematology Parameters)" measures for the platelet shift-to-low rows. Same API field path as the lead above.
- access: OPEN — Public REST API v2, HTTP 200, no credential or payment. NIH/NLM US Government work; no licence page fetched.
- worth: HIGH — A prospectively-defined, confirmation-required platelet threshold reported as a primary endpoint is the cleanest possible negative, and it arrives dose-stratified. Because the compound's sequence and map are already held and adjudicated, this is close to drop-in evidence rather than a new curation project.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, NCT02981602 — IONIS-HBVRx / GSK3228836 (bepirovirsen) Phase 2 in treatment-naive chronic HBV**  
- identifier: `NCT02981602`
- gap closed: H5 in its purest form: 20 raw continuous platelet-count mean changes with standard deviations, placebo-controlled, dose-contrasted (150 vs 300 mg) and time-resolved over four visits to Day 211, for a molecule whose exact sequence AND source-verbatim per-linkage map the project already holds (TOLG175 / TOLG052: GCAGAGGTGAAGCGAAGTGC, eG*eC(5m)*eA*eG*eA*dG*dG*dT*dG*dA*dA*dG*dC(...)). The dataset currently holds curator-derived grades for this class, not continuous values with dispersion. Also supports H4, and it is public domain against a corpus where the comparable 2'-MOE clinical platelet block (387 rows, PMC5467133) is CC BY-NC.
- extract: The PRIMARY measure "Change From Baseline for Hematology Parameters: ... and Platelets" — the four "Platelets, Day N" classes x 5 arms, with means, SDs and the five denominators. Note while extracting that TOLG052 (bepirovirsen) and TOLG175 (ISIS 505358) are the same molecule under two oligo_ids, a second instance of the TOLG072/TOLG073 problem already in the recovery ledger; the rows must land on whichever id German merges to, and must group under one exact_sequence_group for any split.
- access: OPEN — Public REST API v2, HTTP 200, no credential. NIH/NLM US Government work; no licence page fetched.
- worth: HIGH — Continuous values with dispersion and a placebo arm are strictly more informative than the binaries the dataset holds, and they attach to an already-mapped sequence. Small n (6/12/6/5/2) limits it to description rather than inference, which should be stated on the rows.

**US 11,332,489 B2 — "Process for preparing imetelstat" (Geron Corporation)**  
- identifier: `US11332489B2 (and same-family US 12,264,174 B2)`
- gap closed: Imetelstat is TOLG070, the ONLY approved compound in the catalogue with BOTH sequence = TBD and modification_map = TBD, and STATUS.md flags it as a chemistry_disagreement reading "NOTATION EXTENSION REQUIRED". This document supplies the 13-mer base sequence, the uniform N3'->P5' thiophosphoramidate linkage chemistry, the 3'-NH2 terminus, and the exact attachment point of the palmitoyl conjugate (aminoglycerol linker to the 5'-thiophosphate) — i.e. the whole per-position map including the conjugate position, source-verbatim, against an audit that found 0/34 clinical maps source-verbatim. Separately it is the first NUMERIC purity datum I could retrieve for any compound in a catalogue that stands at 0/259, from a public-domain source. Imetelstat matters doubly here because it is a known clinical thrombocytopenia agent and the project already holds its FDA NDA 217779 review.
- extract: Two things, not the paper: (a) the SEQ ID NO:1 definition and the structural paragraph defining R, the aminoglycerol linker and the NPS linkages — this is the modification_map and the sequence for TOLG070; (b) the worked Examples carrying the HPLC tables with 71.6 / 71.2 / 71.3 % full-length and 5.5 / 5.7 / 5.6 % 12-mer shortmer, plus the Kromasil C18 / TEA-HFIP method paragraph. MANDATORY CAVEAT TO CARRY ON THE ROW: these percentages are in-process full-length-product figures from the synthesis examples, NOT a drug-substance release purity or an acceptance criterion. They belong in purity_pct only with purity_method and a process-stage qualifier, or in a characterization note; writing 71.6 into purity_pct unqualified would be a worse error than leaving it TBD. Also pull US 12,264,174 B2 to check whether the later family member reports post-purification purity.
- access: OPEN — Full text retrieved from Google Patents without credential or payment. Granted US patent text is a US Government publication and not subject to copyright. The sibling US 12,264,174 B2 is served as a free PDF from image-ppubs.uspto.gov.
- worth: HIGH — Closes the worst single chemistry hole in the catalogue (an approved drug with neither sequence nor map) with a source-verbatim per-position description including the conjugate position, and simultaneously produces the first retrievable numeric purity figures in a project that has 3 real purity values in 3,034 rows. Public domain. The process-stage caveat is real but it is a labelling discipline, not a reason to pass.

**PMDA Review Report, Viltepso Intravenous Infusion 250 mg (viltolarsen), Nippon Shinyaku Co., Ltd. — English reference translation**  
- identifier: `PMDA file 000237467 (Report on the Deliberation Results, 6 March 2020, bundled with the Review Report of 19 February 2020); https://www.pmda.go.jp/files/000237467.pdf`
- gap closed: H2: viltolarsen is TOLG257, sequence held but modification_map = TBD, and it is the PMO class the recovery ledger flags as critical with no trial-grain representation. The B(n) positional convention plus the 21-mer base sequence CCTCCGGTTCTGAAGGTGTTC is a SOURCE-VERBATIM positional description from a regulator, against an audit finding 0/34 clinical maps source-verbatim. H3: purity_method for TOLG257 currently reads TBD; this document names the complete drug-substance and drug-product purity and identity test set (related substances by HPLC, residual solvents by GC, UV/VIS + IR + MS identification, assay by HPLC, water content, bacterial endotoxins, microbial limit) — that is analytical identity and characterization, which H3 explicitly covers. Not mentioned anywhere in the endpoint directory (git grep on the file id: 0 files).
- extract: From 000237467.pdf: (a) the structural-formula page carrying the B(n) positional convention and "Base sequence: CCTCCGGTTC TGAAGGTGTT C" — transcribe as the source-verbatim modification_map for TOLG257 with the morpholino/phosphorodiamidate backbone it implies; (b) section 2.A/2.B specification paragraphs for drug substance and drug product, verbatim, into purity_method and Analytical_Identity_Method; (c) Tables 4, 5 and 6 with their footnotes, recorded WITH the masking visible, as documented evidence that numeric acceptance criteria are withheld — that is the proper artefact to cite for purity_pct = NOT REPORTED rather than a bare TBD. Then try the companion PMDA file 000282161.pdf, which the same search returned alongside and which I did not open.
- access: OPEN — Downloaded directly from pmda.go.jp without credential or payment, 1,634,124 bytes. Japanese government publication. The document carries the standard PMDA disclaimer that it is a reference translation and "the Japanese original shall take precedence"; I saw no licence statement, so I am recording the retrieval and the disclaimer rather than asserting a licence.
- worth: HIGH — Highest-ranked hit types (H2 then H3) on a critical-flagged compound and class, from a regulator, source-verbatim, and in exactly the non-English regulatory grey literature the brief says is under-searched. It also hands German a citable artefact for the purity-is-withheld finding instead of an assertion.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, NCT02175004 — open-label extension of NEURO-TTR, inotersen 300 mg**  
- identifier: `NCT02175004`
- gap closed: H6: a public-domain replacement for the 19 closed-access rows the dataset draws from NEJMoa1716793 (PMID 29972757), one of the 17 closed sources in the 224-row rights decision. H5: a confirmed-threshold percentage with a traceable denominator and a 272-week observation window, where the dataset holds curator grades. And it is the POSITIVE pole under an endpoint definition (<75 x 10^9/L confirmed) that is directly comparable to the DEVOTE and ISIS 416858 negatives above, which is what makes those three usable together as a calibration ladder instead of three unrelated rows.
- extract: The PRIMARY measure "Percentage of Participants With Clinically Significant Change From Baseline in Laboratory Test Values", specifically the class "Confirmed Value of Platelets <75 x 10^9/L" with both denominators (50, 85) and the exact timeFrame string. Cross-check against study_nesting_ledger.csv before ingesting: this is an EXTENSION of NCT01737398, so its subjects overlap the NEURO-TTR cohort and it must be declared as a nesting edge, not summed.
- access: OPEN — Public REST API v2, HTTP 200, no credential. NIH/NLM US Government work; no licence page fetched.
- worth: HIGH — Retires closed-access rows for the project's single most important positive compound and supplies the matched positive against the two negatives, under one shared threshold definition. The overlap with NEURO-TTR is a handling requirement, not a defect.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, NCT02211209 — APPROACH, volanesorsen in familial chylomicronaemia syndrome**  
- identifier: `NCT02211209`
- gap closed: The sharpest H6 available. I verified in measurements.csv that all 47 APPROACH rows presently cite "PMID 31390500; doi:10.1056/NEJMoa1715944" with source_table "Discussion, p540-541 narrative" — closed-access NEJM prose, and the second-largest closed block in the rights audit at 47 of the 224 contested rows. The placebo arm's 0 of 33 for thrombocytopenia under active monitoring also feeds H4.
- extract: The adverseEventsModule for NCT02211209 — the three platelet terms with numAffected/numAtRisk per group, plus the eventGroups block for the at-risk denominators — and then RE-SOURCE the existing 47 rows wherever the registry covers the same fact, moving them from C_closed_access to A_public_domain. Where the NEJM discussion says something the registry does not (the heparin-induced-mechanism narrative), keep the NEJM citation and leave that row closed rather than forcing a substitution.
- access: OPEN — Public REST API v2, HTTP 200, no credential. NIH/NLM US Government work; no licence page fetched.
