**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, NCT02386553 — NURTURE, nusinersen in presymptomatic infants**  
- identifier: `NCT02386553`
- gap closed: Longest exposure window of any nusinersen source available — up to Day 2891, roughly eight years — under explicitly enumerated platelet monitoring, with small but clean denominators (15, 10) and no placebo arm by design. For an H4 candidate, duration adequacy is usually the weakest clause; this is the strongest duration evidence in the endpoint. Complements DEVOTE, which is dose-escalated but only to Day 302.
- extract: The full class list of "Number of Participants With Shifts From Baseline in Clinical Laboratory Parameters (Hematology Parameters)" — page past the leukocyte classes to the "Platelets Shift to Low" and "Platelets Shift to High" rows and take those two with both denominators. Confirm the platelet classes are actually present before building a row; if they are absent, record that as a verified null and do not infer one from the description's parameter list.
- access: OPEN — Public REST API v2, HTTP 200, no credential. NIH/NLM US Government work; no licence page fetched.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, NCT03847909 (PHYOX2) and NCT05001269 — nedosiran / DCR-PHXC in primary hyperoxaluria**  
- identifier: `NCT03847909 and NCT05001269`
- gap closed: H1 — a new molecule tested in a human system, which is the only hit type that raises the molecule count; nedosiran is a GalNAc-conjugated siRNA, a chemistry class thinly represented among the endpoint's clinical compounds. H5 — placebo-controlled continuous platelet mean changes in PHYOX2 and a three-stratum paediatric set in NCT05001269, including mean platelet volume, which is a readout the dataset has little of. The paediatric decrements (-18.6 and -27.7 x 10^9/L) are the kind of signal that is invisible in a binary grade.
- extract: From NCT03847909: the "...Reticulocytes, Platelets, Leukocytes..." measure, class "Platelets", both arms with n=21/10, and the mean-platelet-volume measure. From NCT05001269: "Change From Baseline in Haematology Assessment: Platelets" with all three denominators, and the mean-platelet-volume companion. SEPARATE PREREQUISITE, and the reason this is MEDIUM not HIGH: H1 requires the sequence, and I did NOT verify a source for the nedosiran duplex sequence. Before cataloguing, resolve it from the WHO INN recommended-list entry for nedosiran or the Dicerna composition-of-matter patent, or from FDA GSRS/GINA at precision.fda.gov/ginas, which the project already uses for givosiran. If no sequence resolves, these two trials still land as H5 rows against a sequence-TBD compound, which is worth less and must not be entered as an H1.
- access: OPEN — Public REST API v2, HTTP 200, both studies, no credential. NIH/NLM US Government work; no licence page fetched.
- worth: MEDIUM — The only new-molecule lead I could verify with human platelet data attached, and it comes with placebo-controlled continuous values. Held at MEDIUM because the sequence leg of H1 is unverified by me and must be closed first.

**EP 4 500 170 A2 — "Methods for Separating Certain Oligonucleotide Compounds" (Ionis Pharmaceuticals, Inc.)**  
- identifier: `EP 4 500 170 A2, published 05.02.2025`
- gap closed: Aimed squarely at purity_pct = 0/259 for the 2'-MOE PS class that carries this endpoint's clinical weight (inotersen, volanesorsen, mipomersen, olezarsen, eplontersen). An Ionis SEPARATION/purification patent is structurally the most likely public document to tabulate per-compound chromatographic purity by name, because the figures are the applicant's own data and are not redacted the way a regulator's CCI section is. Ionis patents have already proven productive on this project (US 2023/0263822 A1 and US 11,535,849 B2 are both in the source register).
- extract: Fetch the full A2 specification, not the bibliographic endpoint — the EPO publication-server document in full, or the equivalent family member on Google Patents / Espacenet — and go straight to the Examples and any table reporting purity, %FLP, area % or impurity profile against a named compound or ISIS number, plus the sequence listing for per-position notation. Treat as a prospecting dig with an honest chance of nothing: record a null result in the coverage log if the examples turn out to be generic rather than compound-named.
- access: OPEN — Served without credential or payment from the EPO publication server, HTTP 200 on the document endpoint. EPO publication-server documents are freely retrievable; I saw no price or access requirement.
- worth: MEDIUM — MEDIUM, and deliberately so. The document is verified to exist and the route is the right one for the project's worst gap, but I have not seen one purity number in it. Ranked here rather than higher precisely so no one mistakes a verified existence for verified content.

**Australian Public Assessment Report (AusPAR) for nusinersen (as heptadecasodium), Spinraza — TGA**  
- identifier: `auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813.pdf (main AusPAR); companion clinical evaluation report auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813-cer.pdf and product information auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813.pi_.pdf, all at tga.gov.au/sites/default/files/`
- gap closed: Targets purity_pct 0/259 and Analytical_Identity_Method via a regulator that FDA/PMDA redaction practice does not cover. AusPAR "Quality findings" sections are generally less redacted than an FDA product-quality review, and the stereochemistry statement alone (phosphorus configuration undefined, 2^17 diastereoisomers) is characterization content the dataset does not hold and that bears directly on what a per-position map can legitimately claim about a PS backbone — relevant to the ARC1779, imetelstat and patisiran notation disputes in STATUS.md section 7.
- extract: Section "II. Quality findings" of auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813.pdf: the drug substance and drug product specification tables (purity / related substances test and its acceptance limit, assay limits, identity methods) and the stereochemistry paragraph. Retry from a different client or later — the three failures above were transport-level, so do not treat them as a null. If the quality section proves informative, the same route exists for the other oligo AusPARs (Onpattro, Tegsedi, Waylivra, Exondys) and would be the cheapest systematic purity sweep left. Also record the TGA copyright/licence statement on the page, since Commonwealth of Australia material is often CC BY and that would make it an H6 source too.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — I could not retrieve it. Three attempts: curl over HTTP/2 failed with "HTTP/2 stream 1 was not closed cleanly: INTERNAL_ERROR", curl forced to HTTP/1.1 failed with "Empty reply from server", and WebFetch returned HTTP 503 Service Unavailable. I observed NO price, NO purchase page and NO stated access requirement — TGA AusPARs are ordinarily free public documents and the failures look like transport or rate-limiting, not a paywall. I am recording ACCESS_UNKNOWN because that is what I can defend, not because I think it is restricted.
- worth: MEDIUM — A genuinely unqueried regulator on the project's worst gap, and the search record already shows the document contains real characterization substance. Held at MEDIUM strictly because I never opened it; a Rocksteady session with a working fetch should promote or kill it in one action.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- VERIFIED DEAD END, worth more than a lead because it stops wasted effort: FDA product-quality reviews do not carry numeric oligo purity. I downloaded 212154Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (viltolarsen, 711 pages, 2,181,318 bytes), extracted the text and grepped it. The word 'purity' occurs ONCE, in boilerplate ('quality, or purity, as'). Four redaction stamps account for the CMC content: '50 Page(s) have been Withheld in Full as b4 (CCI/TS)', '32 Page(s)...', '37 Page(s)...', '1 Page(s) of Draft Labeling...'. PMDA masks the same content differently but just as completely: Viltepso Tables 4, 5 and 6 are present with structure and footnotes intact but every numeric cell is asterisked, leaving only the 100 reference rows and the 80 mg/kg clinical / 360 mg/kg non-clinical doses. CONCLUSION FOR THE PROJECT: numeric purity acceptance criteria are systematically withheld by BOTH major regulators, so H3 numeric purity will not come from review reports. It must come from applicant-authored patents (where the data is the inventor's own and is not redacted, as US 11,332,489 B2 demonstrates with 71.2-71.6% full-length) or from author-deposited analytical supplements. The project's recovery_ledger entries that point at FDA CMC for purity should be re-pointed.
- EMA EPAR quality-aspects sections: NOT opened by me. I found files named ar_onpattro.pdf, ar_givlaari.pdf, ar_waylivra.pdf, ar_spinraza.pdf, ar_wainua.pdf, ar_qalsody.pdf, ar_leqvio.pdf, ar_oxlumo.pdf, ar_amvuttra.pdf, ar_rivfloza.pdf and tegsedi_ar.pdf already present in the shared scratchpad, timestamped minutes before my session's work, so another agent appears to be working that exact route and I deliberately did not duplicate it. Section 2.2 'Quality aspects' of those EPARs remains the most obvious untested purity route after the AusPAR, and whoever downloaded them should be asked what they found before anyone repeats it.
- WHO INN proposed/recommended-list documents: NOT searched. For oligonucleotides these carry the complete per-position chemical name and structural formula, which is a regulator-grade H2 source, and they would serve the 14 approved compounds whose modification_map is TBD. I ran out of budget before testing this route; it is the first thing I would do with another hour.
- Pharmacopoeial monographs: NO published oligonucleotide monograph verified. My search surfaced only USP's standards-development activity and a USP presentation indicating the BIO1 Expert Committee's focus is phosphoramidite raw-material quality rather than finished oligonucleotide products. I did NOT verify that any USP or Ph. Eur. monograph exists for any of the 17 approved oligos, and I am not claiming one does. Treat the pharmacopoeial route as unproven, not exhausted.
- EudraCT, WHO ICTRP and jRCT: NOT searched at all. jRCT in particular is the obvious place for the Japanese PMO and ASO trials (Nippon Shinyaku viltolarsen, NCNP) and would be the natural follow-on to the PMDA report, since the PMDA review I read contains no clinical platelet data.
- The PMO trial-grain gap the recovery ledger flags as critical is only partly addressed. I verified hasResults=true for eteplirsen PROMOVI (NCT02255552), golodirsen (NCT02310906) and viltolarsen RACER53 (NCT04060199), and hasResults=false for NCT02500381, but none of them carries a platelet-specific outcome measure: NCT02255552 has 7 outcome measures with no haematology hit, NCT04060199 has only 1, and NCT02310906's only relevant measure is the generic 'Potentially Clinically Significant (PCS) Laboratory Abnormalities Reported as TEAEs'. Their adverse-event modules may still hold platelet MedDRA terms with denominators and I did not pull them. So PMO class representation stays open.
- Companion PMDA file 000282161.pdf, returned alongside the Viltepso review by the same search, was not opened. PMDA review reports for nusinersen, patisiran, eplontersen and the other Japan-approved oligos were not searched at all — only viltolarsen.
- EP 4 500 170 A2 full specification not retrieved: the EPO publication-server endpoint returned only the bibliographic header, so no purity figure, no named compound and no sequence listing from that document has been seen by anyone on this project. The lead is existence-verified and content-unverified, and is tagged that way.
- The nedosiran duplex sequence is unverified. H1 credit for the two PHYOX trials depends on it, and I did not retrieve it from WHO INN, a Dicerna patent or FDA GSRS/GINA. Until it resolves, those trials are H5 only.
- The 56-row Zaslavsky 2021 block (10.1016/j.thromres.2021.01.006), the largest single closed-access source in the rights audit, has no open replacement in my list. I did not find an openly licensed source covering the same in-vitro hypersensitive-platelet readouts. The project's existing workaround (the NIHMS author manuscript in PMC) solves access but not redistribution, and that distinction should not be allowed to blur.
- Doctoral theses and preprints: NOT searched, despite the brief naming them. Theses on ASO platelet toxicity are the likeliest open source of per-oligo HPLC purity for the in-vitro panels, since candidates must document their own material, and they are usually openly licensed, which would serve H6 at the same time.

### coagulopathy (branch origin/claude/coagulopathy-oligos-toxicity-ap70gf, directory toxicity/coagulopathy) — 14 leads

**Gaps targeted:** Read at origin/claude/coagulopathy-oligos-toxicity-ap70gf: data/oligos.csv (218 rows), data/modifications.csv, data/measurements.csv (2,685 rows), data/studies.csv (198 study records), data/sources.csv (100 sources), SOURCES.md, STATUS.md, BEEBOP_RESEARCH_REQUEST_2026-10-02.md, ROCKSTEADY_REVIEW_REPLY_2026-10-01.md, BEEBOP_SUGGESTIONS_2026-09-30.md, and the root registers RESEARCH_SOURCE_COMPENDIUM.md / RESEARCH_ACCESS_REGISTER.md on origin/claude/amazing-galileo-rwiv95.  Recomputed directly from the tables rather than taken from prose:  (1) PURITY: purity_pct = NOT_REPORTED for 218/218 oligos. purity_method, identity_confirmation and synthesis_platform are also NOT_REPORTED for essentially the whole roster. The prior Rocksteady reply closes this gap as unreachable — "Any certificate of analysis for a clinically dosed compound ... it needs sponsor contact, which is not authorized." My leads attack that conclusion directly, and partly confirm it (see not_reached).  (2) POSITION CHEMISTRY, human participant subset: 38 compounds carry participant (clinical) rows. 27 of those 38 have ZERO rows in modifications.csv: COG-OLG004 rondoraptivon pegol, 006 RB007, 007 pegnivacogin, 008 anivam

**FDA/NCATS Inxight Drugs (Global Substance Registration System) nucleic-acid substance records for the 24 clinical compounds holding zero position-resolved chemistry**  
- identifier: `UNIIs retrieved and confirmed: fitusiran SV9W47ZLE1 (uuid aec13eec-5b61-4169-a9a3-61e49d1c02d4), inclisiran UOW2C71PG5, olezarsen S3RS2SA30L, eplontersen 0GRZ0F5XJ6, fesomersen 0CF4C9C787, teprasiran/QPI-1002 O03W5S19PJ, patisiran 50FKX8CB2Y, givosiran ROV204583W, lumasiran RZT8C352O1, vutrisiran GB4I2JI8UI, nedosiran 13U9R5J3WL, plozasiran XG9ARL6P25, eteplirsen AIW6036FAS, golodirsen 033072U4MZ, viltolarsen SXA7YP6EKX, casimersen X8UHF7SX0R, pegaptanib 2H1PA8H1EN, avacincaptad pegol TT0V5JLG5B, trecovirsen 9GA4548JGJ, alicaforsen/ISIS 2302 J8435V445B, anivamersen 4OXU6KPQ3R, pegnivacogin AYC3D1NPYB, rondaptivon pegol E4U6Y5R3KZ, donidalorsen ZD4D8M32TL`
- gap closed: The single mandatory Phase 2 field with the worst coverage on the clinical subset: 27 of 38 human-participant compounds have zero rows in modifications.csv. This one resource supplies full per-position sugar, backbone-linkage and conjugate assignments for 24 of those 27, plus the sequence for the ~14 of them that currently read sequence_base = NOT_REPORTED (inclisiran, patisiran, givosiran, lumasiran, nedosiran, plozasiran, eplontersen, olezarsen, viltolarsen, eteplirsen, pegaptanib, avacincaptad pegol, fitusiran, fesomersen, teprasiran, trecovirsen, alicaforsen, pegnivacogin, anivamersen, rondaptivon pegol). It also lifts project-wide modification coverage from 52/218. The three it does NOT cover are COG-OLG059/215 defibrotide (polydisperse, no single sequence exists) and COG-OLG113 IONIS-FXIRx/ISIS 416858 (no INN, absent from the registry). It additionally supplies 'rondaptivon pegol' as the registered INN spelling for COG-OLG004, which the repo records as 'rondoraptivon pegol' — the compound is unfindable in substance registries under the repo's spelling.
- extract: For each UNII above: GET https://drugs.ncats.io/api/v1/substances/search?q=<name> to get the uuid, then GET https://drugs.ncats.io/api/v1/substances(<uuid>)?view=full and read exactly four paths — nucleicAcid.subunits[].sequence plus subunitIndex and length (strand-resolved sequence), nucleicAcid.sugars[].sugar with .sitesShorthand (per-position sugar), nucleicAcid.linkages[].linkage with .sitesShorthand (per-position backbone), and nucleicAcid.modifications.structuralModifications / agentModifications (conjugates and terminal groups). The sitesShorthand grammar is subunit_position with ';' separating sites and '-' denoting an inclusive range, which maps one-to-one onto the repo's modifications.csv position rows. Prefer the free-acid record over the '... SODIUM' record: the sodium records re-label the same linkages as naP/nasP, which would create spurious distinct chemistries. Take the free-acid UNII as the identity key and keep the sodium UNII as an alias.
- access: OPEN — Public domain, explicitly. Retrieved from the NCATS Inxight Drugs terms statement: "All facts appearing in these datasets are in the public domain and may be reproduced or copied without NCATS permission." No licence agreement (CC0/MIT etc.) is asserted; the public-domain declaration is the governing term, and users are asked to cite the 2022 Nucleic Acids Research manuscript. API access needed no key and no login.
- worth: HIGH — Ranks first on the project's own ordering (H2 and H3 are the mandatory fields) and is the largest single coverage gain available: one openly licensed, machine-readable, public-domain resource closes the top-ranked mandatory field for 24 of 27 clinical compounds. It is also directly redistributable, which matters for a branch carrying 254 publisher_restricted rows and no LICENSE file. One caveat to carry into curation, found during verification: the rondaptivon pegol record assigns unmodified ribose to positions 1_1-1_29 with a 3' inverted deoxyribose, which is inconsistent with the 2'-OMe chemistry published for that aptamer family. GSRS is a curated registry, not a primary source, so each record should be cross-checked against the primary literature or the originator patent before it is treated as source-verified, and value_origin should record it as registry-derived rather than publication-derived.

**Yu et al. 2024, aptameric hirudins as selective and reversible EXosite-ACTive site (EXACT) inhibitors — Supplementary Information Table 1 and the Source Data workbook**  
- identifier: `PMC11087511 / PMID 38730234 / doi 10.1038/s41467-024-48211-6; supplementary files 41467_2024_48211_MOESM1_ESM.pdf and 41467_2024_48211_MOESM4_ESM.xlsx`
- gap closed: Position chemistry for the 11-compound COG-S010 block, which currently has zero modification rows and reads sequence_base = NOT_REPORTED for 8 of 11: COG-OLG029 HD22-7A-DAB, 032 HD23-7A-DAB, 033 HD22-7A-NH2, 035 HD22-0A-DAB, 036 HD22-20A-DAB, 037 11F7t, 038 11F7t-20A-DAB, plus COG-OLG030 HD22. For 11F7t the table resolves every single position (rG/rA/FC/FU) — this is the hardest kind of chemistry to recover and the paper body does not carry it. It also adds roughly 12 molecules the roster does not hold at all (HD22-2A/5A/10A/15A/25A/30A-DAB, the four Bio- variants, 11F7t-20A-APX, AO1-AO9 antidotes, AO5.4), all tested in human plasma clotting assays. The 'Clotting assays' sheet in the Source Data workbook addresses the H5 gap for the same compounds, and the PAGE/HPLC statements give purity_method and identity_confirmation values for compounds where both fields are NOT_REPORTED.
- extract: From 41467_2024_48211_MOESM1_ESM.pdf: 'Supplementary Table 1. Oligonucleotides used in this work' (the oligo-ID/sequence table with the modification legend immediately beneath it) — transcribe one modifications.csv row per position per compound using the legend tokens, and record the poly-A linker length as a design field rather than folding it into the sequence. From 41467_2024_48211_MOESM4_ESM.xlsx: the 'Clotting assays' sheet for raw continuous aPTT/PT/TT values, and the 'HPLC' and 'SEC' sheets for the analytical identity traces. Do not use the article body for chemistry; it does not carry the per-position notation.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved without login or payment from the Europe PMC REST supplementaryFiles endpoint (HTTP 200, application/zip). The repo already records this source as CC_BY in data/sources.csv, and Nature Communications Source Data files carry the article licence.
- worth: HIGH — Hits the two top-ranked types at once (H2 and H3) for a human in-vitro block, which is the Challenge's explicit priority, and throws in H1 and H5. The file is already in hand and the extraction is a transcription task rather than a judgement call, so a Rocksteady session can land it tomorrow. One honest limit: the PAGE/HPLC statements are a characterisation METHOD, not a numeric purity value, so they populate purity_method and identity_confirmation but leave purity_pct unknown.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results for NCT00770146 — mipomersen as add-on therapy, placebo-controlled, adverse events reported at a frequency threshold of zero**  
- identifier: `NCT00770146`
- gap closed: The project's single highest-value absence: zero clean clinical negatives, which the lead scientist is using to block a classifier. This record supplies every element the H4 definition demands, for a compound whose chemistry the repo ALREADY holds in full — COG-OLG216 mipomersen sodium carries a sequence and 20 position-resolved modification rows. Exact sequence: held. Human exposure with dose and duration: 26 weeks of dosing, observation to Week 28. Explicit platelet and coagulation monitoring: Thrombocytopenia, Platelet count decreased and Prothrombin time prolonged all appear as reported terms, so the readout was unambiguously surveilled. Explicit outcome with traceable denominator: thrombocytopenia 0 of 105 on drug against 1 of 52 on placebo. The decisive detail is frequencyThreshold = 0 — all adverse events are tabulated with no reporting cut-off, so a zero is a measured zero rather than a sub-threshold suppression. That distinction is what makes this a usable negative instead of a missing value, and it is the thing the FDA label and the FAERS tables the repo already holds cannot give.
- extract: resultsSection.adverseEventsModule in full: eventGroups (ids, titles, otherNumAtRisk = 52 and 105) and every otherEvents entry whose MedDRA term is haematological, coagulation or haemorrhagic, each with stats[].numAffected and stats[].numAtRisk. Record adverseEventsModule.frequencyThreshold = 0 as a dataset field, because it is the evidence that an absent term means zero rather than unreported. Also pull protocolSection.armsInterventionsModule for the exact dose and schedule and designModule.enrollmentInfo for the denominator provenance. Fetch via https://clinicaltrials.gov/api/v2/studies/NCT00770146 with no fields parameter — passing a fields list returns HTTP 400 and strips the results section.
- access: OPEN — US Government work, public domain. Retrieved over the public ClinicalTrials.gov v2 REST API with no key, no login and no payment (HTTP 200).
- worth: HIGH — Highest-leverage single record in this report. H4 is the type the project has none of, and this one is unusually strong because the chemistry side is already complete in our own tables, so the row is immediately modellable with no sequence recovery needed. It also carries a placebo arm, which gives the classifier a contrast rather than a bare absence. The only caution to record is that mipomersen is a known hepatotoxicity and injection-site-reaction drug, so the injection-site haematoma imbalance (17/105 vs 3/52) must be curated as local injection-site pathology and kept separate from systemic coagulopathy, or the negative will be contaminated by a positive.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results for NCT04089566 (DEVOTE) — nusinersen, with platelet count and aPTT/PT/INR shifts as prespecified outcome measures**  
- identifier: `NCT04089566`
- gap closed: A second, independent clean clinical negative, and the strongest-designed one available: coagulation monitoring here is not an incidental safety table but a PRESPECIFIED PRIMARY ENDPOINT, which removes any argument that the readout was unmeasured. Nusinersen's exact sequence and all 18 position-resolved modifications are already held (COG-OLG054), and GSRS independently confirms them (TCACCTTTATAATGCTGG, qR 1_1-1_18, sP 1_2-1_18), so the molecular side is complete and double-sourced. Crucially this is NOT already held: studies.csv carries NCT04089566 under COG-STU192 with coagulation_endpoints = NOT_REPORTED, sourced from COG-S049, the SPINRAZA DailyMed label, at locus 'Section 14.1/14.2 ... Section 5.1 Thrombocytopenia and Coagulation Ab...' — label prose with no denominators. The posted results replace that prose with six arm-level denominators and per-parameter numerators, including true zeros (PT 0/6 and 0/34; platelet below LLN 0% of 6, of 8, of 16, of 50). It also supplies dose-response structure across 12/12, 28/28 and 50/28 mg regimens, which is H5 material a binary cannot carry.
- extract: resultsSection.outcomeMeasuresModule — the eight coagulation measures named above (four Parts A/C primaries, four Part B secondaries), taking title, unitOfMeasure, paramType, denoms[].counts (groupId plus value) and classes[].categories[].measurements[] for each arm. Then resultsSection.adverseEventsModule for the threshold-0 haematological and haemorrhagic terms with numAffected/numAtRisk per group. Record frequencyThreshold = 0 and the per-arm dose from protocolSection.armsInterventionsModule. Keep this as a distinct study record rather than merging into COG-STU192, and note the upgrade of its source from label prose to posted results.
- access: OPEN — US Government work, public domain. Retrieved over the public ClinicalTrials.gov v2 REST API, HTTP 200, no key or login.
- worth: HIGH — Close second to NCT00770146 on H4 and arguably better evidence, because prespecified primary coagulation endpoints are harder for a reviewer to dismiss than an adverse-event table, and the high-dose arms give a dose-response rather than a single negative. Combined with threshold-0 AE reporting and 145 participants it gives both clean negatives and graded positives from one record. The curation caveat worth writing down is that 'shift from baseline' is a categorical shift against a laboratory normal range the registry does not publish, which is the same soft-grade problem STATUS.md already flags for grade 1 — so these should be curated as source-stated shift counts with grade_authority = source, not converted into CTCAE grades.

**Soule et al. 2022, anticoagulant aptamer targeting factor V/Va — supplementary mmc1.pdf, Tables S1, S3 and S4**  
- identifier: `PMC8808741 / PMID 35114109 / doi 10.1016/j.chembiol.2022.01.009, Cell Chemical Biology; supplementary files mmc1.pdf, mmc2.zip, mmc3.pdf`
- gap closed: The COG-S007 block is the worst-documented human in-vitro block in the endpoint: six compounds (COG-OLG009 T18.3, 010 Mut1, 011 sequence 2.1, 012 sequence 2.2, 013 T18, 014 SEL2 library) all carrying sequence_base = NOT_REPORTED and zero modification rows, from a source the repo marks publisher_restricted. Table S1 closes the sequence gap for all six by name and makes their chemistry fully position-resolvable, because 'all C and U are 2'F modified' resolves to an exact per-position assignment once the sequence is known — every C and U position is 2'-F, every A and G is unmodified ribose. It also adds about 20 molecules the roster does not hold (T18.1, T18.2, T18.4-T18.9, and the 1.1-7.1 selection panel), each tested in human plasma, which raises the molecule count. Table S4 gives raw aPTT values in COVID-19 patient plasma — human, disease-context, continuous.
- extract: From mmc1.pdf: Table S1 in full (one modifications.csv row per C and U position per compound, plus the 5' biotin terminal group for T18.3-Bio); Table S4 (aPTT values in COVID-19 patient plasma, as raw continuous measurements with the donor context); Table S3 (T18.3 plus LMWH synergy, as co_administered_agent rows). Then open mmc2.zip and mmc3.pdf, which were retrieved but not inspected in this run. Download route that worked: https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC8808741/supplementaryFiles?includeInlineImage=false
- access: OPEN — The supplement downloaded without login or payment (HTTP 200, 3.76 MB zip). But the licence is conditional and must be reported precisely rather than as 'open': the permissions block retrieved from the Europe PMC full-text XML reads '© 2022 Elsevier Ltd.' followed by the COVID-19 resource-centre grant — 'Elsevier hereby grants permission to make all its COVID-19-related research that is available on the COVID-19 resource centre - including this research content - immediately available in PubMed Central and other publicly funded repositories ... with rights for unrestricted research re-use and analyses in any form or by any means with acknowledgement of the original source. These permissions are granted for free by Elsevier for as long as the COVID-19 resource centre remains active.' So re-use for research is expressly granted with attribution, but the grant is time-limited to the resource centre's existence and is not a standing CC licence.
- worth: HIGH — Closes the worst sequence-and-chemistry gap in the human in-vitro population and raises the molecule count, which is the only thing that does. The H6 angle is real but must be stated carefully: this is a conditional publisher grant, not a CC licence, so it supports extracting and citing values and arguably supports redistribution with acknowledgement, but the time limitation means Oscar's licensing call should be recorded rather than assumed. I would reclassify COG-S007 from publisher_restricted to a new 'conditional_publisher_grant' value rather than to CC_BY, and keep the exact quoted grant in sources.csv so the basis is auditable.

**ClinicalTrials.gov threshold-zero adverse-event bundle: twelve further posted-results trials of compounds whose position chemistry the repo already holds**  
- identifier: `NCT00707746 (mipomersen), NCT01703988 and NCT01839656 (nusinersen), NCT04306510 (inotersen), NCT05583552 and NCT01836549 (imetelstat), NCT03728634 (eplontersen), plus NCT01475825, NCT00607373, NCT00694109, NCT00794664, NCT02554773`
- gap closed: Turns the single H4 find into a defensible H4 population. These twelve trials cover compounds whose sequence and per-position chemistry are ALREADY complete in the repo — mipomersen (20 mods), nusinersen (18), imetelstat (13), inotersen (20), eplontersen (0 mods but fully covered by lead 1), custirsen (21) — so each yields immediately modellable sequence-to-phenotype rows. NCT01703988 is a four-dose-level clean negative in 34 participants. NCT01836549 is the opposite pole: graded, dose-stratified aPTT prolongation and platelet decrease with per-stratum denominators, which is exactly the positive-control material a classifier needs opposite the negatives. None of these twelve NCTs appears in studies.csv. NCT01703988 is also a strong candidate identity for COG-STU032, the nusinersen phase 1 whose registry_id currently reads NOT_REPORTED.
- extract: For each NCT: GET https://clinicaltrials.gov/api/v2/studies/<NCT> with no fields parameter, then read resultsSection.adverseEventsModule.frequencyThreshold first and discard the record for negative-control purposes unless it is 0. Then take eventGroups[] (id, title, otherNumAtRisk, seriousNumAtRisk) and every otherEvents[]/seriousEvents[] entry with a haematological, coagulation or haemorrhagic MedDRA term, with stats[].numAffected and stats[].numAtRisk per group. Store frequencyThreshold and the AE timeFrame as dataset columns. Reconcile NCT01703988 against COG-STU032 on enrolment (34) and dose levels (3, 6, 9, 12 mg) before asserting the identity.
- access: OPEN — US Government work, public domain. All records retrieved over the public ClinicalTrials.gov v2 REST API, HTTP 200, no key, no login, no payment.
- worth: HIGH — Second-highest leverage after the GSRS lead, because it converts the project's zero-H4 state into a set with both negatives and dose-graded positives on compounds where the chemistry is already done. The operational insight is reusable and cheap: filter posted-results trials on adverseEventsModule.frequencyThreshold == 0 and only those tables license a zero as a measured zero. Two cautions. First, NCT04306510 (inotersen, n=7) reports no coagulation term, but inotersen is the project's known thrombocytopenia signal and n=7 is far too small to support a negative — report it, do not use it as one. Second, every threshold-5 trial must be excluded from negative-control duty; I verified NCT01475825, NCT04534114 and NCT02851407 all sit at threshold 5.

**Soule, Erin Elizabeth (2016), Development and Characterization of Monovalent and Bivalent RNA Aptamers Targeting the Common Pathway of Coagulation — Duke University doctoral dissertation**  
- identifier: `hdl.handle.net/10161/12224; DukeSpace item uuid f148cd60-8c25-4a5e-ad13-c5ab2d4e395e; file Soule_duke_0066D_13434.pdf`
- gap closed: Independent, openly accessible confirmation and extension of the COG-S007 sequence gap (COG-OLG009-014, all sequence_base = NOT_REPORTED), from the first author of that very paper. Adds molecules the roster does not hold at all — R9c11, 11HMT, 11Mut2, R8c7, 18.3Mut1, RNABA4, R9D-14t and the bivalent constructs of Tables 4 and 5 — each with a sequence and human-plasma aPTT/PT data, so it raises the molecule count. Figures 17, 18, 19 and 22 give raw continuous aPTT and PT titrations in human platelet-poor and platelet-rich plasma and in congenital FV Leiden patient plasma, against curator-derived binaries. Figures 35-38 add paired animal ACT/aPTT, which feeds the human-to-animal extrapolation axis that STATUS.md reports is bounded at 30 of 218 compounds.
- extract: From Soule_duke_0066D_13434.pdf: Table 1 (p.60), Table 4 (p.112) and Table 5 (p.113) for sequences; Table 6 (p.124) and Table 7 (p.125) for binding affinities; Figures 17, 18, 19 and 22 for raw human-plasma aPTT and PT titration values with concentrations; Figures 35-38 for the animal ACT/aPTT series. Read the in-vitro transcription methods section for the 2'-F pyrimidine chemistry before writing any modification rows, and record value_origin as thesis-derived. Retrieval route that worked when the web page did not: the DSpace REST API at /server/api/core/items/<uuid>, then /bundles, then /bitstreams, then the bitstream content href.
- access: OPEN — Freely downloadable with no login and no payment: HTTP 200, 9,470,413 bytes, content-type application/pdf, from https://dukespace.lib.duke.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/0028cc71-02f9-4d1c-a4fe-56b7b71ca975/content. Licence: NOT stated — dc.rights and dc.rights.uri are both empty in the DSpace metadata. So the file is openly accessible but carries no explicit re-use licence, which is a weaker H6 position than a CC grant and should be recorded as such rather than claimed as open-licensed.
- worth: MEDIUM — Strong on H1 and H5 and genuinely open, and the fact that it is by the same first author as a publisher_restricted source we depend on makes it a near-ideal independent route. Ranked below the leads above for two honest reasons. First, I checked and it does NOT serve H3: a term count over the extracted text returned purity 0 occurrences and HPLC 0 occurrences, so despite 'Characterization' in the title it carries no purity or chromatographic identity data — do not propose it as a purity source. Second, its chemistry contribution is partial: '2'-fluoro' appears only once and the aptamers are made by in-vitro transcription, so per-position assignment has to come from the transcription chemistry described in the methods rather than from a modification table, and should be curated at lower confidence than the Soule 2022 Table S1 rule.

**EMA public assessment reports not held by this endpoint: Leqvio (inclisiran) and Oxlumo (lumasiran) — quality-aspects sections and the clinical haematology/coagulation sections**  
- identifier: `Leqvio EPAR public assessment report, EMA/696912/2020; Oxlumo EPAR public assessment report (EMA document, retrieved 2026-10-03)`
- gap closed: On H3, these populate purity_method, identity_confirmation and synthesis_platform — three fields that read NOT_REPORTED across essentially all 218 oligos — for inclisiran (COG-OLG053) and lumasiran (COG-OLG130), with named methods: anion-exchange HPLC for purity and impurity grouping, structure elucidation confirming both single-strand sequences and the duplex, retention-time-based impurity grouping with set limits. Neither EPAR is in data/sources.csv; the repo holds inclisiran only through COG-S018 (Maliglowka, 16 rows) and lumasiran only through the GIVLAARI/OXLUMO negative-control label bundle COG-S054. On H4, the Leqvio report supplies an explicit sponsor-and-regulator-reviewed statement of no clinically meaningful coagulation change in a pooled placebo-controlled safety analysis, for a compound whose exact per-position chemistry lead 1 supplies (UNII UOW2C71PG5).
- extract: From each PDF: section 2.2 Quality aspects, specifically the 'Active substance specification' subsection listing the test battery with its analytical methods, and the impurity-grouping and structure-elucidation paragraphs — these fill purity_method and identity_confirmation. Then the clinical safety section headed 'Haematology and Coagulation' (Leqvio: page 121/143) together with the pooled safety-set denominators from the table the section cites as ISS Table 14.3.3.1.2.2. Do not transfer a group-level impurity limit to any individual batch; record it as a specification, not a measurement.
- access: OPEN — Both retrieved with no login and no payment, HTTP 200, multi-megabyte application/pdf, from www.ema.europa.eu/en/documents/assessment-report/<product>-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf. Note that the repo currently classes held EPARs as publisher_restricted (COG-S076/079/082/083) while classing others as public_domain (COG-S098/099/100) — an inconsistency in our own register worth resolving, since EMA applies one reuse policy to all of them. I did not retrieve the EMA reuse policy text itself, so I am not asserting which classification is correct.
- worth: MEDIUM — Ranked on the project's own criteria this is top-tier (H3 is joint-highest) and both files are in hand, which is why it is here. Ranked below the leads above for one honest reason: these documents supply the purity METHOD and the identity battery, not a numeric purity value, so purity_pct stays NOT_REPORTED. The H4 contribution is also weaker than the registry leads — it is a prose negative, and the denominator has to be taken from the pooled safety-set table that the same section cites as ISS Table 14.3.3.1.2.2 rather than from a per-parameter table, so it should be curated as a source-stated qualitative negative with a traceable but indirect denominator, and ranked beneath NCT00770146 and NCT04089566. A practical note: EMA rate-limits, returning HTTP 429 with a 40,642-byte body. Three of my seven probes (Rivfloza, Wainua, Amvuttra) were rate-limited rather than missing, and identical 40,642-byte files already sitting in the scratchpad from an earlier session are those same 429 pages, not real documents — so retry with spacing rather than concluding the files do not exist.

**Defitelio (defibrotide) EMA public assessment report — active substance characterisation, the only quantitative characterisation available for the project's four polydisperse defibrotide entries**  
- identifier: `Defitelio EPAR public assessment report, EMA/CHMP/824715/2012`
- gap closed: The repo holds four defibrotide entries — COG-OLG057 (porcine API), 058 (ovine API), 059 (DEFITELIO injection), 215 (defibrotide) — all with purity_pct, purity_method, identity_confirmation and sequence all NOT_REPORTED or NOT_APPLICABLE and zero modification rows, and COG-OLG059 and 215 carry participant rows. These are the roster entries where sequence_status correctly records that the gap cannot be closed because the material is polydisperse. This report supplies what CAN be characterised and quantified for exactly that case: mean length 50 bases, mean molecular weight 17 ± 4 kDa, at least 40% above 12 kDa, nucleobase percentage and average double-stranded character, with the explicit regulator statement of what is and is not knowable. It is the only genuinely numeric characterisation I found anywhere in this run.
- extract: Section 2.2.2 Active Substance (the characterisation paragraph quoted above) and section 2.3.1 Introduction (the '50 bases / 17 ± 4 kDa / at least 40% being >12 kDa' sentence) into purity_method, identity_confirmation and a polydisperse-characterisation note; the Specification subsection of section 2.2 for the test battery and the unresolved cell-protection assay validation. Record these as material characterisation of the active substance, not as a per-batch purity value, and keep the porcine (COG-OLG057) and ovine (COG-OLG058) entries distinct — this report characterises the porcine-derived product.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved with no login and no payment, HTTP 200, 1,742,975 bytes, application/pdf, direct from the EMA document server.
- worth: MEDIUM — Serves the top-ranked type (H3) with real numbers rather than methods, which nothing else in this report does, and it resolves a gap the repo had written off as unclosable — turning four NOT_REPORTED rows into a defensible polydisperse characterisation. Ranked mid-list only because it covers four compounds rather than twenty-four and adds no human outcome rows. It carries a second benefit worth noting: the regulator's own sentence about what can and cannot be determined for a polydisperse mixture is quotable justification for the dataset's NOT_APPLICABLE sequence handling, which is the kind of defensibility a reviewer will probe.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results for NCT04534114 (RE-THINC ESRD) — fesomersen factor XI ASO, 307 participants, placebo-controlled bleeding as the primary endpoint**  
- identifier: `NCT04534114`
- gap closed: Dose-resolved human bleeding outcomes against placebo for a factor XI ASO, with person-year denominators — the largest such dataset I found. Fesomersen is COG-OLG115, currently holding zero modification rows and no sequence; lead 1 supplies its exact 20-mer and a position-resolved mixed PO/PS backbone (UNII 0CF4C9C787), so the pair makes a complete sequence-to-phenotype record. The repo currently holds fesomersen only through COG-S041 (Lang et al., phase 1 in healthy Chinese volunteers, 31 rows); this adds a 307-participant phase 2b in ESRD patients across three dose levels. The continuous primary endpoint in n/100 person-years with a spread per arm is H5 material against curator binaries, and it also bears directly on the STATUS.md concern that the full-PS class-effect finding rests on a single source carrying 42 of 48 rows.
- extract: resultsSection.outcomeMeasuresModule: the primary MB+CRNMB composite with unitOfMeasure, paramType, all four denoms[].counts and the per-arm measurements with spread, and the secondary FXI antigen change measure. Then resultsSection.adverseEventsModule for the Thrombocytopenia row with numAffected/numAtRisk per group, recording frequencyThreshold = 5 alongside it so no downstream reader mistakes an absent term for a zero. Pull dose per arm (40/80/120 mg vs placebo) from protocolSection.armsInterventionsModule.
- access: OPEN — US Government work, public domain. Retrieved over the public ClinicalTrials.gov v2 REST API, HTTP 200, no key or login.
- worth: MEDIUM — Excellent H5 and a genuinely useful H4 — bleeding here is the measured primary endpoint with a placebo arm and person-year denominators, which is strong evidence regardless of the AE threshold. Ranked below the two clean-negative leads for a specific verified reason: frequencyThreshold = 5, so the AE table suppresses anything under 5%, and the single Thrombocytopenia term cannot be read as a complete haematological picture. Absences in this record must NOT be curated as zeros. Use the primary and secondary outcome measures, which are threshold-independent, as the evidence; use the AE table only for the terms it actually prints.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results for NCT02876601 — defibrotide in the human endotoxemia model, raw continuous thrombin-generation and thromboelastometry values**  
- identifier: `NCT02876601`
- gap closed: Raw continuous coagulation values with per-arm denominators and dispersion, for defibrotide — a compound with four roster entries (COG-OLG057/058/059/215) all carrying zero modification rows and no purity, where the repo's evidence is otherwise the US label, the Abourahma comparative study and FAERS. The measured panel is specifically mechanistic coagulation chemistry (prothrombin fragment F1+2, thrombin-antithrombin complexes, thromboelastometry clotting time and maximum lysis) rather than clinical event counts, which is the shape H5 asks for. The four-arm design includes a defibrotide-without-LPS arm (n=3) and a double-placebo arm (n=4), giving both a drug-alone control and a true baseline. Not in studies.csv.
- extract: resultsSection.outcomeMeasuresModule: all four coagulation measures named above, taking title, unitOfMeasure, paramType (MEDIAN), denoms[].counts per arm and classes[].categories[].measurements[] with value and spread. Record the normalised unit verbatim and do not convert it to absolute units. Flag the negative clotting-time value in the n=3 arm for adjudication rather than ingesting it silently. Also capture the LPS challenge as co_administered_agent, since the coagulation activation is driven by endotoxin, not by defibrotide.
- access: OPEN — US Government work, public domain. Retrieved over the public ClinicalTrials.gov v2 REST API, HTTP 200, no key or login.
- worth: MEDIUM — The cleanest raw-continuous human coagulation data I found, and it is in a controlled challenge design rather than an observational safety table, so the values are interpretable. Ranked mid-list because n=20 overall with two arms at n=3 and n=4 is small, the units are normalised ('fold-change*h' area-under-curve style) rather than absolute seconds or pmol/L, and one reported value is negative with a nonsensical spread (clotting time -0.2 with spread -6 in the n=3 arm), which should be flagged on ingest rather than curated as-is. Defibrotide is also polydisperse, so these rows will never link to a sequence — valuable as human coagulation phenotype, not as sequence-to-phenotype training data.

**Nagano et al. 2023, neutralisable dimeric anti-thrombin aptamer — supplementary mmc1.pdf Figure S11 and Figure S12, and the uninspected mmc2.pdf**  
- identifier: `PMC10445101 / PMID 37621412 / doi 10.1016/j.omtn.2023.07.030; supplementary files mmc1.pdf and mmc2.pdf`
- gap closed: COG-S074 is the endpoint's largest human in-vitro block — 32 oligos, COG-OLG174-204 — with zero position-resolved chemistry across all of them. Figure S12(a) supplies the exact construct composition for the Pse(08-29) dimer including the 17-mer linker and the TBA29 module, which the article body does not give, plus the nine antidote sequences matching COG-OLG186-193. Figure S12(b) supplies relative clotting time in human plasma with both aptamer and antidote concentrations and the 28.7 s reference value, which is raw continuous human in-vitro data against curator binaries. Figure S11 adds half-lives in 50% human serum paired with 50% mouse serum, which feeds the human-to-animal extrapolation axis directly.
- extract: From mmc1.pdf: the Entry/Sequence/Length table in Figure S12(a) (the Pse(08-29) construct plus the nine antidotes) and the relative-clotting-time values in Figure S12(b) with their stated concentrations and the 28.7 s reference; the half-life values in Figure S11(c) split by human and mouse serum. Then OCR page 13 to recover Table S1, and inspect mmc2.pdf (11,658,451 B), which was downloaded but not opened in this run. Download route: https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC10445101/supplementaryFiles?includeInlineImage=false
- access: OPEN — Retrieved without login or payment from the Europe PMC REST supplementaryFiles endpoint (HTTP 200, 18.3 MB zip). The repo already records this source as CC_BY in data/sources.csv.
- worth: MEDIUM — Targets the biggest human in-vitro block in the endpoint and the files are already in hand. Ranked mid-list for an honest reason I verified rather than assumed: the one table that would close the chemistry gap for all 32 compounds, Table S1, has no text layer, so this lead is an OCR job on mmc1.pdf page 13 plus an inspection of the 11.7 MB mmc2.pdf, neither of which I completed. There is also a real possibility that these are unmodified DNA aptamers, in which case the per-position chemistry is uniformly 2'-deoxyribose with phosphodiester linkages — still a legitimate position-resolved assignment and worth recording explicitly rather than leaving as NOT_REPORTED, but a smaller gain than the 2'-F and 2'-OMe panels in leads 2 and 5.

**Registry-identifier resolution for the headline trials reading NOT_REPORTED: HELIOS-A confirmed, custirsen and mipomersen narrowed to candidate sets**  
- identifier: `NCT03759379 (HELIOS-A, vutrisiran); NCT01188187 SYNERGY, NCT01578655 AFFINITY, NCT01630733 ENSPIRIT (custirsen phase 3); NCT00607373 RADICHOL 1, NCT00706849 RADICHOL II, NCT00770146, NCT00794664, NCT01475825 FOCUS FH, NCT00694109, NCT01598948 MICA (mipomersen phase 3)`
- gap closed: 12 of 30 headline trials read registry_id = NOT_REPORTED, and the Beebop request states plainly that trial-registry sources with verifiable identifiers are valuable and that a defensible distinct human-trial total is currently unknown. This resolves COG-STU094 (HELIOS-A) to NCT03759379 on an exact enrolment match, and reduces the custirsen cluster (COG-STU030/075/076/077) and the mipomersen cluster (COG-STU003/010/011/015) from unidentified to small candidate sets that can be disambiguated on enrolment and arm structure. Because six of the seven mipomersen phase 3 records carry posted results, resolving that cluster also unlocks the H4/H5 tables in lead 3 and lead 6 against the right study records instead of orphaned ones.
- extract: For each candidate NCT: protocolSection.identificationModule (nctId, acronym, officialTitle, secondaryIdModule for the sponsor protocol number), designModule.enrollmentInfo.count and designModule.phases, then match against studies.csv on enrolled, phase, compounds and sponsor_protocol before writing any registry_id. Record the match basis in identity_basis and leave registry_id as NOT_REPORTED wherever the match is not unique — a wrong identifier is worse than a missing one on a project already damaged by two misattributions.
- access: OPEN — US Government work, public domain. All queries over the public ClinicalTrials.gov v2 REST API, HTTP 200, no key or login.
- worth: MEDIUM — Cheap, verifiable and it directly attacks a gap the review names, while also being the prerequisite for ingesting the H4 and H5 material above against correct study identities. Ranked here rather than higher because it is metadata rather than new measurement, and because I must be explicit about what is NOT resolved: STU011 ('29 mip...') and STU015 ('141 pa...') match none of the seven mipomersen enrolments I retrieved; the IONIS-PKKRx phase 1 (STU091, n=49 randomised) does not appear on ClinicalTrials.gov — the only PKKRx record returned is NCT03108469, a phase 2 with n=30, which is a different study; the fitusiran phase 1 (STU089, n=46/45) matches neither NCT02035605 nor NCT02554773 (n=34); and the nusinersen phase 1 (STU032) has four plausible candidates. Beebop's instruction stands: multiple or ambiguous identifiers should trigger linkage review, not automatic assignment.

**Buller et al. 2015, factor XI antisense oligonucleotide for prevention of venous thrombosis (FXI-ASO TKA) — NEJM supplementary appendix via the PubMed Central author manuscript**  
- identifier: `PMC4367537 / PMID 25482425 / doi 10.1056/NEJMoa1405760`
- gap closed: COG-S039 is the NEJM FXI-ASO TKA paper, marked publisher_restricted, contributing 34 rows and zero oligos. It is one of the restricted sources the Phase 2 licensing requirement puts at risk, and its supplementary appendix holds the arm-level bleeding and adverse-event tables with denominators that the 34 curated rows summarise. An NIH-funded author manuscript in PMC is reachable under the NIH Public Access Policy, which would give a route to the underlying tables that does not depend on the publisher — serving H5 (raw arm-level values behind curator binaries) and partially H6 (a public-access route to a restricted dependency).
- extract: The supplementary appendix tables giving per-arm bleeding and adverse-event counts with at-risk denominators for the 200 mg and 300 mg FXI-ASO regimens against enoxaparin, plus the protocol-discontinued 100 mg regimen that COG-S039 already notes. Try in order: the PMC article page at pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4367537/ for named supplementary objects; then the NIHMS manuscript package; then retry the Europe PMC supplementaryFiles endpoint. Record the outcome against the access-register status definitions, distinguishing missing supplement from confirmed publisher paywall — do not infer the latter.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — I did not retrieve the file and I did not see any price, purchase page or stated access requirement. The Europe PMC supplementaryFiles endpoint returned an empty 296-byte XML response rather than an error or a payment prompt. This is an unexplained empty response, not evidence of a paywall — the record itself reports hasSuppl Y. Access must be reported as unknown until the PMC article page or the NIHMS package is checked directly.
- worth: LOW — Included deliberately as an honest unverified lead rather than dropped, because the review explicitly asks for the FXI-ASO safety appendices and because the same block is also prioritised for the ATLAS-A/B and APPROACH appendices. Ranked last: I could not retrieve it, the open-registry fallback is verified not to exist, and its value is partly superseded by lead 10 (NCT04534114), which gives 307 participants of placebo-controlled factor XI ASO bleeding data in the public domain and needs no appendix at all. A session should try the PMC article page and the NIHMS package before spending long on it, and should not record it as acquired or as paywalled on this evidence.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- NUMERIC PURITY FOR CLINICALLY DOSED MATERIAL — NOT FOUND, and I now believe it is largely not public. This is the most important negative result in this report and it qualifies lead 8 and lead 9. I checked four independent document classes and each redacts or omits the number while publishing the method. FDA Chemistry Review: the viltolarsen review text in the session scratchpad prints acceptance criteria as '(b) (4) %' — trade-secret redaction. EMA EPAR: the Waylivra report lists the full battery ('identification (sequencing by IP-HPLC-TOF-MS and Tm ...), assay, purity, specified impurities, unspecified impurities, total degradation products, total impurities (all by IP-HPLC-UV-MS) ...') with no numeric limits, and Leqvio likewise says only that 'The proposed limits for purity ... are acceptable'. PMDA: the nusinersen review (files/000228334.pdf) gives the battery in prose — 'The proposed specifications for the drug substance consist of content, appearance, identification ..., purity (related substances [LC-MS], residual solvents ..., elemental impurities ...), bacterial endotoxins, microbial limits, and assay (LC-MS)' — and masks the impurity tables with rows of asterisks. Held patents: grep returned only boilerplate, e.g. US 8,389,488 'Analysis methods include capillary electrophoresis (CE) and electrospray-mass spectroscopy', with no values. Practical recommendation: stop pursuing purity_pct for approved products and populate purity_method, identity_confirmation and synthesis_platform from regulatory quality sections instead; chase numeric purity only for research-grade material in supplements and theses. I did not verify that any such numeric value exists even there.
- accessdata.fda.gov is BLOCKED from this container by Akamai abuse detection, not paywalled. Every probe returned HTTP 302 to /apology_objects/abuse-detection-apology.html with a 420-byte body, then 404 on the apology page. This affects all FDA Drug Approval Package Chemistry Review and Product Quality Review PDFs, which were my intended primary H3 route, and it is why the ~17 approved-oligo CMC packages are unreached. Note for whoever picks this up: the 420-byte files named 212154ChemR.pdf and vilt_chemr.pdf already sitting in the session scratchpad are these redirect stubs, not documents. This is a technical block and must not be recorded as a paywall.
- web.archive.org is unavailable as a fallback. The availability API confirmed a 200-status snapshot of the Kynamro NDA 203568 Chemistry Review at timestamp 20250326034820, but retrieval failed: the http CDX endpoint returned 'Blocked by egress policy' and the https tunnel closed mid-exchange (the proxy status endpoint recorded 'ws_closed_mid_exchange ... web.archive.org:443'). So an archived copy of that document is known to exist and is known to be unreachable from here. Worth one retry from a differently configured session.
- EMA rate-limiting left three EPARs unretrieved: Rivfloza (nedosiran), Wainua (eplontersen) and Amvuttra (vutrisiran) all returned HTTP 429 with a ~40.6 kB body. These are rate limits, not missing documents, and the four I did fetch at the same URL pattern all returned multi-megabyte PDFs. Retry with spacing. The identically sized 40,642-byte files already in the scratchpad for Leqvio, Oxlumo, Rivfloza, Amvuttra and Spinraza are these 429 pages, and ar_wainua.pdf is an HTML error page — none of them is a document.
- Sixteen of the twenty resources the review requires a coverage log for were NOT searched in this run: OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar, ResearchRabbit, Undermind, Elicit, Consensus, GEO, SRA, PRIDE, ProteomeXchange, BioStudies, ArrayExpress, Comparative Toxicogenomics Database, ICE, ToxCast, Dryad. I used PubMed/Europe PMC and ClinicalTrials.gov heavily; I touched Zenodo only through a single web search that returned no deposit. Figshare and OSF were not searched. Do not mark any of these as covered on the strength of this report.
- Non-English and Japanese regulatory grey literature barely touched. I retrieved two PMDA PDFs and found the specification tables asterisk-masked. I did not retrieve any jRCT registry record — the brogidirsen search returned jRCT URLs (jRCT2031210162, jRCT2041250028, jRCT2031200136) but I did not open them, so I am making no claim about their contents. The brogidirsen phase 1/2 bundle that STATUS.md lists as cut short by a session limit therefore remains unaddressed. EudraCT/CTIS and WHO ICTRP were not searched at all, which also leaves COG-STU006's EUDRACT2012001831-30 unverified.
- Patent searching was deliberately dropped once GSRS proved to cover the same ground more cheaply, so this report proposes no new patent despite patents being flagged as proven productive here. Specifically unexamined: the Duke/Archemix 'Modulators of coagulation factors' family (US 7,304,041, 7,723,315, 8,389,489, 8,859,518) and US 7,998,939 'Aptamers that bind thrombin with high affinity', both of which surfaced in search results and would carry sequence listings with per-position notation for the aptamer blocks; and US 5,223,609, the defibrotide process patent, which may carry characterisation data beyond what the Defitelio EPAR prints. Note that US 8,389,489 is a different document from the US 8,389,488 the repo already holds.
- Files retrieved but not inspected, so their contents are unverified: Nagano mmc2.pdf (11,658,451 B); Nagano Table S1, whose heading is present on page 13 of mmc1.pdf but whose body has no text layer and needs OCR; Soule 2022 mmc2.zip (247,621 B) and mmc3.pdf (3,611,551 B); Yu 2024 MOESM2 and MOESM3; the Oxlumo EPAR (downloaded, 3.74 MB, not read — my quality-section evidence is from Leqvio only); the Gunaratne 2017 Duke dissertation (record and handle 10161/16779 verified, content never opened). The Europe PMC supplementaryFiles call for PMC12822440 (Yu 2025, COG-S009) returned HTTP 500, so that supplement is unreached.
- Registry identities NOT resolved, listed so nobody treats lead 13 as more complete than it is: COG-STU011 ('29 mip...') and COG-STU015 ('141 pa...') match none of the seven mipomersen phase 3 enrolments I retrieved; COG-STU091, the IONIS-PKKRx phase 1 with n=49 randomised, appears absent from ClinicalTrials.gov entirely (the only PKKRx record is NCT03108469, a phase 2 with n=30 — a different study); COG-STU089, the fitusiran phase 1 with n=46/45, matches neither NCT02035605 nor NCT02554773; COG-STU032 has four plausible nusinersen phase 1 candidates and I did not disambiguate them. The four custirsen records are narrowed to candidate sets, not identified.
- Two things I checked only lightly and would not want relied on. First, the licence classification of EMA documents: the repo classes some held EPARs publisher_restricted and others public_domain, which cannot both be right, but I did not retrieve the EMA reuse policy text and so make no claim about which is correct. Second, the alias-duplicate finding that COG-OLG006 'RB007' and COG-OLG008 'anivamersen' are the same molecule rests on the GSRS record (ANIVAMERSEN, UNII 4OXU6KPQ3R) plus the repo's own naming; it is a flag for curation review, not a verified adjudication, and it is a validated-data question for German rather than something a scout should close.
- I did not attempt any purchase, subscription, credential, login or author/sponsor contact, and nothing in this report requires one. I observed no price and no purchase page anywhere in this run, so no lead is tagged PAYWALLED_VERIFIED — the three access failures above are a bot block, an egress-policy block and a rate limit, none of which is evidence of cost.

### hepatic (toxicity/hepatic, branch origin/claude/amazing-galileo-rwiv95) — 10 leads

**Gaps targeted:** Read toxicity/hepatic/BEEBOP_RESEARCH_REQUEST_2026-10-02.md and ROCKSTEADY_REVIEW_REPLY_2026-10-01.md (incl. Revision A of 2026-10-03) in full, plus RESEARCH_ACCESS_REGISTER.md and RESEARCH_SOURCE_COMPENDIUM.md at root, and ls-tree of the endpoint directory.  Measured state confirmed: 9 blobs (4 .md, 5 PDFs), zero machine-readable data, zero ingested rows. The 5 PDFs are only 3 documents (Dieckmann article = mmc2 pp.1-10; mmc1 = mmc2 pp.11-14) and only 2 independent experiments (Dieckmann Table 1 ALT and its 236-ASO statement both cite Hagedorn 2013, the PDF already held). All extractable per-oligo records are mouse in vivo.  Specific gaps my leads are aimed at: 1. HUMAN liver in-vitro with sequences = the critical path. Repo's own evidence-class table records "Human laboratory (liver injury readout): 0 ingested" and "Human 3D / MPS: 0", with finding §2.9 asserting "No peer-reviewed human 3D / microphysiological oligonucleotide hepatotoxicity study exists, on my search. The only candidate is a gated vendor poster." Leads 1, 3, 8 are aimed squarely here; lead 1 shows that §2.9 is now false. 2. PURITY / analytical characterization = 0/80 Burdick, 0/6 Dieckmann, process-level only for
