**Predicting antisense oligonucleotide-mediated toxicity using three-dimensional liver microtissue models (Zenodo code deposit + its companion article/supplement)**  
- identifier: `10.5281/zenodo.18776233 (Zenodo record 18776233)`
- gap closed: Directly falsifies the endpoint's §2.9 finding that 'No peer-reviewed human 3D / microphysiological oligonucleotide hepatotoxicity study exists' and turns the repo's 'Human 3D / MPS: 0' from a verified zero into a reachable source. Two of its compounds, LNA32 and LNA33, are character-for-character the Dieckmann compounds already held AND two of Sewing 2016's seven Table 1 SSOs, so this is a third, independent, human-in-vitro measurement on molecules whose sequence and per-position chemistry the project has already source-verified - no new sequence verification needed to use it. Mipomersen is also in lead 2, giving a cross-source link. Supplies per-compound IC50s for ALT, ATP and LDH across three species at multiple doses (dose==100 is one filter level, so a dose-response exists) = H5 raw continuous against the repo's current zero rows. Authored at NCATS, the agency running the challenge, under CC BY 4.0, which also makes it an H6-grade licence anchor against the unresolved Hagedorn 2013 licence.
- extract: Two distinct actions. (a) The six named data workbooks are NOT in the deposit - insphero_raw_data_alt_v4.xlsx, insphero_raw_data_atp_v3.xlsx, insphero_raw_data_ldh_v3.xlsx (raw, InSphero 3D human liver microtissues) and processed_alt_data.xlsx, processed_atp_data.xlsx, processed_alt/atp/ldh with the compound/species/dose/IC50 columns. The script header calls the intended archive root 'zenodo_archive/', so the authors meant to ship them; find them in the companion article's supplementary material. (b) First locate that article: there is no isSupplementTo/isPublishedIn relation in the Zenodo metadata, so resolve it via the seven ORCIDs (Jyoti Roy and Bryan Traynor are the handles), and check lead 8 - K. Sanchez and B. Filippi are authors on BOTH, so lead 8's conference abstract is the same collaboration and its full paper is very likely the host. Also pull figure_5_transcript_hc_v4_lna39highlighted.R's input, since LNA39 is highlighted there and is in the safe_asos list - an LNA-numbered compound the project does not yet hold.
- access: OPEN — Zenodo API license field returns exactly cc-by-4.0 (Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International). I downloaded four files anonymously with no barrier of any kind.
- worth: HIGH — It is the single best match to the endpoint's stated top need - human liver in-vitro data - and it arrives openly licensed, machine-readable in intent, from the challenge's own agency, on compounds the project has ALREADY sequence-verified. It also corrects a standing negative finding in the repository, which is worth something on its own. The honest discount: the deposit as published is code only, so tomorrow's work is locating the data, not parsing it. That is a 1-2 hour retrieval task with a named target, not a speculative hunt.

**Toxicity of three antisense oligonucleotide drugs and eighteen of their impurities in primary human hepatocytes (2023 FDA Science Forum poster)**  
- identifier: `https://www.fda.gov/media/168708/download (landing page: fda.gov/science-research/fda-science-forum/toxicity-three-antisense-oligonucleotide-drugs-and-eighteen-their-impurities-primary-human)`
- gap closed: This is the strongest purity-side find available and it closes the gap from an angle the project has not tried. The endpoint has 0/80 and 0/6 tested-material characterization and the project has 3 real purity values in 3,034 rows; the whole purity plan currently hangs on Stanton 2012, which the register classes as a confirmed paywall. This document does not report a purity percentage - it does something more useful for the challenge's 'purity and characterization' must: it names and individually assays the eighteen impurity species that CONSTITUTE the purity profile of three approved ASOs, in a human system. It also answers the exact question the repo cannot: whether a purity shortfall is toxicologically meaningful. Note the poster's own framing - 'Impurity concentrations can be as high as 10%' and 'It is unknown if different sequences or subclasses of impurities are associated with greater risks'. Adds up to 21 distinct molecules in a human liver system (H1): three approved drugs whose sequences are public from their FDA labels, plus 18 impurity species. Supplies continuous albumin, urea and ATP values at a stated concentration and duration (H5). Mipomersen also appears in lead 1's compound list, and all three parents are the subjects of lead 6, so this document is the hinge between the human in-vitro gap and the purity gap.
- extract: The eighteen impurity IDENTITIES, which are in the figure axis labels, not in the body text - the prose says only 'such as n-1'. Render Figures 1, 2 and 3 as images and read the category labels to get the 6 mipomersen / 5 inotersen / 7 nusinersen impurity designations, then digitise the paired albumin and urea bars (three daily timepoints each, medium collected every 24 h). Then render Figure 4 and digitise the ATP value for all 21 materials. Also lift the Materials block verbatim as provenance: XenoTech-BioIVT catalog 1810050, 10 donors 5M/5F Caucasian aged 9-63, GenScript Biotech as synthesis source, 24-well sandwich format, 56.6 / 89.0 / 11.1 uM, 3 days. Carry the n=1 caption into every row.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved anonymously from fda.gov with no barrier. Work of US federal employees (FDA/NCTR and FDA/CDER) published on a .gov site; the poster carries only a disclaimer, quoted verbatim: 'This presentation reflects the views of the authors and does not necessarily reflect those of the U.S. Food and Drug Administration. Any mention of commercial products is for clarification only and is not intended as approval, endorsement, or recommendation.' No copyright notice and no licence statement appears anywhere on it.
- worth: HIGH — Four hit types at once, human-only, public domain, and it is the only route I found that puts real analytical-identity substance against the project's worst field. The honest discounts, both of which must travel with every row: it is a conference poster, not peer-reviewed, and every figure says n=1 - single replicate, no uncertainty, so these are H5 raw values with no variance and must never be pooled with replicated data. The impurity identities also need image rendering, exactly like the Sewing Table 1 bitmap the endpoint has already dealt with successfully.

**US10955407B2 - In vitro toxicity screening assay**  
- identifier: `US10955407B2 (application US16/068,963; USPTO full-text PDF at image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/10955407)`
- gap closed: This is the hepatic sibling of the two Roche in-vitro NEPHROtoxicity patents the project already holds and has already found productive, and it is the patent form of Sewing 2016 - same five-author team (Boess, Sewing, Moisan, Roth), same BioreclamationIVT donor source, same gymnotic 3-day LDH/ATP design. It is named nowhere in the endpoint as an acquisition target. Two gaps: (a) FIG. 7 covers 34 ASOs with paired in-vitro readouts and in-vivo plasma ALT - roughly five times Sewing 2016's seven Table 1 compounds, and if any of the 34 carry human values it directly feeds the 'Human laboratory: 0 ingested' line; (b) FIG. 9 covers STEREODEFINED ASOs, which is precisely the notation gap the repo flagged - Dieckmann has zero occurrences of beta-D-oxy/oxy-LNA/alpha-L and US11105794 lists compounds 10-1 to 10-5 as five distinct molecules under one SEQ ID differing only by Rp/Sp. A stereodefined panel with per-position notation is the H2 material the endpoint cannot currently locate. Patent specifications also routinely tabulate compound design in a way journal figures do not.
- extract: From the USPTO PDF (not Google Patents - see caveat): the compound design table in the Examples that lists the 34 ASOs of FIG. 7 with their sequences and per-position chemistry, the stereodefined panel behind FIG. 9 with its Rp/Sp notation, and any Example table reporting LDH or ATP in cryopreserved HUMAN or iPSC-derived hepatocytes as opposed to mouse. Then cross-walk against Dieckmann LNA32/33/37/41/43 and Sewing SSO 32/33/35/36/37/43/47 on nucleobase sequence, recording match_basis = nucleobase_sequence per the repo's existing identity_crosswalk convention and NOT as a same-material claim.
- access: OPEN — USPTO full-text and image retrieval is free and unauthenticated; the document was returned as a direct downloadPdf URL. Granted US patent specifications are not subject to copyright.
- worth: HIGH — Patents have already proven productive on this project, this is the one patent in the family the endpoint has never pursued, and it is from the exact team whose Sewing 2016 human arm the endpoint calls its critical path. Two honest caveats a Rocksteady session must respect. First, my Google Patents read reported NO sequence listing with SEQ ID NOs in this patent - that contradicts the pattern of its kidney siblings and must be checked against the USPTO document itself before the lead is costed; if it holds, the H2 value drops sharply and only the assay data remains. Second, the repo has already established that patent OCR text layers are unreliable here and produced at least three spurious chemistry conflicts when read without rendering the page images - so render, do not grep.

**NCT02594124 (SHINE) - open-label extension for participants with SMA who previously participated in nusinersen studies: posted results, hepatobiliary adverse-event tables**  
- identifier: `NCT02594124 (ClinicalTrials.gov, results posted; enrollment 292). Contrast case: NCT02292537 (CHERISH, n=126).`
- gap closed: The project has ZERO clean clinical negatives and that single absence is blocking the lead scientist's classifier. This record has the exact shape H4 requires and - critically - it survives the trap the repo itself identified in §1.4, that 50 of ~74 candidate trials filter AE tables at a sponsor threshold so absence means 'below threshold', not 'measured and normal'. SHINE is usable precisely because it does not rely on absence: it PRINTS explicit zeros against stated denominators in six of nine arms, including 0/42 and 0/83 on both hepatic terms. That is a reported zero, not a missing row. Nusinersen is also the designated non-hepatotoxic comparator in lead 2, which is independent corroboration from a second source class. Exact sequence: nusinersen is an approved drug with a public sequence from its FDA label. Dose and duration: 12 mg intrathecal, multi-year extension. Explicit organ monitoring: the Spinraza label carries hepatic-test monitoring language, and the FDA review records Study CS3B as 2 of 55 (4%) ALT >3x ULN versus 0 of 25 controls, which bounds the parent-study context. The side-by-side with CHERISH is itself worth recording as the project's worked example of why a silent table is not a negative.
- extract: Not the paper - the registry JSON. Pull resultsSection.adverseEventsModule from the API for NCT02594124: the full eventGroups array with seriousNumAtRisk/otherNumAtRisk per arm, the two hepatic rows above with per-arm numAffected/numAtRisk, and the module's frequencyThreshold field, which must populate the mandatory non-null ae_frequency_threshold column the repo's §1.4 specifies. Then pull the same module for NCT02292537 and record it as a negative CONTROL case - present AE module, zero hepatic terms, therefore NOT usable as a negative. Add the nusinersen sequence and per-position chemistry from the FDA label, and cite lead 6's 209531Orig1s000MedR.pdf for the CS3B 2/55 vs 0/25 ALT figure so the extension is anchored to its parent without summing participants.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved anonymously from the ClinicalTrials.gov public API v2. ClinicalTrials.gov content is US government work in the public domain.
- worth: HIGH — Highest-value find available per the task's own framing, and it is verified down to the individual numerator/denominator cell rather than inferred. It is also cheap - an API call, no PDF, no rendering, no access question. The honest caveats: a 9-arm open-label extension needs the repo's stated extension-counting convention applied so SHINE's participants are never added to the parent trials' totals, and 3 hepatic steatosis plus 5 GGT events do occur in the drug-exposed arms, so this is a clean negative for specific arms at a stated threshold, not a blanket 'nusinersen is hepatically safe' claim.

**Tegsedi (inotersen) EPAR - Public assessment report, CHMP**  
- identifier: `EMA/411876/2018; 142 pages; ema.europa.eu/en/documents/assessment-report/tegsedi-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf`
- gap closed: Supplies the analytical identity and characterization METHOD set for a named approved ASO, which the endpoint has for nothing: the full CQA panel, the identification methods (accurate-mass MS plus MS-MS sequencing), the counter-ion method (ICP-OES), and the single method that measures full-length product content, purity and product-related impurities (IP-HPLC-UV-MS). The repo records Hagedorn's process-level IEX-HPLC / UPLC >85% / LC-MS as 'more than any other hepatic source provides' - this is a complete, regulator-reviewed version of that, for a molecule with a public sequence. It also documents the impurity-GROUPING convention for synthetic ASOs, which is exactly the vocabulary the project needs to design its proposed purity_status enum, and it states that impurity limits were qualified by toxicological and clinical studies - the regulatory counterpart to lead 2's experiment. Second gap: the hepatic TEAE percentages give human liver outcomes with a traceable CS2 denominator of 112, and the ALT-specific comparison is 2.7% inotersen versus 3.3% placebo - lower on drug - which is H4-shaped for the ALT endpoint specifically. Third, as an EMA public document it is an H6-grade openly reusable anchor against the unresolved Hagedorn 2013 licence.
- extract: Section 2.2 'Quality aspects' - the active substance specification paragraph (the CQA list and methods quoted above), the impurity-grouping paragraph, and the drug-product specification paragraph naming inotersen content and product-related impurities by IP-HPLC-UV-MS. Then section 2.6 clinical safety for the hepatic TEAE table and the CS2/CS3 denominators. Record the methods as analytical identity with purity_status = method_reported_only, NOT as numeric purity.
- access: OPEN — Downloaded anonymously from ema.europa.eu, HTTP 200, no barrier, no login, no price. EMA publishes EPARs as public assessment reports for free reuse with acknowledgement.
- worth: MEDIUM — Promoted to the top five because it is fully verified, openly licensed, text-layered and attacks the project's worst field from a source class it has never used. Demoted from HIGH by one specific thing I checked and must report straight: I searched the extracted text for numeric purity acceptance criteria and found NONE. The EPAR names every attribute and every method and says the limits were 'tightened' and 'qualified', but it does not print the numbers. So this is method-level H3, not numeric H3. It does not replace Stanton 2012 for the 80 Burdick compounds - different molecule, different sponsor - and must not be recorded as if it did. Sister EPARs worth the same treatment once this one is proven: Waylivra (volanesorsen) and the Kynamro CHMP refusal/withdrawal assessment, the latter being hepatotoxicity-specific regulatory adjudication on the drug in lead 2.

**FDA Drug Approval Package Chemistry Reviews for the three ASOs in lead 2: mipomersen (Kynamro), nusinersen (Spinraza), inotersen (Tegsedi)**  
- identifier: `NDA 203568Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (mipomersen, 6,623,728 bytes); NDA 209531Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (nusinersen, 3,156,001 bytes); NDA 211172Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (inotersen, 763,139 bytes) - all under accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/{2013,2016,2018}/`
- gap closed: These are the CMC files for exactly the three molecules lead 2 assays, so the pair is tight: lead 2 gives the impurity toxicology in human hepatocytes, these give the regulator's view of the impurity and purity specification for the same three drugs. Together they are the project's best shot at putting something real behind the challenge's p.6 'must' on purity and characterization without waiting on the Stanton 2012 access request.
- extract: Priority order reflecting what I verified. (1) 209531Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (nusinersen, 3.2 MB) - UNCHECKED by me, and the only one of the three not yet known to be redacted or image-only; check it first. (2) 203568Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (mipomersen) - 49 pages but only 9,667 characters of extractable text, i.e. a scanned image PDF with no usable text layer; it needs page rendering or OCR, and mipomersen is the most hepatically relevant of the three (boxed warning, EMA refusal twice). (3) 211172Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (inotersen) - do NOT spend time on it for purity; see caveat. Separately, 203568Orig1s000MedR.pdf (3.2 MB) and 209531orig1s000medr.pdf (13.9 MB, lowercase filename) both return 200 and carry the hepatic clinical safety reviews, including the CS3B 2 of 55 (4%) ALT >3x ULN versus 0 of 25 control figure that anchors lead 4.
- access: OPEN — All files return HTTP 200 as application/pdf from accessdata.fda.gov with no login and no price. US government work, public domain. OPERATIONAL NOTE worth recording in the access register: accessdata.fda.gov returns a 420-byte HTTP 404 for every one of these URLs when the request carries no browser User-Agent, and HTTP 200 with the real PDF when it does. My first probe of eleven URLs returned 404 for all eleven and was a false negative. Any earlier session that logged these as missing or blocked should recheck before that finding is cited - this is exactly the 'technical access block misread as absence' failure the register warns about.
- worth: MEDIUM — Honest downgrade based on first-hand checking rather than assumption, and the negative result is itself the useful part. I counted 56 instances of the FOIA (b)(4) trade-secret redaction in the inotersen ChemR and found ZERO numeric purity values in it - the impurity identities, the endotoxin limit ('Not greater than (b)(4) EU/mL') and the retest period are all redacted. So the natural assumption that 'FDA CMC equals purity numbers' is false for at least one of these three, and reporting this saves a Rocksteady session a day of dead-end work. The lead stays worth acting on because the nusinersen file is untested and the mipomersen file is six times larger than the one I disproved, but it should be costed as a 30-minute triage of two files, not as a solved purity source. Record the outcome either way: a verified 'redacted under (b)(4)' is a legitimate, citable reason for a purity gap and is far better than a bare NOT_REPORTED.

**In Vitro Assessment of the Hepatotoxicity Potential of Therapeutic Oligonucleotides**  
- identifier: `10.1007/978-1-4939-9670-4_15; Methods in Molecular Biology, 2019, pp. 249-259`
- gap closed: The endpoint's single named unread item is Sewing 2016 Figure 8A - the per-compound HUMAN hepatocyte LDH and ATP values and their concentrations - and Revision A is explicit that no human concentration range can be asserted because the published '1-30 uM' belongs to the MOUSE arm while the human arm is described only as 'required higher concentrations'. This is the protocol chapter by the same first and last author, published three years later, and it is the document class that states the human assay parameters the paper compresses: donor sourcing and lot handling, plating format, gymnotic delivery conditions, the human concentration range, and the LDH/ATP acceptance and normalisation criteria. Without it, Figure 8A can be digitised but its x-axis cannot be safely interpreted or unit-normalised. It also supports lead 3, being the published protocol behind the same patent family.
- extract: The Materials and the Notes sections specifically: human hepatocyte source and lot handling, plating format and density, gymnotic delivery conditions, the concentration range used for the HUMAN arm, treatment duration, and the LDH and ATP measurement and normalisation protocol. Use it to annotate, not replace, Sewing 2016 Figure 8A once that figure is rendered.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — I did not retrieve the chapter and did not see a price, a purchase page or a subscription notice. The only access signal I have is the Crossref licence field, which returns http://www.springer.com/tdm - a text-and-data-mining licence URL. That is NOT evidence of a paywall and must not be recorded as one. Routes not yet tried: Europe PMC, PubMed for a PMCID, author or institutional repository, and the publisher's own stated access condition.
- worth: MEDIUM — Narrow but precisely aimed: it unblocks the one item the endpoint has explicitly flagged as unread and as the reason its human-laboratory evidence is still qualitative. Low page count, cheap to read if reachable. Honest limits: it is a protocol chapter, so it will carry no new per-oligo measurements and no new molecules - zero contribution to molecule count - and its access condition is genuinely unknown rather than open. Check Europe PMC before escalating it to Oscar as a document request.

**Toxicity of Antisense Oligonucleotides is Determined by the Synergistic Interplay of Chemical Modifications and Nucleotide Sequences, Not by Either Factor Alone**  
- identifier: `10.26434/chemrxiv-2025-d2bhr (ChemRxiv item 688d3c2323be8e43d657dcc9), posted 2025-08-05`
- gap closed: Aimed at the endpoint's H2 gap, which ranks highest alongside H3. The repo has established that it cannot encode chemistry reliably - four incompatible 5-methylcytosine notations across its four core documents, letter-case encoding round-tripping none of them, Dieckmann's sugar stereochemistry unstated, and Burdick's own prose rule failing to reproduce 5 of its own 80 compounds. A study whose entire thesis is 'the specific position context of those modifications' is, by construction, a source that must report per-position chemistry for matched sequence/chemistry pairs - which is the shape H2 needs. It also covers hepatic and renal in one design, so it would serve two endpoints.
- extract: The compound design table - the matched sugar/backbone variant series with per-position modification notation and the sequence each variant is built on - plus whichever table carries the hepatotoxicity readout against those designs. Resolve the 403 first by trying the ChemRxiv public API, the item landing page from a different route, or a published journal version (posted 2025-08, so one may now exist).
- access: OPEN — Crossref returns the licence as exactly https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/ - CC BY-NC-ND 4.0. Note this is the most restrictive licence on my list: NonCommercial and NoDerivatives. Record that before any extracted table is staged, because an openly licensed AI-ready dataset is the Phase 2 deliverable and ND is a real constraint on redistributing a derived table. Separately: both the ChemRxiv article page and the direct PDF asset URL returned HTTP 403 to this environment. A 403 is a technical access block, NOT a paywall, and must be classed as such - the item is openly licensed per its own metadata.
- worth: MEDIUM — Strong thesis match to the endpoint's highest-ranked gap and openly licensed, but three honest discounts keep it out of the top tier. The abstract names no human system, so it probably contributes animal support rather than the human data this endpoint needs above all else. It is a preprint, not peer-reviewed. And its CC BY-NC-ND licence is a genuine redistribution constraint for a project whose deliverable must be openly licensed. I could not read the body text from here, so the compound count and the species are both unverified - treat the H2 payload as expected, not established.

**P18-33 Initial evaluation of the utility of human liver microtissues for prediction of clinical liver injury caused by antisense oligonucleotides**  
- identifier: `10.1016/j.toxlet.2025.07.486; Toxicology Letters, volume 411, page S203, September 2025`
- gap closed: Second independent human 3D/MPS source against the repo's 'Human 3D / MPS: 0', and the one that identifies what the repo dismissed: §2.9 calls the only 3D candidate 'a gated vendor poster', but this work now has a Crossref DOI, a journal, a volume and a page. Fourteen ASOs in a human liver system is double Sewing 2016's seven. Its real strategic value is as the bridge to lead 1: K. Sanchez and B. Filippi are authors on BOTH this abstract and the NCATS Zenodo deposit, so this is the InSphero side of the same collaboration and the fastest route to identifying lead 1's host article and therefore lead 1's missing data workbooks.
- extract: The abstract body on page S203: the identities of the fourteen ASOs, the microtissue model and donor basis, the concentration range, and the caspase 3/7 and cytotoxicity endpoints. Use it primarily to resolve the author chain to lead 1's full article. Do not expect per-compound numeric tables from a one-page conference abstract.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — I did not retrieve it. Crossref shows only Elsevier TDM licence URLs, which are not a price. A WebFetch of the ScienceDirect landing page returned HTTP 403 to this environment - a technical block, not an observed paywall, and it must not be recorded as one. No price, purchase page or subscription notice was seen.
- worth: MEDIUM — Cheap, verified down to volume and page, corrects a standing negative finding, and is the most direct lever on lead 1 - which is the highest-value item on this list. Discounted because it is a single conference-abstract page in a supplement: it will carry compound names and a qualitative result, almost certainly not the fourteen-compound numeric table, and probably no sequences at all. Treat it as a pointer and a citation, not as a data source.

**ASOptimizer: Optimizing Chemical Diversity of Antisense Oligonucleotides through Deep Learning - supporting material**  
- identifier: `10.6084/m9.figshare.28829144 (figshare, 2025); related article PMC12230697`
- gap closed: Marginal for this endpoint and reported for completeness rather than recommended. Its only real relevance is as a possible large pre-curated chemistry corpus: the associated article is described as having curated roughly 6,000 publicly available rodent experiments with plans to extend to ALT and AST hepatotoxicity prediction. If that corpus is released with per-position chemistry encodings, it would be an H2 resource at a scale nothing else here offers, and relevant to the project's open question on adopting HELM as a cross-endpoint chemistry standard.
- extract: Check whether the deposit or PMC12230697 actually contains the curated experiment table with per-oligo sequences and position-level chemistry, as opposed to only model code and a web-server pointer. The DataCite description suggests code and a server link, so verify before spending time. If the corpus is absent, close this lead.
- access: OPEN — DataCite rightsIdentifier returns 'mit' - MIT licence. Metadata retrieved anonymously with no barrier.
- worth: LOW — Rodent, not human, so it contributes nothing to this endpoint's stated top priority. The hepatotoxicity ALT/AST extension is described as planned rather than delivered. The deposit's own description points to code and a web server, not a dataset. Listed so the ranking is honest about what I looked at and so nobody re-finds it and over-rates it; do not spend a Rocksteady day on it while leads 1 through 4 are open.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- The companion ARTICLE behind Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.18776233 - my single biggest miss, and it is what makes lead 1 actionable. The deposit carries no isSupplementTo or isPublishedIn relation and my bioRxiv and title searches did not surface it. The data workbooks named inside the R scripts (insphero_raw_data_alt_v4.xlsx, _atp_v3.xlsx, _ldh_v3.xlsx and the three processed_*.xlsx) are NOT in the deposit and I never obtained them. I verified that the human/mouse/rat IC50 tables exist as code inputs; I did NOT see a single numeric value from them. Fastest untried route: the shared authors K. Sanchez and B. Filippi link lead 1 to lead 8, so resolve lead 8's full paper first.
- Numeric purity acceptance criteria for ANY oligonucleotide. I checked three routes and all three came up short in ways I can document: the inotersen FDA ChemR is redacted (56 x (b)(4), zero numeric limits), the mipomersen FDA ChemR has no text layer, and the Tegsedi EPAR names every attribute and method but prints no numbers. The nusinersen ChemR (3,156,001 bytes) is the one file in that set I did not open. So the project's purity 'must' remains unmet by anything I verified - I moved the gap from unexamined to characterised, not from open to closed.
- Stanton 2012 (10.1089/nat.2012.0366), the register's A1 and the stated only route to purity for all 80 Burdick compounds. I did not attempt it at all and have no new information on its access condition. I also did not find an openly licensed replacement for it (H6), which remains an open gap.
- The eighteen impurity IDENTITIES in the FDA poster. The prose says only 'such as n-1'; the identities are in the axis labels of Figures 1-3, which are images. I read the poster's full text layer but did not render the figures, so I verified that 6+5+7 impurities exist per drug and did not verify what any single one of them is. No numeric albumin, urea or ATP value was extracted either.
- Whether US10955407B2 actually contains a sequence listing. My Google Patents read reported no SEQ ID NOs, which contradicts the pattern of the two kidney patents already held. I did not open the USPTO document to resolve this, and it materially changes the lead's H2 value. The repo has already shown that patent OCR text layers here are unreliable, so this needs page rendering, not grep.
- Full text of lead 8 (Toxicology Letters, 403 from ScienceDirect) and lead 9 (ChemRxiv, 403 on both the landing page and the direct PDF asset). Both are technical blocks and I have recorded them as such; neither is evidence of a paywall and neither should ever be logged as one. Consequences: the identities of lead 8's fourteen ASOs are unverified, and lead 9's species and compound count are unverified.
- Sewing 2016 Figure 8A - still unread, exactly as the repository already records. I did not retrieve it. I only identified the two documents (leads 3 and 7) that would let its x-axis be interpreted, and lead 7's own access condition is unknown.
- Thirteen of the twenty mandated resources in the endpoint's search-coverage log. I used PubMed/Europe PMC only indirectly through general web search, and did NOT search OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar, ResearchRabbit, Undermind, Elicit, Consensus, GEO, SRA, PRIDE, ProteomeXchange, BioStudies, ArrayExpress, CTD, ICE, ToxCast or Dryad. GEO and ArrayExpress are the notable omission given lead 1's figure_S4_transcriptomics and figure_5_transcript scripts imply a transcriptomics deposit I never looked for. This report is not a substitute for that twenty-row log.
- Non-English and Japanese regulatory grey literature - PMDA review reports were not searched at all, despite being named as under-searched. Nor were pharmacopoeial monographs (USP/Ph. Eur. oligonucleotide monographs), doctoral theses, EudraCT, WHO ICTRP or jRCT.
- Sister EPARs I recommend but did not open: Waylivra (volanesorsen) and the Kynamro CHMP refusal / withdrawal assessment report. The latter is hepatotoxicity-specific regulatory adjudication on mipomersen, the most hepatically relevant molecule in this whole set, and I only inferred its existence from the FDA poster's statement that mipomersen was 'rejected twice by EMA due to hepatotoxicity'. I did not verify that any such document is published, so treat it as an unverified lead.
- A systematic H4 sweep. I verified one clean clinical negative (lead 4) and one instructive negative control (CHERISH), both nusinersen. I did not screen the 224 ClinicalTrials.gov JSON files on claude/hydrocephalus-toxicity-oligos-t172zv that the repo reports as holding 88 records with liver AE terms and arm-level denominators - that in-repository pool is probably a richer H4 source than anything I searched for externally, and it needs the re-screen the repo's own section 3 already calls for.
- Burel et al. 2016 (NAR, PMC4797265), 'Hepatotoxicity of high affinity gapmer antisense oligonucleotides is mediated by RNase H1 dependent promiscuous reduction of very long pre-mRNA transcripts'. Surfaced twice in my searches, not held in the repository, and I did not evaluate it - it is an Ionis source likely carrying a sequence table, but I could not establish whether it has any human arm, so I have not listed it as a lead rather than guess.

**Rytelo (imetelstat) EMA European public assessment report - active-substance structure/stereochemistry, active-substance and finished-product specification panels, and the normal-human-serum complement screening study 004-TOX-2007**  
- identifier: `EMA/13310/2025; EU marketing authorisation 07 March 2025; CHMP opinion 12 December 2024; retrieved as https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/documents/assessment-report/rytelo-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf`
- gap closed: Closes, for one compound, three of the gaps at once. (a) H2: the location of every modification for a 13-mer - 12 internucleoside N3'->P5'-thio-phosphoramidate linkages plus one 5'-terminal phosphorothioate carrying the palmitoyl-aminoglycerol - against a recorded 0 of 12 position-specific chemistry for human clinical compounds. (b) H3: the complete analytical identity and purity test panel with named methods, for both active substance and finished product, in a project holding 3 real characterization values in 3,034 oligo rows; it also names 'unlipidated species' as the product-related impurity to track for a lipid-conjugated oligo. (c) H1: a second HUMAN-matrix complement dataset for imetelstat with a stable study identifier (004-TOX-2007), where the repository's only human imetelstat complement evidence is AACR conference abstract 7276, explicitly held as 'a lead, not evidence'. The EPAR also independently corroborates that abstract's direction ('activated the alternate complement pathway'), upgrading a grey-literature lead to a regulator-adjudicated statement. 'Rytelo' returns 0 files on the branch.
- extract: Four specific passages, not the whole PDF: (1) section 2.4.2.1 'General information' p.14 - Figure 1 molecular structure, molecular formula, the chemical name, and the stereochemistry paragraph giving the 12 thio-phosphoramidate linkages + 1 terminal phosphorothioate (H2 row); (2) section 2.4.2.3 'Specification, analytical procedures, reference standards, batch analysis' p.16 - the active-substance test panel verbatim, plus the 15-batch compliance statement (H3 row); (3) section 2.4.3.3 'Product specification' - the finished-product test panel, plus the stability paragraph naming purity and unlipidated species OOS results; (4) section 2.5.4.8 'Other toxicity studies / Immunotoxicity' p.35 - the complete 004-TOX-2007 paragraph, keyed as human serum, analytes haemolytic activity / C5a-dependent chemotaxis / Bb, concentration 150 ug/mL, with the comparator labels '163', '833', '878', '163L' carried verbatim and NOT resolved to compounds (the EPAR never defines them). Record pathway_label_source = 'alternate complement pathway' per the source's own wording.
- access: OPEN — Freely downloadable with no login and no payment: HTTP 200, 2,964,174 bytes, first attempt, no rate-limit retry needed. Licence: EMA public document; no Creative Commons grant is stated on the document or the product page. This matches the folder policy already in research-staging/README.md ('EMA assessment reports | public documents, no CC grant | not committed (bulk; freely re-fetchable) | record only'), so the file stays out and only derived numerical values enter the dataset, with attribution.
- worth: HIGH — It is the only lead that serves the two top-ranked hit types (H3 and H2) and the molecule-count hit type (H1) in one free document, and it does so for precisely the compound on which the endpoint's single positive human signal rests. It also converts the endpoint's weakest-provenance item (a conference abstract read through an inverted index) into something corroborated by a regulator. Cost is one download the session can repeat in under a minute.

**NCATS GSRS / Inxight Drugs public substance API - per-residue sugar-modification site lists and per-linkage backbone site lists for eight complement-endpoint oligonucleotides**  
- identifier: `UNIIs retrieved first-hand: DRISAPERSEN 73D586DAMF; INOTERSEN 0IEO0F56LV; MIPOMERSEN 9GJ8S4GU0M; VOLANESORSEN 2O4BE0K238; EPLONTERSEN 0GRZ0F5XJ6; ALICAFORSEN J8435V445B; CEMDISIRAN S66Z65E10T; BEPIROVIRSEN XCC829VFT7. Endpoint: https://drugs.ncats.io/api/v1/substances(<UNII>)?view=full`
- gap closed: Directly closes the endpoint's single mandatory-field hole: 'Position-specific chemistry: 0 of 12' human clinical compounds. Eight of the endpoint's named compounds get residue-indexed sugar chemistry and linkage-indexed backbone chemistry in machine-readable form, from the agency that runs this challenge. Eplontersen is the clearest demonstration of what no paper in the endpoint supplies: linkages resolve as 6 phosphodiester plus 13 phosphorothioate, i.e. a mixed backbone located per linkage - exactly the distinction the endpoint's whole scientific question (PS versus PO backbone activation) turns on. Alicaforsen also gains full position chemistry, and it is the one compound the repository records as having an exact sequence (1 of 12). Bepirovirsen's 19 'nasP' linkages are a non-standard code that must be resolved against the GSRS codebook, not guessed.
- extract: Per UNII, the single JSON object at $.nucleicAcid - specifically the subunits[].sequence string, the sugars[] array (each {sugar, sites[{subunitIndex, residueIndex}]}) and the linkages[] array (each {linkage, sites[...]}). Expand the site lists into one row per residue and per linkage; do not store only the counts. Also store the UNII as the join key and record substanceClass, so the two verified non-nucleic-acid registrations (imetelstat as 'chemical', pegnivacogin as 'polymer') are recorded as explicit absences rather than as missing data. Before use, resolve the sugar codes (mR, MOE, qR, dR, fR) and linkage codes (P, sP, nasP) against the GSRS controlled vocabulary rather than inferring them - 'qR' on the mipomersen record in particular should not be assumed to mean 2'-MOE without checking.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200 on every call, no login, no key, no rate limiting encountered, JSON returned directly. Work of the US Government (NIH/NCATS). I did not retrieve a separate explicit licence statement from the service, so record the terms as 'US Government work, licence statement not separately retrieved' rather than asserting CC0.
- worth: HIGH — Highest-ranked hit type (H2), eight molecules at once, free, machine-readable, no extraction judgement required, and sourced from the challenge's own sponsor - which makes it about as defensible a provenance as this project can get for the mandatory modification-location field. It is also the cheapest item on this list: a loop over eight UNIIs.

**EU Clinical Trials Register posted results for EudraCT 2007-004819-54 (sponsor protocol PRO051-02, drisapersen phase I/II, Prosensa) - adverse-event tables carrying per-arm complement C3 counts with denominators**  
- identifier: `EudraCT 2007-004819-54; Sponsor Protocol Number PRO051-02; sponsor Prosensa Therapeutics BV; results page https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/trial/2007-004819-54/results`
- gap closed: (a) H5: gives numeric per-arm counts for a complement outcome where the repository currently holds only EPAR free text - the Kyndrisa assessment report states that complement split products were 'measured in two phase I/II studies (DMD114118 and PRO051-02)' with 'no strong evidence', and no measurement row exists for either. Here PRO051-02 yields 3/12 (25.00%) complement factor C3 decreased, 1/12 (8.33%) increased, and explicit 0/3 zeros in four dose arms - against zero numeric human complement values anywhere in the project. (b) H4 in part: exact sequence obtainable (drisapersen UCAAGGAAGAUGGCAUUUCU, GSRS UNII 73D586DAMF, Lead 2), human exposure with stated dose and duration (0.5-6 mg/kg/week for five weeks, plus timed IV infusions), explicit platelet monitoring in the same AE table, explicit outcome, and traceable denominators per arm - the four lower-dose arms are a denominator-bearing negative. The repository reports ZERO clean clinical negatives project-wide and the lead scientist blocking a classifier on that absence. (c) It also supplies the registry identifier for a study the repository holds only as a sponsor protocol code.
- extract: From the results page, the 'Adverse events' section only: the two rows 'Complement factor C3 decreased' and 'Complement factor C3 increased' with every per-arm 'subjects affected / exposed' cell AND the 'occurrences all number' cells, keyed to the arm titles from the 'Subject disposition' / arm-definition block above them; plus the 'Thrombocytopenia' and 'Platelet count increased' rows from the same table so the platelet-monitoring condition of H4 is evidenced from the same denominator set. Carry two cautions onto every row: these are MedDRA adverse-event terms, not analyte measurements - 'Complement factor C3 decreased' is component abundance, which the endpoint's own schema keeps separate from activation fragments; and the 12-subject and 7-subject arms are the continued-treatment and IV sub-study populations, so they overlap the dose-escalation arms and must not be summed.
- access: OPEN — Public register, no login: HTTP 200, 1,172,937 bytes. Published by EMA on behalf of the sponsor under the EU clinical-trial transparency rules; no Creative Commons grant is stated, so treat like the EMA assessment reports already in the folder - extract values, do not commit the page.
- worth: HIGH — It is the only item I verified that puts actual numbers, with denominators and an arm structure, against a human complement outcome - the endpoint's defining absence. It is free, it is registry-identified, and it simultaneously repairs the sponsor-code-to-registry link that the last round named as blocking any defensible trial count.

**EU Clinical Trials Register sponsor-protocol-to-EudraCT mapping and posted-results index for the regulatory-only ASO studies (drisapersen, mipomersen, volanesorsen, inotersen/eplontersen programmes)**  
- identifier: `Eleven mappings retrieved first-hand. Drisapersen: BMN-051-302 = 2015-001955-54 (results), BMN-051-303 = 2014-005296-81 (results), PRO051-02 = 2007-004819-54 (results). Mipomersen: ISIS 301012-CS6 = 2005-003450-10 (results), MIPO3500108 = 2008-006020-53 (results), MIPO3801011 = 2011-001480-42 (results). Volanesorsen: ISIS304801-CS7 = 2015-003755-21 (results), ISIS-304801-CS17 = 2015-000493-35 (results), ISIS304801-CS20 = 2020-002997-28 (no results). Inotersen/eplontersen: ISIS420915-CS4 = 2017-005115-13 (no results), ION-682884-CS3 = 2019-001698-10 (results), ION-682884-CS13 = 2021-001427-40 (no results). Search endpoint https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/search?query=<drug>`
- gap closed: Closes the dependency Rocksteady flagged as blocking: 'cross-linking them to a registry is a separate, unperformed mapping step, and until it is done no regulatory study may be reconciled against a registry-derived count' (§3 row 4, §7.3 item 11). Three of the eleven codes mapped here are exactly the codes the regulatory register names as carrying complement statements - PRO051-02 (drisapersen split products), ISIS 301012-CS6 (mipomersen OLE), ISIS304801-CS7 (volanesorsen). It also supplies H4-shaped material the project has none of: adverse-event tables with explicit platelet-count and thrombocytopenia rows and per-arm 'subjects affected / exposed' denominators, for five studies with posted results. And it gives four validated absences - complement terms are not in the mipomersen CS6, eplontersen CS3, volanesorsen CS7 or CS17 results sections - so a later session cannot score them as positive by assumption.
- extract: Two passes. Pass 1, cheap and immediate: for each of the eleven rows, store sponsor_protocol_code, eudract_number, sponsor_name, results_flag - this is the crosswalk table, and it should be written beside REGISTER_regulatory_draft.md as the registry-identifier column that file currently cannot fill. Pass 2, per results page that is flagged 'View results': the 'Adverse events' section tables, taking every row whose MedDRA term matches complement, platelet or haemolysis, with its per-arm 'subjects affected / exposed' and 'occurrences' cells and the arm titles; plus the 'Subject disposition' block for the enrolment denominators. Record the parent/extension relationship explicitly - ION-682884-CS3 and CS13 are a parent and an extension and must never be summed, the same convention the Tegsedi EPAR forces for CS2/CS3.
- access: OPEN — Public register, no login, HTTP 200 on all search and results pages, sizes as listed. No Creative Commons grant stated; treat as EMA public material - derive values, do not redistribute pages.
- worth: HIGH — It removes a stated blocker rather than adding a document, and it is the only route I found to denominator-bearing human safety tables for the eleven studies that exist in the repository with no registry identifier at all. The EU Clinical Trials Register is not among the twenty compendium resources and was never searched, so this is a whole unexamined resource class, not a single paper.

**EU Clinical Trials Register posted results for EudraCT 2015-001955-54 (sponsor protocol BMN-051-302, drisapersen open-label extension, BioMarin) - complement C3 adverse-event counts under a subcutaneous versus intravenous route contrast**  
- identifier: `EudraCT 2015-001955-54; Sponsor Protocol Number BMN-051-302; sponsor BioMarin Nederland BV; results page https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/trial/2015-001955-54/results`
- gap closed: A second, independent registry record giving numeric human complement counts with denominators for drisapersen - 1/8 (12.50%) versus 0/11 (0.00%) - where the repository holds zero numeric human complement values. Its particular value is the arm structure: 6 mg/kg/wk subcutaneous against 3 mg/kg/wk intravenous, i.e. a route contrast inside one study. The endpoint has already identified route and infusion rate as a live variable (ARC-520 cut its infusion rate from 3.5 to 2 mL/min 'to lessen risk of infusion reaction' with complement among the tracked readouts), and this is a human complement observation split by route, which nothing in the repository currently provides. Also extends the BMN-051-302 to EudraCT crosswalk.
- extract: The single adverse-event row 'Complement factor C3 decreased' with both per-arm 'subjects affected / exposed' cells and both 'occurrences' cells, keyed to the arm titles '6 mg/kg/wk SC' and '3 mg/kg/wk IV'; plus the subject-disposition enrolment figures for the two arms. Carry the caveats: n=8 and n=11 are very small, the record is an open-label extension so these patients overlap earlier drisapersen studies and must not be added to them, and the term is a MedDRA AE for component abundance rather than an activation-fragment measurement. Note for the record that this page carries no 'Thrombocytopenia' or platelet AE row, which is itself worth storing as an absence rather than inferred as a negative.
- access: OPEN — Public register, no login, HTTP 200, 145,257 bytes. Same terms as the other EU CTR pages: EMA public material, no CC grant stated.
- worth: MEDIUM — Smaller and thinner than 2007-004819-54, and the page is short enough that it may be a partial results posting - but it is a second independent numeric human complement record for the same molecule, it carries a route contrast nothing else in the endpoint has, and it costs one page fetch.

**Crystal structure of the mixed L-RNA/L-DNA Spiegelmer NOX-D20 bound to complement C5a - the openly licensed sequence and positional L-ribonucleotide/L-deoxyribonucleotide chemistry for the AON-D21 aptamer lineage**  
- identifier: `doi 10.1038/ncomms7481; PMID 25901944; PMCID PMC4423239; Nature Communications 2015;6:6481`
- gap closed: The repository holds three AON-D21 human trials (NCT05018403 single ascending dose, NCT05343819 multiple ascending dose, NCT05962606 Phase 2 in 150 ICU patients) and the Immunobiology 2025 programme review, and records for all of them that the sequence is absent and the analytes UNVERIFIED. This supplies, under CC BY 4.0, an explicit 44-nt sequence for the lineage parent plus the positional L-ribo-to-L-deoxy exchange scheme that defines the mixed-chemistry variant and the 5'-PEG attachment point - that is H1 identity and H2 modification locations for an aptamer-class molecule the project currently has by name only. H6 as well: the endpoint's redistribution problem is that Mangsbo 2009 has no CC licence, Crooke is NC-SA and Demirjian is ND; this is a CC BY file that can actually be committed.
- extract: From the Methods section: the NOX-D19 44-nt sequence string, and the sentences and table/figure specifying which residues were exchanged from L-ribonucleotide to L-deoxyribonucleotide to give NOX-D20, plus the 40-kDa Y-shaped methoxy-PEG / aminohexyl-linker 5' attachment description. Also take the PDB accession(s) deposited with the paper if the extracting session wants the per-residue chemistry independently. CRITICAL caveat to attach to every row: the repository's held Immunobiology 2025 review states the lineage as NOX-D19 -> NOX-D20 -> NOX-D21/AON-D21, but I did NOT independently verify that AON-D21 is chemically identical to NOX-D21, and this paper is about D19 and D20. Do not key the clinical molecule to this sequence until that identity is verified from a source that states it; record it as lineage evidence, not as the administered sequence.
- access: OPEN — Europe PMC record reports isOpenAccess 'Y', license 'cc by'; full text XML retrieved HTTP 200, 155,160 bytes; the article's own permissions block states 'Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International License'. Note the standard carve-out in that same block: third-party material in figures may carry different terms.
- worth: MEDIUM — Real H1/H2/H6 content, retrieved and quotable, and CC BY so it can be committed rather than held as a provenance record. Ranked below the first four because the aptamer's target IS complement, so the programme is efficacy and the repository has correctly partitioned it out of any toxicity tally - this lead raises molecular coverage and redistributable-source coverage, not the human toxicity observation count.

**FDA Product Quality Review (chemistry) for Tegsedi/inotersen, with the parallel reviews for Kynamro, Rytelo and Wainua confirmed to exist**  
- identifier: `NDA 211172Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (inotersen). Also verified to exist by range request: 203568Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (mipomersen, 2013), 217779Orig1s000ChemR.pdf and 217779Orig1s000MultidisciplineR.pdf (imetelstat, 2024), 217388Orig1s000ChemR.pdf plus IntegratedR and OtherR (eplontersen, 2024). Review-package table of contents for Rytelo confirmed via the openFDA drugsfda API as 217779Orig1s000TOC.html.`
- gap closed: Partial H3 against 'tested-material characterization 0 of 12' for the human clinical compounds: supplies analytical identity and formulation facts for inotersen (phosphorothioate backbone confirmed by the regulator, salt-to-free-acid conversion 284 mg = 300 mg sodium salt, excipients, pH range 7.5-8.5, presence of an endotoxin specification), and confirms that chemistry reviews exist for three further compounds in the endpoint's compound list. It must be reported with its limit, which is itself a finding worth carrying: the numeric impurity and purity acceptance criteria are redacted as '(b) (4)' throughout, so an FDA chemistry review will NOT yield purity numbers for an oligonucleotide. The EMA assessment report route (Lead 1) gives the test panel and methods instead, and for numeric limits neither regulator publishes them. A session that budgets time expecting purity figures from FDA reviews will spend it for nothing.
- extract: From 211172Orig1s000ChemR.pdf: the 'Quality Assessment Overview' section - the backbone statement, the drug-substance description, the 284 mg / 300 mg equivalence, the excipient list and pH range, and the specification-adequacy sentence; record every redacted limit explicitly as '(b)(4) redacted' rather than as missing. Then, for the three sibling packages, pull the equivalent overview section only: 217779Orig1s000ChemR.pdf for imetelstat (highest priority, the compound with the human signal, and worth comparing against the EMA panel in Lead 1), 203568Orig1s000ChemR.pdf for mipomersen, 217388Orig1s000ChemR.pdf for eplontersen. Use a browser user agent on every fetch.
- access: OPEN — Free, no login, no payment; US Government work, public domain. HTTP 200 and 763,139 bytes for the downloaded review; HTTP 206 on range requests for the other four confirmed URLs. The two 404s were specific document types that do not exist in those packages, not an access barrier.
- worth: MEDIUM — Genuine H3 identity content and a confirmed route for three more compounds, but demoted from HIGH because I verified that the numbers the project most needs are redacted. Its second value is negative and practical: it tells the team where not to look for oligonucleotide purity figures, which protects the next session's time.

**Sara Mangsbo doctoral thesis, 'Immunological Checkpoint Blockade and TLR Stimulation for Improved Cancer Therapy', Uppsala University - candidate openly available route to the Mangsbo 2009 human whole-blood complement content**  
- identifier: `Record retrieved from the Swedish dissertation index avhandlingar.se (search 'Sara Mangsbo'): author Sara Mangsbo; opponents/committee listed as Thomas Totterman, Angelica Loskog, Rolf Kiessling; institution Uppsala universitet; keywords 'CpG ODNs ; TLR-9 ; CTLA-4 ; PD-1 ; PD-L1 ; B7.H1 ; immunotherapy ; checkpoint blockade ; bladder cancer ; cancer ; complement ; TLRs ; Compstatin ; properdin ; C3 ; experimental animal model ; whole blood loop system'. No DiVA URN or handle obtained.`
- gap closed: Aimed at the endpoint's redistribution problem. Mangsbo 2009 is one of only three usable human-laboratory sources and the folder README records it as 'AAI copyright, no CC licence - record only', so its file cannot ship and only derived values can be used. A compilation thesis would carry the same experiments in the author's own summary chapter, where Swedish university repositories usually permit free download and often an open licence - which would make an openly available account of the whole-blood-loop complement work committable. It may also carry figure-level or appendix data the journal version compressed, which would serve H5.
- extract: First resolve the DiVA record and read its licence and full-text availability - do not treat the index hit as the thesis. Then, if the full text is available: the summary chapter's treatment of the whole-blood-loop complement experiments, and any appendix or figure giving per-donor or absolute C3a/C4a/C5a values; and the methods paragraph naming the heparinised surface, the 0.5 U/mL soluble heparin, the 10 mM EDTA stop and the Compstatin and anti-properdin controls, since the endpoint's anticoagulant convention turns on exactly those details and the journal abstract contradicts the paper on whether the blood was anticoagulated. Confirm whether any reproduced journal reprint inside the thesis carries the publisher's terms rather than the thesis licence - a reprint chapter does not become open merely by being bound into an open thesis.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — The index record was free (HTTP 200). The thesis itself was not retrieved: uu.diva-portal.org reset the connection from this container (curl exit 35) and I saw no price, no paywall notice and no login requirement anywhere. No cost is asserted or implied.
- worth: MEDIUM — It targets the endpoint's one legal exposure - half its usable human-laboratory base cannot be redistributed - and theses are the source class the repository's own guidance names as 'usually openly licensed'. Held at MEDIUM and not higher because I verified only that the thesis exists; that it contains the complement data, and that the licence helps, are both unverified.

**InvivoGen catalogue documentation for ODN 2395 (class C CpG) and ODN 2216 (class A CpG) - vendor characterization of the exact material behind the Sewing and de Boer human whole-blood experiments**  
- identifier: `Vendor product listings https://www.invivogen.com/odn2395 and https://www.invivogen.com/cpg-odns-class-c-tlr9-agonists (the class-C listing URL appeared in a retrieved web-search result set; no catalogue number, purity figure or quality specification was retrieved)`
- gap closed: Aims at the flattest number in the endpoint: 'Tested-material characterization: 0 of 7' human-laboratory sources, with the repository noting that Sewing 'reports no supplier, no purity, no Tm, no MS' while the mechanism is driven by polyanionic charge and backbone linkage count, so n-1 shortmers and incomplete sulfurisation change the measured quantity rather than diluting it. Vendor documentation for a catalogue oligonucleotide is the only characterization that exists for research-grade material of this kind. A second target in the same place: ODN 2216-class CpG-A has a MIXED phosphodiester/phosphorothioate backbone, and its per-linkage placement is an open H2 item for de Boer's CpG-A, which the Coley patent above does not supply.
- extract: Whatever quality statement the listing actually carries - purity method and figure, endotoxin level, sterility, lot/catalogue number, formulation and the sequence as the vendor prints it, including any lowercase/uppercase convention that encodes phosphorothioate versus phosphodiester per position (that convention, if present, is the H2 content for CpG-A). Two hard preconditions before any of it is used: confirm from Sewing 2017 and de Boer 2022 themselves that the vendor and catalogue number match the material each actually used - Sewing's constructs may have been synthesised in-house - and record vendor documentation as vendor-asserted specification for a catalogue line, never as analysis of the specific lot in the experiment.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — HTTP 403 Forbidden on the direct fetch, with no response body. That is a server-side block on this client, not a paywall: no price, purchase page or subscription notice was displayed. Nothing about cost is claimed. A different user agent, or the class-C listing URL, may well succeed.
- worth: LOW — Cheap to attempt and pointed at a named zero, but unverified in content and vendor-grade in provenance, and it would only ever supply specification rather than lot analysis. Listed last and honestly: if the next session has an hour, the first four leads are worth far more than this one.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- Figshare and OSF are NOT among the twenty compendium resources and have never been searched on this endpoint. I ran two Figshare searches; both returned noise, with one borderline item (doi 10.1021/acs.molpharmaceut.4c00177.s001, supporting information for a harmonized physicochemical profiling platform for therapeutic oligonucleotides, aptamer case study) that I did not open and that targets methods rather than any molecule we hold. OSF I did not search at all. Treating this as a bounded result over two queries, not as a finding that the repositories are empty.
- VERIFIED NEGATIVE worth recording rather than a failure: the FDA Integrated Review for Wainua/eplontersen (217388Orig1s000IntegratedR.pdf, 8,103,260 bytes, 8,229 text lines, downloaded and converted) contains the word complement in only four places, all nonclinical ('A transient increase in complement Bb', 'increases in complement Bb in HDF/HDM') plus one occurrence of 'complementary'. There is NO human complement measurement in it. I had expected this to be an H4 candidate, given NCT05071300 carries complement in a primary outcome; it is not. Do not spend a session on it.
- VERIFIED NEGATIVE, method-validated: the AON-D21 EudraCT numbers carried in the ClinicalTrials.gov secondary-ID fields (2021-000935-30 for NCT05018403 and 2021-006551-33 for NCT05343819) both return 'Trials with a EudraCT protocol (0)' in the EU Clinical Trials Register, as does 2012-004909-27 (the apo(a) ASO trial, access item A14, whose paywalled article body the repository wants). Control queries on the same endpoint return 4 records for volanesorsen and 3 for inotersen, so these zeros are real absences rather than a broken query. The EU-CTR bypass route for A14 therefore does not exist; CTIS (the post-2023 register) was not searched and is the remaining place to look for the AON-D21 records.
- Numeric purity and impurity ACCEPTANCE LIMITS were not obtained for any oligonucleotide, from either regulator. The FDA inotersen product quality review redacts every impurity and endotoxin limit as '(b) (4)'; the EMA Rytelo report gives the full test panel, the methods and batch-compliance statements but prints no numeric limits. PMDA review reports, which are generally less redacted on chemistry, were not searched at all - no complement-endpoint compound appeared to be Japan-approved, but I did not verify that, and PMDA remains the untried route to numeric specifications. Pharmacopoeial monographs were also not searched.
- H2 for de Boer 2022's CpG-A (ODN 2216 class) is still open. ODN 2216 has a mixed phosphodiester/phosphorothioate backbone and its per-linkage placement is exactly an H2 item, but I did not retrieve any patent, vendor page or deposit giving it. The Coley/UIRF patent I did verify, US 8,834,900, lists ODN 2395 as SEQ ID NO: 1 but the fetch found no per-position modification notation in it, so it does not close H2 and I have not listed it as a lead. GSRS does not help here either: these are research reagents, not registered substances.
- PMID 39893955, 'Inhibiting the alternative pathway of complement by reducing systemic complement factor B: Randomized, double-blind...' (Immunobiology 2025, licence cc by-nc-nd per the Europe PMC record) was surfaced but not opened. It is a human randomised trial of a factor-B ASO with alternative-pathway readouts, which makes it mechanistically on-point for this endpoint, but the drug's target IS complement, so the repository's existing rule classifies IONIS-FB-LRx as efficacy and partitions it out. Whether it publishes a sequence, and therefore whether it qualifies as H1 at all, is UNVERIFIED. Likewise AZD6912 (NCT06115967, AstraZeneca, n=40, with 'Complement factor B protein' and 'Complement functional activity (CAP)' as secondary outcomes) and ADX-038 (NCT05876312, ADARx, described in the registry as an 'siRNA duplex oligonucleotide'): both newly surfaced, neither has posted results, and I did not verify the modality of AZD6912 or the analyte list of ADX-038.
- PMID 29100032, 'Extracorporeal human whole blood in motion, as a tool to predict first-infusion reactions and mechanism-of-action of immunotherapeutics' (Int Immunopharmacol 2018, cc by-nc-nd) - the whole-blood-loop assay platform behind AACR abstract 7276 and the Mangsbo/Fletcher lineage. Retrieved as a search record but deliberately NOT listed as a lead: it supplies assay context, not any of H1-H6, since its test articles are immunotherapeutics rather than oligonucleotides. Flagged here because it is the obvious document to cite if the endpoint ever needs to describe the system that produced the imetelstat human data.
- No full publication exists yet behind AACR 2025 abstract 7276 as far as two author searches can show (Ponandai-Srinivasan S, 11 records, none relevant; Fletcher EAK, 4 records, none relevant). The imetelstat human whole-blood dataset therefore remains grey literature, and the Rytelo EPAR (Lead 1) is the best corroboration available rather than a replacement.
- Four compendium resources remain unsearched for the same reason as last round - ResearchRabbit and Undermind require a login or application, Elicit and Consensus sit behind browser checks. I did not attempt them and created no accounts. The repository's confirmed-paywalled items (A1 Henry 2014, A2 Shen 2014, A5 the Povsic Online Repository, A6 Shaw 1997) were not re-attempted; nothing in this round changes their status, and no price was observed anywhere by me today.
- I did not verify that AON-D21 is chemically identical to NOX-D21. The repository's held Immunobiology 2025 review states the lineage NOX-D19 -> NOX-D20 -> NOX-D21/AON-D21 in prose; Lead 6 supplies sequence and chemistry for D19 and D20 only. Any row keyed from Lead 6 to the clinical molecule would be an inference, and must not be made until a source stating the identity is retrieved.

### immunotoxicity — 12 leads

**Gaps targeted:** Read at origin/claude/amazing-galileo-rwiv95: toxicity/immunotoxicity/ROCKSTEADY_RESEARCH_REPORT_2026-10-02.md (incl. Revision A), research_staging/PROVENANCE.md, source_coverage_log.json, alias_archive_search.json, BEEBOP_RESEARCH_REQUEST_2026-10-02.md, RESEARCH_SOURCE_COMPENDIUM.md, RESEARCH_ACCESS_REGISTER.md.  My task brief was STALE on two of its three named gaps. Goodchild 2009 S1 (246 guide / 243 passenger strands) and Valentin 2021 S1/S2 (127 constructs, 80-ASO screen, per-position 2'OMe + PS maps) were BOTH acquired on 2026-10-03 from Europe PMC and are committed as derived CSVs. I did not re-propose them. The genuine Hornung 2005 gap is real and I closed it (lead 2).  The gaps my leads are actually aimed at, as measured in the repo: - H2, worst gap: positional chemistry exists for only 21 of 54 approved records, all from P15 Riera-Tur (17/17) and P09 Jung (4/4). ZERO of the 26 APPROVED_PATHWAY_CONTROL records (P05 Forsbach 12, P02 Coch 7, P20 Vollmer 4, P07 Heil 3), ZERO of the 5 APPROVED_AUXILIARY (P10 Kandimalla), ZERO of the 42 HOLD_OUTCOME_EXTRACTION, ZERO of the 6 HOLD_SUPPLEMENT. - The ONLY retrieval that releases already-held records is Fucini 2012 Suppl Table S1 —
