**Fucini et al. 2012, Adenosine Modification May Be Preferred for Reducing siRNA Immune Stimulation — Supplementary Data (Supp_Data.pdf), containing Supplementary Tables S1-S4**  
- identifier: `DOI 10.1089/nat.2011.0334; PMID 22519815; PMCID PMC4047996; file Supp_Data.pdf, 251.7 KB`
- gap closed: Releases the 6 HOLD_SUPPLEMENT records P06_001-006 (beta-gal 728 guide and passenger, beta-gal control guide and passenger, ApoB guide and passenger), whose Remaining Action reads 'Retrieve Supplementary Table S1 and create one experiment-level row pe...'. Per the repo's own Revision A sec A.1 and sec B this is the ONLY outstanding retrieval that releases already-held records, and it was logged as having no open route. Table S1 supplies per-position modification listings for ALL siRNAs in the study, i.e. H2 against 6 records that currently have 0/6 positional chemistry. Tables S2-S4 supply TNF-alpha numeric values (H5), against a Gate 5 that currently reads FAIL and 42 holds that are all figure-bound.
- extract: Download https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/instance/4047996/bin/Supp_Data.pdf (251.7 KB). From it pull (a) Supplementary Table S1 — the per-siRNA listing of specific sequence modifications, mapped onto P06_001-006; (b) Supplementary Tables S2 and S3 — TNF-alpha levels across the modification experiments; (c) Supplementary Table S4 — TNF-alpha reduction for guanosine modifications. Note the PMC deposit is the author submittedVersion per Unpaywall, so check the supplement's table numbering against the published article's citations before mapping rows.
- access: OPEN — Free to READ. The PMC article page serves full text and the supplementary PDF at no cost and with no login. Copyright line shown on the page verbatim: 'Copyright 2012, Mary Ann Liebert, Inc.' with a reference to the PMC Copyright notice — so this is free-to-read, NOT openly licensed, and must not be counted toward H6. Unpaywall for 10.1089/nat.2011.0334 returns is_oa=True, oa_status='green', one oa_location: host_type=repository, version=submittedVersion, license=None, url https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/4047996 . No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed anywhere.
- worth: HIGH — Highest-value lead available. It is the only file in the endpoint that converts held-but-blocked records into usable ones, it serves the top-ranked hit type (H2) and H5 simultaneously, and it costs one HTTP GET. It also corrects a standing repo classification ('no open route', 'the single highest-value remaining file ... and it is blocked') and removes an access request from Oscar's queue.

**Hornung et al. 2005, Sequence-specific potent induction of IFN-alpha by short interfering RNA in plasmacytoid dendritic cells through TLR7 — green open-access repository copy at EPFL Infoscience**  
- identifier: `DOI 10.1038/nm1191; PMID 15723075; repository record infoscience.epfl.ch/record/198831; file nm1191.pdf`
- gap closed: This is the exact source the lead scientist named as still needed: the file held as 'Hornung 2005.pdf' is actually Herzner 2015 (PMID 26343537, Nature Immunology, cGAS/Y-form DNA), confirmed by the repo's own Citation_QC. Hornung 2005 fills the empty P08 slot. It is squarely on the priority axis — TLR7 and IFN-alpha in primary human pDC/PBMC — and it is the origin of the sequence-specific siRNA immunostimulation motif work, so it supplies named siRNA sequences tested in a human system (H1) with IFN-alpha response data (H5). It also directly serves H6: the corpus currently depends on two non-OA Nature-family papers (Hornung 2005, Herzner 2015) and this gives a licensed copy of one of them.
- extract: Retrieve nm1191.pdf from the EPFL Infoscience record (record 198831). Extract the siRNA sequence table / Figure 1-2 sequence series and the per-sequence IFN-alpha values from human pDC and PBMC, with donor count and concentration. Because this is the author submitted version, cross-check every sequence and value against the published article's figure numbering before ingesting, and record version='submittedVersion' and licence='CC BY-NC-ND' in provenance. CC BY-NC-ND permits redistribution of the verbatim file but NOT derivative works — so derived sequence tables need the same treatment the project gave Goodchild/Valentin (facts extracted, publisher file not committed).
- access: OPEN — The retrieved Unpaywall record states a free repository location with an explicit licence: license='cc-by-nc-nd', host_type=repository, url https://infoscience.epfl.ch/record/198831/files/nm1191.pdf . Stated honestly: I did NOT succeed in pulling the file from this container. GET on that PDF URL returned HTTP 200 but 685,900 bytes of text/html (the Infoscience single-page-app shell, not a PDF), and WebFetch on the record page https://infoscience.epfl.ch/record/198831 returned HTTP 429 Too Many Requests. Both are bot/rate-limit obstacles, not payment barriers. No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed at any point. Note the version is submittedVersion (author manuscript), so figure and table numbering may differ from the published Nature Medicine article.
- worth: HIGH — Closes a gap the lead scientist named by name, removes the 'Confirmed no open route via Europe PMC' entry from the access register, and does so on the endpoint's top-priority receptor/cytokine axis (TLR7, IFN-alpha, primary human cells). The method generalises: a one-line Unpaywall query found an open route that a Europe-PMC-only check reported as absent, and the same check should be re-run across every 'paywalled' entry in RESEARCH_ACCESS_REGISTER.md.

**US Patent 7,884,197 B2 — Synthetic agonists of TLR9 (Idera Pharmaceuticals; Kandimalla and Agrawal)**  
- identifier: `US7884197B2 (also at image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/7884197); assignee Aceragen Inc, originally Idera Pharmaceuticals Inc; status Expired - Fee Related, anticipated expiration 2027-12-12`
- gap closed: P10 Kandimalla contributes 5 APPROVED_AUXILIARY records with 0/5 Modification Positions. This is the same inventors' own patent family and it states modification chemistry position by position, including the exact analog classes (7-deaza-dG, AraG, N1-Me-dG, 2'-OMe) and the glycerol/pentanetriol linker architecture that the 'immunomer' constructs depend on — chemistry the journal papers describe in prose but do not tabulate per position. It is also the fix for the normalization defect the repo flagged at P10_001/P10_002, where '*G1' vs '*G2' collapse to the same normalized string: the patent's G(1)/G(2)/G(3) notation is exactly the chemistry-resolved key sec C.2 recommends. Beyond the 5 held records it adds roughly 97 sequences tested in human PBMC with named cytokine readouts (H1), and per-oligo cytokine values at a stated 10 ug/ml (H5).
- extract: Pull Table I in full (all ~97 oligo entries: Oligo No., sequence string, and the modification/linker notation) and Tables VI through IX (per-oligo human PBMC cytokine secretion values with the stated 10 ug/ml concentration, cell population, and cytokine identity). Transcribe the legend defining G(1)/G(2)/G(3)/o/X/Y/L and decompose each sequence string into per-position chemistry the way the Valentin 2021 notation was decomposed. Join to P10_001-005 on construct identity, NOT on normalized sequence. Record species per table — the patent contains mouse data too and it must not be pooled with the human PBMC tables.
- access: OPEN — US patents and published applications are US Government publications and are not subject to copyright; full text and drawings are served free and without login by both Google Patents (https://patents.google.com/patent/US7884197B2/en, which I retrieved) and USPTO Patent Public Search (image-ppubs.uspto.gov). No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed. This is the one lead in this set that is unambiguously free of reuse restriction, which also makes it usable for H6 purposes.
- worth: HIGH — Serves the two top-ranked hit types at once (H2 primarily, plus H1 and H5), against a record block that currently has zero positional chemistry, from the construct designers themselves. It is openly usable with no licence caveat, the content was directly observed rather than inferred, and patents are the class the project has already found productive. The ~97-sequence table is the largest per-position chemistry set available to this endpoint that is not already held.

**EP 1 957 647 A2 / US 2007/0142315 A1 — Immunostimulatory oligoribonucleotides (Coley Pharmaceutical; Forsbach, Vollmer, Lipford)**  
- identifier: `EP1957647A2, Procedure/publication server document EP1957647NWA2; US family member US20070142315A1; applicants Coley Pharmaceutical GmbH (Duesseldorf, DE) and Coley Pharmaceutical Group, Inc. (Wellesley, MA, US); US application now assigned to Zoetis Belgium SA`
- gap closed: P05 Forsbach is the largest un-chemistried block in the endpoint: 12 APPROVED_PATHWAY_CONTROL records plus 11 HOLD_OUTCOME_EXTRACTION records, 0 of 23 carrying Modification Positions, and all 11 holds reading 'Extract Figure 1/2 motif-series outcomes and exact assay condition.' This is the same three authors' own patent family (Forsbach, Vollmer, Lipford), filed in the exact window that produced Forsbach 2008 J Immunol, and covering the same subject — immunostimulatory oligoribonucleotides acting through TLR8. Patents tabulate per-position chemistry that the J Immunol paper leaves in prose, and they tabulate cytokine values that the paper leaves in figures, so this one document can serve both the H2 gap and the H5 figure-digitization problem for the Forsbach series. P20 Vollmer (4 records, 0 chemistry) and P07 Heil (3 records, 0 chemistry) are the same Coley/TLR7-8 lineage and may be reachable from the same family.
- extract: Open the full US20070142315A1 specification (Google Patents full-text, or the USPTO PDF) rather than the EP cover page, and locate (a) the sequence tables / SEQ ID listing of oligoribonucleotides with their 2'-O-methyl positions and phosphorothioate-versus-phosphodiester linkage pattern, and (b) the Examples' human PBMC cytokine tables (IFN-alpha, TNF-alpha, IL-6, IL-12) with concentration and donor information. Then match sequences to the 23 P05 Forsbach records by base sequence AND motif name. If the match holds, check the same family (Coley/Vollmer: US8304396B2 'Immunostimulatory nucleic acids', US8198251B2 'Combination motif immune stimulatory oligonucleotides with improved activity', US8834900) for P20 Vollmer and P07 Heil.
- access: OPEN — Both documents are served free and without login: the EPO publication server (data.epo.org) and Google Patents, both of which I retrieved successfully. Patent documents carry no reuse restriction for the US member. No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed. Honest limitation on content: the EPO HTML I fetched rendered only the cover/bibliographic page — I did NOT see the sequence tables, example sequences, SEQ ID numbering or cytokine tables in the document itself. The statement that per-position chemistry and human PBMC cytokine data are present comes from the retrieved description of the US family member US20070142315A1, not from direct inspection of the tables. Treat the table contents as a strong expectation, not a verified finding.
- worth: HIGH — Aimed at the single largest positional-chemistry hole in the endpoint (23 Forsbach records) from the authors' own filings, and openly accessible. Ranked just below US7884197 only because I confirmed the document's identity but not its table contents, so a Rocksteady session must verify before counting it. The onward path to Vollmer and Heil through the same assignee makes the expected yield larger than the 23 records alone.

**ClinicalTrials.gov NCT01263691 — AV7909 (AVA + CPG 7909) Phase 1 dose-ranging safety trial in healthy adults, POSTED RESULTS with laboratory-abnormality primary endpoints**  
- identifier: `NCT01263691; official title 'A Parallel-arm, Double-blind, Randomized, Placebo-controlled, Dose-ranging Clinical Trial Evaluating the Safety, Tolerability and Immunogenicity of AV7909 in Healthy Adults'`
- gap closed: The project has ZERO clean clinical negatives and the lead scientist is blocking a classifier on that absence. This record supplies, for a molecule whose exact sequence the catalog ALREADY HOLDS, every element H4 requires: (1) exact sequence — AV7909's adjuvant is CPG 7909, i.e. agatolimod / PF-3512676 / ODN 2006, sequence TCGTCGTTTTGTCGTTTTGTCGTT, held in 7 records (P02_001 'CpG 2006', P15_026-030 ODN2006 variants, P20_001 ODN2006) per the repo's own sec C.2 cross-paper cluster; (2) human exposure in healthy volunteers; (3) adequate dose and duration — four formulations giving a dose-ranging ladder, two-dose IM schedule, Day 0 to Day 84 plus telephone follow-up; (4) EXPLICIT organ monitoring, as the registered PRIMARY endpoint: haematology (which carries the platelet count the project needs), serum chemistry and urinalysis, graded against a prespecified Toxicity Grading Scale; (5) explicit outcome — only 3 serious event terms recorded across 105 subjects; (6) traceable denominator — per-arm numAtRisk 18/18/17/19/18/15, with a 15-subject saline placebo arm giving the background rate. The saline control arm is what makes this a genuine negative rather than an absence of reporting.
- extract: Pull the resultsSection of NCT01263691: (a) adverseEventsModule — the seriousEvents and otherEvents tables in full, with each eventGroup's numAtRisk (18/18/17/19/18/15) and numAffected per term, so the denominator is carried with every row; (b) the two primary outcomeMeasures on haematology / serum chemistry / urinalysis abnormalities, with their per-arm classes and counts; (c) the baselineCharacteristicsModule for the donor/subject context. SEPARATELY, and this is required before the row can be called H4-complete: the per-formulation CPG 7909 MASS is not in the registry record. Read it from the posted protocol at cdn.clinicaltrials.gov/large-docs for this NCT, or from the CYFENDUS (AV7909) FDA prescribing information. Do not carry the 0.25 mg per 0.5 mL dose figure that circulates in secondary summaries until it is read from one of those two documents — I did not verify it. Record the system as a vaccine adjuvant exposure, and keep intended immunostimulation separate from unintended toxicity exactly as the repo's three-class convention requires.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved free, without login or key, from the public ClinicalTrials.gov API v2. ClinicalTrials.gov content is produced by the US National Library of Medicine and is a US Government work in the public domain; posted results are reusable. No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed.
- worth: HIGH — The task names H4 the highest-value find available and the project has none. This is the cleanest H4 shape I could verify: healthy volunteers, placebo-controlled, dose-ranging, with laboratory organ monitoring as the REGISTERED PRIMARY endpoint rather than an afterthought, graded on a prespecified scale, with small exact per-arm denominators, for a sequence already in the catalog. The small n (105) is a limitation for rare events but is a strength for H4, because every subject is accounted for.

**ClinicalTrials.gov NCT03877926 — VELOCITY, Phase 3 AV7909 (AVA + CPG 7909) trial, n=3,689, POSTED RESULTS including adverse events of autoimmune etiology**  
- identifier: `NCT03877926; official title 'A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-blind, Parallel-group Trial to Evaluate the Lot Consistency, Immunogenicity, and Safety of AV7909 for Postexposure Prophylaxis of Anthrax in Healthy Adults'`
- gap closed: Supplies the largest human denominator in reach for a sequence the catalog already holds (CPG 7909 = ODN 2006, 7 held records). Two things make it uniquely valuable for this endpoint. First, 'Adverse Events of Special Interest (Events of Autoimmune Etiology)' is a registered, prospectively collected IMMUNOTOXICITY endpoint with a denominator — not a post-hoc narrative — over a 394-day window, which is the longest exposure-to-outcome interval available here. Second, the comparator is BioThrax, which is the same AVA antigen WITHOUT the CpG oligonucleotide: the AV7909-versus-BioThrax contrast therefore isolates the contribution of the oligonucleotide itself, which no other human record in this project can do. 3,151 CpG-exposed subjects against 533 CpG-naive controls on an autoimmune endpoint is the strongest human immunotoxicity comparison I found.
- extract: Pull the resultsSection: (a) the adverse events of special interest / autoimmune-etiology outcomeMeasure table with per-arm counts and denominators; (b) the full seriousEvents table (63 terms) and otherEvents table (21 terms) with each eventGroup's numAtRisk (1050 / 1053 / 1048 / 533); (c) the solicited systemic and injection-site reactogenicity outcome tables, which are graded continuous-ish measures rather than binaries (H5). Preserve the lot structure — three AV7909 lots are three separate arms and must not be silently pooled unless the lot-consistency analysis justifies it. Record the BioThrax arm explicitly as the CpG-naive comparator, since that is what gives the oligonucleotide-attributable contrast. As with NCT01263691, the CPG 7909 mass per dose must be read from the posted protocol (cdn.clinicaltrials.gov/large-docs/26/NCT03877926/Prot_000.pdf, which appeared in search results) or the CYFENDUS label; it is not in the registry record and I did not verify it.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved free, without login or API key, from the public ClinicalTrials.gov API v2. US Government work, public domain. No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed.
- worth: HIGH — Second-ranked H4 behind NCT01263691 only because its monitoring is autoimmune-AESI and reactogenicity rather than registered laboratory-abnormality endpoints, so the explicit platelet/organ-monitoring element H4 requires is weaker. Against that, its denominator is 35 times larger, its follow-up is 394 days, and its comparator arm isolates the oligonucleotide. The two records are complementary and a Rocksteady session should take both.

**FDA Summary Basis for Regulatory Action, HEPLISAV-B (STN 125428/0) — exact CpG 1018 sequence, and the adjuvant CMC specification framework**  
- identifier: `STN# 125428/0; https://www.fda.gov/media/109808/download (336.6 KB PDF, retrieved and text-extracted to 1,520 lines)`
- gap closed: H1: supplies a 22-mer phosphorothioate oligodeoxynucleotide with its exact base sequence stated by the regulator, administered at 3,000 mcg per dose to more than 9,000 humans — a genuinely new molecule for this corpus, on the TLR9 axis, with the strongest provenance a sequence can have. H2 follows for free: 'phosphorothioate-linked' across a 22-mer fully determines per-position backbone chemistry, with no unmodified-linkage ambiguity, so this is one of very few records where positional chemistry is complete by construction. It is also the reference point the endpoint lacks for an approved, large-exposure TLR9 agonist.
- extract: From https://www.fda.gov/media/109808/download: the CpG 1018 Overview subsection (exact sequence, 22-mer, phosphorothioate backbone, manufacturer lineage Hybridon Specialty Products to Nitto Denko Avecia); Table 3 'HEPLISAV-B Drug Product Release and Stability Specifications' rows for '1018 identity' and '1018 content' (attribute names are visible even where values are redacted, which is still more analytical-identity structure than this endpoint currently holds); and the clinical section denominators for DV2-HBV-10, -16 and -23. Do NOT file HEPLISAV-B as an H4 clean negative: the document itself records that 'The clinical review group and biostatisticians conclude that the safety data for HEPLISAV-B are not adequate and, therefore, do not recommend approval', and notes an acute myocardial infarction imbalance with 'a greater frequency observed in the HEPLISAV-B group' in DV2-HBV-23. That is a contested safety record, not a clean negative, and mislabelling it would be exactly the kind of error this endpoint has already had to correct twice.
- access: OPEN — Freely downloadable from fda.gov with no login; I retrieved the 336.6 KB PDF and extracted its full text. FDA review documents are US Government works in the public domain. No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed. IMPORTANT NEGATIVE, stated plainly so nobody repeats this: this document does NOT deliver H3. 'Table 2. Adjuvant Release Specifications and Methods' exists but its entire contents are withheld as '(b) (4)' (the FOIA trade-secret/confidential-commercial exemption), as are the adjuvant's molecular mass and molecular formula. There is no purity value, no impurity limit and no named analytical method for the oligonucleotide anywhere in the readable text. I searched the extracted text for purity, impurity, HPLC, capillary, electrophoresis, mass spectrometry, release specification and assay.
- worth: MEDIUM — Strong, fully verified H1 with complete positional backbone chemistry and regulator-grade provenance, openly licensed. Graded MEDIUM rather than HIGH because it adds one molecule rather than a table of them, and because the H3 purity data I went looking for is redacted — which is itself a finding worth recording, since the project's standing assumption is that FDA CMC packages carry purity specifications. For this product they do not.

**EMA Withdrawal Assessment Report, HEPLISAV (EMA/186212/2014) — narrative CMC characterization of the 1018 ISS oligonucleotide drug substance**  
- identifier: `EMA/186212/2014; Procedure No. EMEA/H/C/002603/0000; https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/documents/withdrawal-report/withdrawal-assessment-report-heplisav_en.pdf (1,256,867 bytes, retrieved and text-extracted to 2,731 lines)`
- gap closed: This endpoint holds ZERO purity or analytical-identity values, and the project holds 3 real values in 3,034 oligo rows. This is a PARTIAL H3: it supplies, for a named oligonucleotide, the synthesis route (solid-phase, 4-sub-step cycle), the purification train (ion-exchange chromatography plus UF/DF plus lyophilisation), a higher-order-structure characterization method (scanning calorimetry), an explicit product-related impurity CLASS (failure sequences from random additions and deletions, assessed as functionally equivalent to the main product), and the named release attributes for identity, content and integrity. The project currently cannot populate a single one of those fields for any immunotoxicity construct. It is also the most informative characterization narrative I found for any oligonucleotide in this endpoint.
- extract: From the retrieved PDF, the '1018 ISS adjuvant' quality subsection (around pages 7-8 of 56) and the drug-product specification paragraph: transcribe the synthesis and purification route, the scanning-calorimetry higher-order-structure finding, the product-related impurity class statement, and the list of release attributes (1018 ISS Adjuvant Identity, Content, Integrity). File these against the CpG 1018 record created from lead 7 as characterization METHOD and impurity-CLASS fields, explicitly flagged as no-numeric-value. This also argues for a schema distinction the project will need anyway: analytical method and impurity class are separate fields from a purity value, and recording the first two is not evidence of the third.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved free from ema.europa.eu with no login (HTTP 200, 1,256,867 bytes). EMA assessment reports are published documents, reusable under the EMA/EU legal notice. No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed. HONEST LIMIT: the cover page states 'with all information of a commercially confidential nature deleted', and accordingly there is NO numeric purity percentage, no impurity limit and no specification value anywhere in the document. It gives methods, attributes and impurity classes, not numbers. Anyone treating this as a source of a purity VALUE will be disappointed.
- worth: MEDIUM — Fully verified and openly accessible, and it is the richest oligonucleotide characterization text available to this endpoint — but it yields no numeric purity value, so it narrows H3 rather than closing it. Its second use is strategic: taken with lead 7, it establishes that for this product BOTH the FDA and EMA dossiers withhold the purity numbers, which means the project should stop expecting regulatory CMC alone to close H3 and should test suppliers' certificates of analysis, patents' synthesis examples, and pharmacopoeial monographs instead.

**Burel et al. bioRxiv preprint of Burel 2022 — 'Early-Stage Identification and Avoidance of Antisense Oligonucleotides Causing Species-Specific Inflammatory Responses in Human Volunteers Peripheral Blood Mononuclear Cells', v1 and v2, with its supplementary material**  
- identifier: `DOI 10.1101/2021.10.30.466173; Europe PMC record PPR414449; v1 posted 2021-11-02, v2 posted 2021-11-10; bioRxiv asserts published-as DOI 10.1089/nat.2022.0033`
- gap closed: Burel 2022 is the corpus's clinical anchor and holds 0 sequence rows; its Supplementary Table S1 (ODN sequences) is the repo's top-priority access gap and the one item the last round asked Oscar for directly. Unpaywall confirms the journal version is closed (see access evidence), so this preprint is the only free route and the repo named it as 'the remaining open avenue' that was 'not retrieved in this round'. Beyond sequences, the abstract's discriminator/non-discriminator finding is exactly the donor context the priority endpoints require, and it is a per-donor IL-6 result (H5). The bioRxiv 'published' field also settles the two-way deduplication for this preprint authoritatively, from the repository rather than by inference.
- extract: Retry https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2021.10.30.466173v2.supplementary-material after the Retry-After window and list the attached files; also try v1, since supplementary attachments sometimes differ between versions, and the full-text JATS XML at https://www.biorxiv.org/content/early/2021/11/10/2021.10.30.466173.source.xml which may embed the tables directly. Target the ODN sequence table corresponding to the journal's Supplementary Table S1, and the per-donor IL-6 values behind the discriminator/non-discriminator classification. Record version (v1 or v2) and licence 'cc_no — no reuse licence' in provenance, and extract facts rather than redistributing the file, exactly as was done for Goodchild and Valentin. If the sequences are present, they resolve the identity of ISIS 353512, ISIS 104838 and ISIS 330012, and the Oscar access request in the 2026-10-02 report sec 5 can be withdrawn.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — Split status, stated precisely. The PREPRINT RECORD is open: I retrieved its full metadata and abstract free from both Europe PMC and the bioRxiv API. The SUPPLEMENTARY FILE LIST is unverified: my fetch of https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2021.10.30.466173v2.supplementary-material returned HTTP 429 Too Many Requests with Retry-After 89, so I did not see whether a sequence table is attached, or under what filename. That is a rate limit, not a paywall. Reuse licence: the bioRxiv API reports license 'cc_no', which is bioRxiv's 'no reuse licence' / all-rights-reserved setting. So this is FREE TO READ but NOT openly licensed, and it must NOT be counted as an H6 replacement for the closed journal version. For the journal version itself, Unpaywall on 10.1089/nat.2022.0033 returns is_oa=False, oa_status='closed', with no OA locations — consistent with the repo's Europe PMC finding of no PMCID and isOpenAccess=N. No price was observed for either.
- worth: HIGH — It targets the gap the repo itself ranks first among access requests, on the endpoint's only clinical anchor, and the route was explicitly left unattempted last round. Ranked below leads 1-6 because I could not confirm the supplement is actually attached — the bioRxiv page was rate-limited and Europe PMC reports hasSuppl=N for the preprint record, which is weak evidence against. Worth one retry before anyone asks Oscar for the journal file.

**Pollak et al. bioRxiv preprint — 'Mechanism Driven Early Stage Identification and Avoidance of Antisense Oligonucleotides Causing TRL9 Mediated Inflammatory Responses in Bjab cells' (never published; resolves the repo's open deduplication question)**  
- identifier: `DOI 10.1101/2021.12.12.472280; Europe PMC record PPR431755; v1 posted 2021-12-14; bioRxiv published field = 'NA'`
- gap closed: First, it ANSWERS a standing open question rather than adding one. The repo's sec 4.4 records Burel as 'a three-way deduplication case' and flags that 'Whether preprint 2 is the same study or a companion needs inspection before the extension-counting convention is applied'. The bioRxiv 'published' field is 'NA' and the study is a different first author, a different system (Bjab line vs PBMC) and a different readout (Ccl22 mRNA vs IL-6 protein). It is therefore a SEPARATE, NEVER-PUBLISHED COMPANION STUDY, not a second preprint of Burel 2022, and it must be counted once in its own right. Second, per proposal 3's classification rule it is human_cell_line, so its outcomes must not be pooled with PBMC data — the same constraint already applied to Yoshida's HEK-Blue reporter rows. Third, its benchmark panel of PS-ASOs 'previously shown to be either safe or inflammatory in clinical trials' is a named list of oligos carrying clinical safety classifications, which is an H4 feeder even though the preprint is not itself a clinical record.
- extract: Fetch the full text and any supplementary material from https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2021.12.12.472280v1 (or the JATS at /content/early/2021/12/14/2021.12.12.472280.source.xml). Pull (a) the benchmark PS-ASO control panel with each oligo's sequence and its stated clinical safety classification (safe vs inflammatory) — this is the highest-value content; (b) the Ccl22 mRNA RT-qPCR values per construct, as raw continuous outcomes; (c) the stated calibration mapping between the Bjab assay and the hPBMC assay, which is what would license any cross-system comparison. Register the study with system='human_cell_line', published='no', and counted separately from Burel 2022, and correct the repo's sec 4.4 'three-way deduplication' framing to two sources plus one companion.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — The record and abstract are open: retrieved free from both Europe PMC and the bioRxiv API. The supplementary file list was NOT checked (I prioritised the v2 supplement of lead 9, which itself hit HTTP 429), and Europe PMC reports hasSuppl=N. Reuse licence from the bioRxiv API is 'cc_no' — bioRxiv's no-reuse-licence setting — so free to read, NOT openly licensed, and not usable for H6. No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed.
- worth: MEDIUM — Its main immediate value is adjudicative — it closes an open question in the repo with repository-level evidence rather than inference, and prevents a wrong collapse of two distinct studies. Its data value is real but secondary and constrained: cell-line Ccl22 values cannot sit beside PBMC cytokine data without German's ruling, the same limitation already recorded for Yoshida. The benchmark-panel-with-clinical-classification is the part worth chasing.

**Riera-Tur et al. 2024, Characterization of the TLR9-Activating Potential of LNA-Modified Antisense Oligonucleotides — CC BY published version**  
- identifier: `DOI 10.1089/nat.2024.0013; PMID 39018509; no PMCID; publisher PDF https://www.liebertpub.com/doi/pdf/10.1089/nat.2024.0013?download=true`
- gap closed: Two purposes. (1) H6, verified: P15 Riera-Tur is the single largest APPROVED_CORE_HUMAN block (17 records) and one of only two blocks in the endpoint carrying positional chemistry (17/17). Its licence being CC BY, confirmed by two registries, means this block's underlying source can legally ship in an openly licensed dataset — which is exactly what Phase 2 requires and what the project cannot say for Burel (closed), Hornung (CC BY-NC-ND, no derivatives), Fucini (free-to-read, publisher copyright) or either bioRxiv preprint (cc_no). This should be recorded in the access register as a licence FACT about an already-held source, which it currently does not carry. (2) H3, a candidate only: a paper titled 'Characterization of...' from an industrial ASO developer is the most plausible remaining place to find purity or analytical identity for constructs the project already holds.
- extract: Fetch the CC BY PDF at https://www.liebertpub.com/doi/pdf/10.1089/nat.2024.0013?download=true (or via the Sage platform) and check the Materials and Methods for ASO synthesis and quality control — specifically any purity percentage, HPLC or UPLC method, mass-spectrometry confirmed mass, or supplier certificate reference — and record the result either way, because a verified negative here is worth having. Also capture the human donor context (PBMC or whole blood, donor numbers) for the 17 held P15 records. Then add 'licence: CC BY (confirmed Europe PMC + Unpaywall)' to the access register against P15. BE AWARE: Europe PMC reports hasSuppl=N for this article, which is evidence AGAINST a supplementary table existing, so do not plan on one.
- access: OPEN — Licence CC BY confirmed by two independently retrieved records: Europe PMC license='cc by' and Unpaywall license='cc-by' on a publisher publishedVersion location. Retrieval from this container FAILED, for non-payment reasons I am recording exactly: WebFetch on https://www.liebertpub.com/doi/10.1089/nat.2024.0013 returned HTTP 301 redirecting to https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1089/nat.2024.0013?cf-mal-redirected=true& (the Liebert-to-Sage platform migration), and fetching that returned HTTP 403 Forbidden — a Cloudflare bot check. No price, purchase page or access requirement was observed at any point, and a 403 bot check is not a paywall.
- worth: MEDIUM — The H6 half is verified and immediately useful: it is a licence fact about the endpoint's largest approved human block, cheap to record, and directly relevant to whether the dataset can ship. The H3 half is explicitly an expectation, not a finding — I could not open the article, and hasSuppl=N argues against a characterization table. Ranked last of the HIGH/MEDIUM set for that reason, and listed with the negative evidence visible so nobody mistakes the hypothesis for a result.

**Forsbach et al. 2008, Identification of RNA Sequence Motifs Stimulating Sequence-Specific TLR8-Dependent Immune Responses — free publisher published-version PDF (registries disagree)**  
- identifier: `DOI 10.4049/jimmunol.180.6.3729; PMID 18322178; candidate free PDF https://journals.aai.org/jimmunol/article-pdf/180/6/3729/983301/zim00608003729.pdf`
- gap closed: P05 Forsbach holds 11 of the endpoint's 42 HOLD_OUTCOME_EXTRACTION records, every one reading 'Extract Figure 1/2 motif-series outcomes and exact assay condition', plus 12 APPROVED_PATHWAY_CONTROL records with 0/12 Modification Positions. All 42 holds are figure-bound and none can be reconstructed from text or tables, so the published figures at full resolution are the only route, and Gate 5 stays FAIL until someone digitizes them. If the bronze route works, 11 holds become workable with no access request at all. This is the companion to lead 4: the patent family supplies the chemistry, the paper supplies the figure values, and together they cover both gaps on the same 23 records.
- extract: Retry https://journals.aai.org/jimmunol/article-pdf/180/6/3729/983301/zim00608003729.pdf with a normal browser user agent, or reach it from the AAI article landing page for J Immunol 180(6):3729. If it opens, extract (a) Figures 1 and 2 at full resolution and digitize the motif-series cytokine values for the 11 P05 holds, recording the exact assay condition, concentration and donor count alongside each value and flagging every value as curator-derived with its digitization method and uncertainty, per the validation memo; (b) the RNA oligonucleotide table from Materials and Methods, to test whether per-position chemistry can be read from the paper before falling back to the patent family in lead 4. If the bronze route stays blocked, say so plainly in the access register as a technical block with conflicting registry evidence — not as a paywall.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — Honest default, because two registries disagree and I could not retrieve the file. Unpaywall asserts a free publisher publishedVersion; Europe PMC asserts 'Subscription required'. My GET on the AAI PDF URL returned HTTP 200 but only 1,482 bytes of text/html — a bot-check or interstitial stub, not a PDF. That is a technical obstacle and NOT proof of a paywall, and I observed no price, no purchase page and no stated access requirement. The 'bronze' classification means free-to-read at the publisher's discretion with NO reuse licence (Unpaywall license=None), so even if it opens it cannot serve H6 and derived values must be handled as facts-only, like Goodchild and Valentin.
- worth: MEDIUM — Aimed at the largest single block of figure-bound holds, and if the free route opens it costs nothing. Graded MEDIUM and placed last because I could not retrieve it, two registries disagree about whether it is free, and the repo's own analysis warns that digitized figure values attached to constructs of unverified chemistry carry two uncertainties into the training view — which is why lead 4 should be attempted first, so the chemistry is resolved before the values are derived.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- NO NUMERIC PURITY VALUE WAS FOUND ANYWHERE THIS ROUND. H3 remains effectively open. Both regulatory dossiers I retrieved withhold the numbers: the FDA SBRA redacts Table 2 'Adjuvant Release Specifications and Methods' entirely as '(b) (4)', along with the molecular mass and molecular formula; the EMA report states on its cover that all commercially confidential information is deleted and gives methods and impurity classes but no values. This is a substantive finding about the source class, not just a miss: the project's working assumption that FDA/EMA CMC packages carry purity specifications did not hold for this product. Suppliers' certificates of analysis, patent synthesis examples, and pharmacopoeial monographs are the untested alternatives.
- I verified the EXISTENCE and IDENTITY of every lead but did not read the target table inside any of them. In particular I did not open Fucini's Supp_Data.pdf (lead 1) — I confirmed the file, its size, its link and Supplementary Table S1's caption from the article text, but a Rocksteady session must still download it and confirm the table's contents and row count before the 6 held records are called released.
- EP 1 957 647 A2 (lead 4): the EPO HTML rendered only the cover/bibliographic page. I did NOT see its sequence tables, SEQ ID numbering, example sequences or cytokine tables. The claim that per-position chemistry and human PBMC data are present rests on the retrieved description of the US family member US20070142315A1, not on direct inspection. Verify before counting.
- Riera-Tur 2024 (lead 11): the H3 content is unverified. liebertpub redirected to journals.sagepub.com, which returned HTTP 403 (Cloudflare). I never saw the Materials and Methods, so I cannot say whether purity, HPLC or mass-spectrometry data are reported. Europe PMC reports hasSuppl=N, which is evidence against a supplementary characterization table.
- Both bioRxiv supplementary file lists (leads 9 and 10) are unseen — HTTP 429. I cannot confirm that a sequence table is attached to either preprint, and Europe PMC reports hasSuppl=N for both preprint records.
- The CPG 7909 mass per dose in AV7909 is UNVERIFIED. The 0.25 mg per 0.5 mL figure appeared only in a WebSearch summary; I did not open the CYFENDUS prescribing information or the AV7909 BLA. Both H4 leads need that figure read from the posted trial protocol or the FDA label before the dose element of H4 is satisfied.
- The CYFENDUS (AV7909) FDA approval package and any Summary Basis of Approval were NOT retrieved. AV7909 was approved in July 2023, so a CBER review package likely exists and is the most promising untested H3 target for CPG 7909 specifically — a molecule whose sequence the project already holds in 7 records. This is the single best next lead I did not get to.
- PMDA review reports and Japanese ASO grey literature: NOT SEARCHED. Named in the brief as under-searched and I did not reach it, despite Yoshida 2024 being a Japanese source already in the corpus.
- Pharmacopoeial monographs (USP, Ph. Eur., JP) for oligonucleotides: NOT SEARCHED. Given that both regulatory dossiers redacted their purity values, a general chapter or monograph specifying oligonucleotide purity methods is now the more likely H3 route and should be tried next.
- Doctoral theses: NOT SEARCHED. The brief flags these as often carrying the full sequence tables that journal versions truncate, and as usually openly licensed (so they serve H6 too). Secarna (Riera-Tur, Jaschinski) and the Bonn/Munich groups behind Hornung, Coch and Herzner are the obvious places to look.
- EudraCT, WHO ICTRP and jRCT: NOT SEARCHED. Only ClinicalTrials.gov was queried, so the H4 registry sweep is one registry deep. The ISIS 329993-CS1 registry identifier that the repo lists as unresolved was not pursued.
- Zenodo, Figshare, Dryad and OSF: NOT re-searched. The prior round's keyword pass was shown to be tokenizer noise (GEO translating 'SY-ODN18' to 'SY[All Fields]'), and the correct method — exact-accession lookup from each priority paper's data-availability statement — was not run by me either.
- The 30 Sioud 2005 HOLD records, the endpoint's single largest figure-bound block, got no dedicated source search. I found no patent family, preprint or repository deposit for Sioud; the 30 holds still appear to require digitizing Figure 1 TNF-alpha and IL-6 values from the paper itself.
- The four reporter-cell negatives (P15_031, P15_032, P15_035, P15_036) that the repo calls the highest-severity open item in this endpoint are a scientific adjudication for German, not an acquisition target, so I did not search for sources against them. Flagging that they remain open and that nothing I propose addresses them.
- I did not attempt any of the 9 blocked or API-less services from the prior round's coverage log (OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar, ToxCast/CTX, CTD, ICE, ResearchRabbit, Undermind, Elicit, Consensus). I used Unpaywall and the bioRxiv API instead, neither of which is on the project's 20-resource list — and Unpaywall in particular found open routes for two sources the register lists as inaccessible, so it is worth adding to the compendium.

### cns-original (mapped to the 21-oligo human-reaching lineage in toxicity/_shared/cns on origin/claude/oligo-toxicity-dataset-k394sz, which feeds chronic-neurotoxicity, hydrocephalus and acute-neurotoxicity) — 9 leads

**Gaps targeted:** Read from toxicity/_shared/cns/docs/CHARACTERIZATION_COVERAGE.md (generated, do-not-hand-edit): Human-reaching subset = 21 oligos. Sequence as printed 10/21 (48%); per-position modification map 8/21 (38%); of those source-resolved 6/21 (29%); purity value 0/21; analytical identity confirmation 0/21; purification method 13/21. OPEN_ITEMS.md OI-02 is still OPEN and names purity/characterization as "the single largest gap between what this dataset is and what the challenge text describes".  Read the actual rows, not just the summary. chronic-neurotoxicity/data/oligos.csv: 13 oligos. Exactly two carry sequences from the clinical layer — C1-OLG-0001 tofersen and C1-OLG-0002 nusinersen — and BOTH have modification_position_basis = derived_from_motif, purity_pct = NOT_REPORTED, identity_confirmation = NOT_REPORTED. Six carry NOTHING: CT1-OLG-0002 tominersen, CT1-OLG-0004 BIIB080_MAPTRx, CT1-OLG-0005 WVE-120101, CT1-OLG-0006 WVE-120102, CT1-OLG-0007 BIIB105, CT1-OLG-0008 WVE-003 — sequence, backbone, modification_positions, purity and identity all NOT_REPORTED, source_location "NCTxxxx protocolSection", with the note "The registry prints no sequence or chemistry".  Read acute-neurotoxicity

**FDA/NCATS Global Substance Registration System (G-SRS) nucleicAcid substance records, retrieved through the public API**  
- identifier: `UNII 7QI41X9QWC tominersen (INN 11052, CAS 1709886-74-7, USAN HI-118); UNII 96J3L33PWD diranersen = BIIB080 (INN 13317, CAS 2304711-06-4); UNII 7OXK16K08B rovanersen = WVE-120101 / WV-1092 / BMTR-5150 (INN 11852, USAN HI-114, CAS 2072901-32-5); UNII AMW2VPZ3XD lexanersen = WVE-120102 / WV-2603 / BMTR5149 (INN 11353, USAN GH-20, CAS 2073015-40-2); UNII 2NU6F9601K tofersen; UNII 5Z9SP3X666 nusinersen; UNII TND56EA1LE ulefnersen = ION363`
- gap closed: Closes the mandatory per-position modification field for the four compounds that currently have modification_positions = NOT_REPORTED and carry chronic-neurotoxicity rows: CT1-OLG-0002 tominersen, CT1-OLG-0004 BIIB080_MAPTRx, CT1-OLG-0005 WVE-120101, CT1-OLG-0006 WVE-120102. Per-position maps 8/21 -> 12/21. Source-resolved positions 6/21 -> 12/21, because the same records also convert tofersen and nusinersen from derived_from_motif to position-resolved. Sequence as printed 10/21 -> 14/21. The rovanersen and lexanersen records additionally carry per-linkage phosphorothioate R/S stereochemistry, which the current schema has no column for - these are stereopure molecules and the dataset would be silently wrong to record them as plain full_PS. ulefnersen/ION363 (GCAAUGTCACCTTTCAUACC) is a further molecule with human intrathecal CNS exposure not yet in the dataset, available as an H1 route.
- extract: For each UNII: GET https://drugs.ncats.io/api/v1/substances(<UNII>)?view=full and read four JSON paths - nucleicAcid.subunits[].sequence, nucleicAcid.sugars[].{sugar,sites[].residueIndex}, nucleicAcid.linkages[].{linkage,sites[].residueIndex}, and modifications.structuralModifications[].{molecularFragment.name, structuralModificationType, sites[].residueIndex}. Linkage sites are indexed by the residue index of the 3' partner, so a 20-mer yields sites 2..20 = 19 linkages; verify that sP + P site counts equal length-1 before writing a row. Use the API, not the web UI: https://drugs.ncats.io/substance/7QI41X9QWC returned "Verification Required - To protect this public service from automated bulk retrieval, please verify your browser", while the API needed no authentication. TWO CAUTIONS, both verified by me and both load-bearing. (1) The nusinersen record's subunits.sequence field reads TCACCTTTATAATGCTGG, which contradicts its own systematic name in the same record (M5U-C-A-C-M5U-M5U-M5U-C-A-M5U-A-A-M5U-G-C-M5U-G-G = TCACTTTCATAATGCTGG), contradicts the Spinraza EPAR, and contradicts the value already in the repo; its own 5-methylcytosine sites [2,4,8,15] point at a T in its own subunit string. Do not overwrite the repo's nusinersen sequence from the subunit field - take nusinersen from the EPAR (lead 3) and treat the GSRS subunit field as a known-bad cell. (2) tominersen's sequence CUCAGTAACATTGACACCAC is the same molecule as the repo's L1-OLG-0005 "Kuroda2025_ASO5" (CTCAGTAACATTGACACCAC). The dataset currently holds tominersen twice under two oligo_ids from two sources. That is a dedup and train/test leakage issue, not a new molecule.
- access: OPEN — Public NCATS/FDA API, no authentication, no rate-limit challenge, no stated licence restriction. Work of the US federal government (FDA Substance Registration System); UNII data are published as public-domain reference data. Record each UNII and the retrieval date as provenance.
- worth: HIGH — Ranked first because it hits H2, the highest-ranked gap type, for four compounds that are currently completely blank, and it does so from a structured machine-readable government register rather than from prose that needs interpreting. It is cheap (seven HTTP GETs), public domain, and it independently reproduces the tofersen map that lead 2 gives from a different regulator - which is the kind of two-source agreement this project needs after being burned twice. It also surfaces two concrete data-quality defects in the existing dataset.

**Qalsody : CHMP assessment report (European Public Assessment Report), tofersen**  
- identifier: `EMA/276404/2024, Procedure No. EMEA/H/C/005493/0000; file qalsody-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf at https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/documents/assessment-report/qalsody-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf`
- gap closed: H2: gives tofersen's map at full source resolution, including the exact positions of the four phosphodiester linkages (linkages 2, 4, 16 and 18 by the s/o string) and the five 5-methyl residues. The repo's C1-OLG-0001 row currently records modification_position_basis = derived_from_motif with the explicit note "Positions are expanded from that stated motif" - this replaces an inference with source content. H3: gives the first non-empty analytical identity for a human-reaching compound, taking analytical identity confirmation off 0/21, and a real purification/analytical method string for purity_method.
- extract: Three specific places, not the paper. (a) Section 2.4.1.1, pages 13-14: the shorthand sequence string, the s/o linkage legend, the molecular formula C230H317N72O123P19S15 and MW 7127.86 - these populate modification_positions, backbone_linkage_positions, ps_linkage_count and n_5methyl_C. (b) The characterisation paragraph immediately after Figure 1 - copy verbatim into identity_confirmation. (c) Section 2.4.1.3 specification test list - copy verbatim into purity_method. HONEST LIMIT, verified: no numeric purity or impurity limit appears anywhere in the document. The cover page states "Assessment report as adopted by the CHMP with all information of a commercially confidential nature deleted", and section 2.4.1.3 says only "appropriate limits have been set in line with guidelines". purity_pct must stay NOT_REPORTED. Do not let a method string be recorded as a value - that is exactly the distinction CHARACTERIZATION_COVERAGE.md calls load-bearing.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved by direct unauthenticated HTTPS download. Licence stated in the sibling Spinraza report and standard across EPARs: "Reproduction is authorised provided the source is acknowledged." Attribution-only, no NC or ND clause - so unlike K1/HV3 this is shippable.
- worth: HIGH — Hits both of the two highest-ranked gap types for the dataset's flagship molecule, is openly licensed, and agrees residue-for-residue with the independent GSRS record in lead 1. The s/o notation is the only public source I found that pins down WHICH four linkages are phosphodiester - the INN description the repo currently relies on states the counts but not the positions.

**Spinraza : CHMP assessment report (European Public Assessment Report), nusinersen**  
- identifier: `EMA/289068/2017, Procedure No. EMEA/H/C/004312/0000; file spinraza-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf-0 at https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/documents/assessment-report/spinraza-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf-0`
- gap closed: H2: upgrades C1-OLG-0002 nusinersen from derived_from_motif to position-resolved, and - the part the current row is actually missing - gives the 5-methyl positions. The repo's modification_positions string for nusinersen records only sugar chemistry (1:T:2'-MOE ... 18:G:2'-MOE) and leaves n_5methyl_C blank; the EPAR sequence marks MeU and MeC residue by residue. H3: analytical identity for the second of the two sequenced clinical compounds, taking analytical identity confirmation to 2/21, and purity_method from 13/21 toward 15/21.
- extract: Section 2.2.2 "Active Substance / General information", pages 11-13 of the PDF: the MeU/MeC sequence string, the full chemical name, the molecular formula and MW -> modification_positions and n_5methyl_C. The characterisation paragraph (NMR/MS/ICP-OES/combustion/FTIR plus failure sequence analysis) -> identity_confirmation verbatim. The specification test list on page 14 -> purity_method verbatim. TWO LIMITS, both verified. (1) No numeric purity limit: the report carries the same "all information of a commercially confidential nature deleted" cover note, and the text says only "Specification limits are based on ICH requirements, process capability". purity_pct stays NOT_REPORTED. (2) The document is internally inconsistent about the sugar: section 2.2.1 calls the active substance "the sodium salt of a 18-mer 2'-O-methyl-phosphothioate oligoribonucleotide" while section 2.2.2 and the chemical name both give 2'-O-(2-methoxyethyl). Extract from 2.2.2 and record the 2.2.1 wording as a known source error, or a later reader will think the dataset contradicts the EPAR.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved by direct unauthenticated HTTPS download. Page 1 states: "(c) European Medicines Agency, 2017. Reproduction is authorised provided the source is acknowledged." Attribution-only.
- worth: HIGH — Same two top-ranked hit types as lead 2, openly licensed, and it is the arbiter for the nusinersen sequence conflict that lead 1 exposes. Taken together leads 2 and 3 move analytical identity for the human subset off zero, which is one of the two zeros the brief names.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted Protocol and Statistical Analysis Plan documents for four Huntington/tau ASO trials the repository already holds as JSON**  
- identifier: `NCT03225833 Prot_SAP_000.pdf (dated 2020-01-21); NCT03225846 Prot_SAP_000.pdf (2019-09-17); NCT03186989 Prot_SAP_000.pdf (2022-04-27); NCT04617847 Prot_SAP_000.pdf (2020-03-31)`
- gap closed: This is the project's zero-to-one on H4, and it does not require acquiring a single new clinical dataset. H4 needs six things at once; after lead 1 the repository can have all six for WVE-120101/rovanersen and WVE-120102/lexanersen: exact sequence and per-position chemistry (GSRS UNII 7OXK16K08B / AMW2VPZ3XD), human exposure (held JSON), adequate dose and duration (2/4/8/16/32 mg intrathecal, Day 1 to Day 210, plus the open-label extensions NCT04617847/NCT04617860 for longer exposure), explicit platelet and organ monitoring (quoted above from the posted protocol - platelet count, prothrombin time, CBC, complement, fibrinogen, clinical chemistry, hematology, urinalysis), explicit outcome (12 itemised serious events, 109 itemised other events), and a traceable denominator (9, 9, 9, 8, 10 by dose arm against 16 pooled placebo). It also serves H5: the AE module holds raw per-arm affected/at-risk counts rather than curator-derived binaries.
- extract: Pull the four Prot_SAP_000.pdf files from https://cdn.clinicaltrials.gov/large-docs/<last two digits of the NCT number>/<NCT id>/Prot_SAP_000.pdf. From each, extract two things only: the safety-laboratory schedule table (the "Clinical laboratory safety testing" footnote and the schedule-of-assessments rows naming platelet count, prothrombin time, CBC, complement, fibrinogen, chemistry, urinalysis) and the stopping/withdrawal criteria - these are the "explicit monitoring" element and they exist nowhere in the JSON the repo already holds. Then join to the already-held resultsSection.adverseEventsModule by eventGroup. ADJUDICATION WARNING, not a data gap: a clean clinical NEGATIVE is a judgement, not a field. The 8 mg (0/9 serious) and 16 mg (0/8 serious) arms are the candidates, but the verdict must be read from the itemised seriousEvents and otherEvents arrays per arm, not from the group totals, and it must be decided whether a non-CNS adverse event in a CNS endpoint counts against a CNS-specific negative. Note also that PRECISION-HD1/HD2 were discontinued for lack of efficacy, not for safety - which is what makes them candidate negatives rather than positives.
- access: OPEN — Both URLs I tested returned HTTP/2 200 with content-type application/pdf, unauthenticated. ClinicalTrials.gov is a US National Library of Medicine service and its posted records and documents are US government works in the public domain - the same basis on which the repo already ships CT1 as "public domain".
- worth: HIGH — The brief calls a clean clinical negative "the highest value find available" and says its absence has the lead scientist blocking a classifier. This is the cheapest credible route to it: four public-domain PDFs plus four API calls from lead 1, against trials whose results JSON the repository already holds and has already committed. Nothing has to be bought, requested or licensed.

**Antisense oligonucleotide-mediated knockdown therapy in two infants with severe KCNT1 epileptic encephalopathy**  
- identifier: `PMID 41981306; PMCID PMC13099374; DOI 10.1038/s41591-026-04314-9; Nature Medicine 2026`
- gap closed: H1 if the supplement prints the sequence: a new molecule tested in a human system, which is the only thing that raises the molecule count. It is also the single most on-target source I found for the sibling hydrocephalus endpoint, which holds just 2 oligos and 12 measurements - here the adverse outcome IS ventricular enlargement, in 2 of 2 patients, under repeat intrathecal dosing, with a stated denominator and a stated monitoring framing ("a potential monitorable toxicity"). H5 if the supplement carries per-dose ventricular-volume measurements rather than a narrative.
- extract: The supplementary information file (hasSuppl Y on PMC13099374), specifically any table giving the KCNT1 ASO sequence and chemistry, the dosing schedule with dates and milligram amounts, and the serial imaging measurements of ventricular volume per patient. The article body for the monitoring and outcome narrative. Do NOT record this as a clean negative - it is a positive toxicity finding, and it is a 2-patient n-of-few, so the denominator is 2.
- access: OPEN — Europe PMC reports inEPMC Y with license "cc by-nc-nd" - freely retrievable in Europe PMC, licence recorded as CC BY-NC-ND. I did not open the PDF or the supplement, so I have not confirmed the licence statement on the file itself.
- worth: HIGH — A new human-exposed CNS molecule with an explicit CNS toxicity, a denominator, and chronic dosing is rare, and this one lands precisely on the hydrocephalus signal the repository has almost no rows for. Ranked below leads 1-4 for one reason the project must weigh before investing: the recorded licence is CC BY-NC-ND, the same restriction that already prevents K1 and HV3 from shipping. It is an H1 that may deepen the H6 problem rather than relieve it, so decide the licensing question before extraction, not after.

**To scramble or not: Steric blocking 2'-MOE/PS control antisense oligonucleotides across cellular models**  
- identifier: `PMID 42733805; PMCID PMC13571576; DOI 10.1016/j.omtn.2026.103051; Molecular Therapy Nucleic Acids 2026`
- gap closed: Potentially supplies a small set of fully sequence- and chemistry-specified 2'-MOE/PS control ASOs with measured toxicity across several cell models. That matters disproportionately here because the repo's control_role column exists and the Hagedorn panel's controls are already the dataset's negative-control backbone - well-characterised human-cell control ASOs with sequences would strengthen the control arm rather than just add rows. Chemistry is 2'-MOE/PS, the same class as tofersen and nusinersen, so it is directly comparable to the clinical layer.
- extract: The supplementary tables (hasSuppl Y on PMC13571576): the control-ASO sequence and modification table, and the per-model RNA/protein and toxicity readouts with their numeric values. Before extraction, check two things I could not: whether any of the seven cellular models is human and neural (the abstract does not say - it only says "species-specific and cell-type-specific"), and whether the models include anything beyond acute exposure. If no model is human and neural, this belongs to a different endpoint, not cns-original.
- access: OPEN — Europe PMC records license "cc by-nc-nd" and the record is in Europe PMC. I did not open the article or its supplement.
- worth: MEDIUM — Honest MEDIUM, not HIGH. The shape is right - sequences plus chemistry plus multi-model toxicity, with a supplement - but I verified only the abstract, and the two facts that decide its value for THIS endpoint (any human neural model, any non-acute exposure) are not stated in it. It is also CC BY-NC-ND. A Rocksteady session should spend thirty minutes checking the supplement before committing to it.

**Safety and toxicity assessment of antisense oligonucleotides in brain-relevant human cellular models**  
- identifier: `PMID 42724647; PMCID PMC13560553; DOI 10.1016/j.omtn.2026.103062; Molecular Therapy Nucleic Acids 2026`
- gap closed: Supplies no measurement rows - hasSuppl N and it is a review, so it closes no characterization gap directly. Its value is twofold and I am labelling it plainly so nobody over-reads it. First, it is CC BY, which the repo's O1 instruments source (CC BY-NC) is not - so it is a candidate openly-licensed replacement for the scales-and-instruments layer, serving H6. Second, it is the field's own 2026 survey of precisely this endpoint's gap, so its reference list is a pre-filtered candidate pool for extending the human in-vitro backlog beyond the eight sources already queued.
- extract: The reference list and the assay/platform comparison tables - mine these for primary studies in human iPSC neurons, astrocytes, microglia and organoids that are NOT already in RESEARCH_QUEUE.md, then verify each candidate independently before proposing it. Also the assay-definition text, which can serve the SCORING_INSTRUMENTS layer under a CC BY licence. Extract no measurement rows from it.
- access: OPEN — Europe PMC records license "cc by", inEPMC Y.
- worth: LOW — Deliberately LOW: it contributes zero rows and the project has repeatedly been damaged by secondary material being treated as evidence. I am reporting it because the brief asks for every hit type found and because a CC BY instruments source has real H6 value against a CC BY-NC incumbent - but it is a search accelerant, not a data source, and should not consume a session on its own.

**Milasen n-of-1 clinical report paired with its originating patent (sequence-source candidate)**  
- identifier: `NEJM article: PMID 31597037, PMCID PMC6961983, DOI 10.1056/NEJMoa1813279. Patent: US 11,999,953 B2, "Compositions and methods for treating transposon associated diseases", assignee Boston Children's Hospital / The Children's Medical Center Corporation, priority 2017-09-13, filed 2018-09-12, granted 2024-06-04`
- gap closed: Would be the project's cleanest-shaped H4 if the sequence can be pinned: a single named molecule, human, intrathecal, repeat-dosed over roughly a year, with explicit safety monitoring, an explicitly stated outcome of no serious adverse events, and an unambiguous denominator of n = 1. It would simultaneously be H1 (a new human-exposed molecule) and H2 (per-position chemistry from the patent's sequence listing). A 22-mer uniform 2'-MOE/PS chemistry also makes it directly comparable to nusinersen, which this dataset already holds in its clinical layer.
- extract: Two files, in this order. (1) The US11999953B2 sequence listing, from the USPTO full-document PDF at https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/11999953 (this URL appeared in a search result but I did not fetch it) or from Google Patents' downloadable sequence listing - look for a SEQ ID with 22 residues targeting the MFSD8 intron 6 cryptic splice acceptor, with per-position 2'-MOE / phosphorothioate / 5-methylcytosine notation. (2) The NEJM supplementary appendix via PMC6961983 for the dosing schedule, the safety-monitoring schedule and the adverse-event statement. CRITICAL HONESTY FLAG: I have verified that both documents EXIST; I have NOT verified that either prints the milasen base sequence, and the patent may claim a genus of oligonucleotides without identifying which one was dosed. If the specific dosed sequence cannot be tied to the specific clinical exposure, this is not an H4 - it is a clinical narrative with no molecule, and it must not be recorded as one.
- access: OPEN — Patent: US patents and their sequence listings are public-domain US government publications. NEJM article: Europe PMC reports inEPMC Y with isOpenAccess N and license None - that is free to read through PMC but with no open licence recorded, so it is a read-only source like the repo's S1/B2/M1 and cannot be redistributed. Extract the clinical facts from it, cite it, do not commit the file. I observed no price and no purchase page.
- worth: MEDIUM — The H4 shape is the best in the literature for a sequence-identified chronic human CNS exposure with a negative safety outcome, which is why it is here at all. It is MEDIUM rather than HIGH because the one thing that makes it an H4 - the sequence-to-exposure link - is exactly the thing I could not verify, and because the clinical document has no open licence. Pull the patent sequence listing first; if the dosed molecule is not identifiable there, drop the lead rather than reasoning toward it.

**FDA Product Quality Review(s) for QALSODY (tofersen), NDA 215887 - VERIFIED DEAD END, recorded so no session repeats it**  
- identifier: `Application number 215887Orig1s000, PRODUCT QUALITY REVIEW(S), Reference ID 5110399; https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2023/215887Orig1s000ChemR.pdf`
- gap closed: Closes nothing. I am reporting it because the obvious first move on a 0/21 purity gap is "get the FDA approval packages for the ~17 approved oligo therapeutics", and for the one approved CNS oligo where I tested it, that move returns three pages of boilerplate and 84 withheld pages. Recording this as a verified negative is worth more than another speculative lead: it stops a Rocksteady session spending a day on the Drugs@FDA route and redirects it to the EMA assessment reports (leads 2 and 3), which for the same two molecules do publish the sequence, the molecular formula, the analytical identity methods and the specification test list.
- extract: Nothing. Do not queue this file. The generalisable finding, which I checked on a third regulator as well: a purity VALUE for an approved oligonucleotide does not appear to be in the public regulatory record at all. FDA withholds the CMC review in full as confidential commercial information; the EMA assessment reports for both Qalsody and Spinraza carry the cover note "all information of a commercially confidential nature deleted" and state only that "appropriate limits have been set in line with guidelines"; and the Swissmedic SwissPAR for Qalsody (68577, 2026-02-10, downloaded and searched) says only "the specifications include all relevant test parameters" with no numeric limit anywhere. Three regulators, same answer. OI-02's framing is correct and the honest route to a purity value is not regulatory - it is per-compound characterization tables in synthesis-chemistry supplements, patent synthesis examples, or the HV1-HV3 supplementary methods the repo already names as its own lead.
- access: OPEN — Freely downloaded from accessdata.fda.gov without authentication; US government work, public domain. The barrier is redaction under FOIA exemption b(4) (confidential commercial information / trade secret), not cost. No price, purchase page or subscription requirement was observed anywhere - this is explicitly NOT a paywall and must not be recorded as one.
- worth: LOW — LOW as a source and deliberately so - it yields no data. Its worth is as a closed door, documented with the exact sentence that closes it, on the single route most likely to be tried next for the project's largest gap.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- No purity VALUE (a percentage) was found anywhere, for any compound. This is the endpoint's largest gap (0/21) and I did not close it. What I did establish, by retrieval rather than inference, is that three separate regulators do not publish one: FDA withholds the tofersen CMC review in full (84 pages, FOIA b(4)); both EMA assessment reports carry 'all information of a commercially confidential nature deleted' and state only that appropriate limits were set; the Swissmedic SwissPAR names test parameters with no limits. Routes I did NOT reach and that remain open: the ORIGINAL PMDA Spinraza review report and the PMDA Qalsody review report (I retrieved only PMDA 000228335.pdf, which is the August 2017 PARTIAL CHANGE approval for a new indication and has no quality section at all); Health Canada Summary Basis of Decision SBD00361 for Spinraza (URL seen in a search result, not opened); pharmacopoeial monographs (USP, Ph. Eur., Japanese Pharmacopoeia) - not searched; and patent synthesis examples, which sometimes print per-compound mass found/calculated and HPLC purity - not searched.
- BIIB105 (CT1-OLG-0007) and WVE-003 (CT1-OLG-0008) remain without any sequence. Both carry chronic-neurotoxicity rows. I searched the NCATS GSRS register for BIIB105 (0 hits), WVE-003 (159 hits, none a matching nucleic acid), ION-541, ION541, IONIS-ATXN2, WV-3972, zalfonersen and HTT SNP3, and a bare 'WVE' query returned only rovanersen, lexanersen and suvodirsen. Neither compound appears to have an INN or a UNII yet, so the register route that works for the other four does not work for these two. The patent route for them was NOT attempted - Ionis ATXN2 and Wave SNP3-selective HTT patents would be the next move, and patents are already proven productive on this project.
- I did not verify the actual sequence content of the two documents in lead 9. For milasen I confirmed that the NEJM article (PMID 31597037, PMCID PMC6961983) and the patent (US 11,999,953 B2) both exist, and nothing more; I did not open the NEJM supplementary appendix and the Google Patents rendering did not expose the SEQ ID listing. If the dosed 22-mer cannot be tied to the clinical exposure, lead 9 is not an H4 and should be dropped rather than argued for.
- H5 for the existing 34 human in-vitro rows is untouched. I confirmed the rows carry no parseable numeric readout but made no attempt to recover the underlying values from the HV1/HV2/HV3 supplementary methods and source-data files - RESEARCH_QUEUE.md and CHARACTERIZATION_COVERAGE.md already name those as the module's own named leads, so re-proposing them would have been noise rather than a new source.
- H6 is largely unaddressed. The brief says half of one endpoint cannot legally ship; the restricted CNS sources are K1 Miller 2024 and HV3 Woffindale 2026, both CC BY-NC-ND. I found no openly licensed replacement for either. Worse, two of my own leads (5 and 6) are themselves CC BY-NC-ND and would deepen the problem if committed - flagged in each entry. The only H6-positive item I found is lead 7, a CC BY review that could replace the CC BY-NC incumbent in the instruments layer only.
- Repository searched only: Zenodo, via one API query that returned 12 records, none relevant. Figshare, Dryad, OSF, GEO, ArrayExpress, BioStudies, PRIDE and SRA were NOT searched, despite the brief weighting data repositories and the compendium naming all of them.
- Trial registries beyond ClinicalTrials.gov were NOT searched: no EudraCT, WHO ICTRP or jRCT queries were run. The jRCT gap matters most, because it is the registry where Japanese ASO development appears and the brief explicitly flags Japanese ASO work as under-searched. Non-English grey literature was effectively not reached - my single Japanese-language attempt returned the wrong PMDA document.
- No preprint servers or doctoral theses were searched (bioRxiv, medRxiv, institutional repositories), despite the brief naming them as both sequence-rich and usually openly licensed.
- I did not open toxicity/_shared/cns/deliverables/OligoTox-CNS_SourceRegister.pdf, which RESEARCH_QUEUE.md states is the authoritative generated provenance record and whose section 7 lists the human in-vitro backlog with payloads. My duplicate-check therefore rests on the hand-maintained queue file, which that same file warns can disagree with the register. Leads 1-4 are regulatory and registry classes the queue's licence notes suggest are untouched; leads 5-8 carry the residual duplication risk.
- For leads 5, 6 and 7 I verified the Europe PMC core record and the abstract, and did not open the articles or their supplements. Everything I claim about their contents beyond the abstract is explicitly marked unverified in the lead itself - in particular whether lead 6's seven cellular models include a human neural one, which decides whether it belongs to this endpoint at all.

### cns-alternate (branch origin/claude/oligo-cns-toxicity-dataset-tijib6, dir toxicity; 592 oligos / 2,538 measurements; chronic partition 2,393 rows / 573 oligos) — 12 leads

**Gaps targeted:** Read off the branch, not assumed.  G2 CHARACTERIZATION: recomputed from toxicity/notes/cns/corpus/cns_oligos.csv. All 592 rows carry purity_pct, purity_method, identity_confirmation; distribution for all three is exactly {NOT_REPORTED: 585, NOT_APPLICABLE: 7}. Zero real values. Matches sec.9 of toxicity/chronic-neurotoxicity/ROCKSTEADY_RESEARCH_REPORT_2026-10-02.md.  G1 SEQUENCE/MODIFICATION POSITION: 466/592 rows have a sequence; 126 do not, 109 non-preclinical. No modification-POSITION column exists - the 21 columns hold only backbone_chemistry, sugar_modifications, gapmer_design, conjugate, ps_count (counts and class labels, never positions). Clinical molecules with sequence_5to3=TBD include CNS001 atipeksen/AT008 (ATM ph2), CNS005 BIIB105/ION-541 (ATXN2), CNS015 WVE-003/HTT-1526 (HTT ph2), CNS002 BIIB078, CNS004 BIIB094, CNS008 ION582, CNS288 rugonersen. CNS001's note says the AT008 alias and 2'-MOE/PS chemistry "come from search-result summaries only, not from a primary document fetched here - treat as unverified". CNS015's note says PMC11255113 was fetched in full and establishes HTT-1526 = WVE-003 "but it prints no nucleotide sequence anywhere in the full text ... sequences
