**Iwamoto et al., Preclinical evaluation of stereopure antisense oligonucleotides for allele-selective lowering of mutant HTT - SUPPLEMENT ONLY (Document S1 = mmc1.pdf)**  
- identifier: `PMCID PMC11255113; PMID 39027419; doi:10.1016/j.omtn.2024.102246`
- gap closed: Closes CNS015 WVE-003/HTT-1526 sequence, currently TBD with the repo's note that the main text 'prints no nucleotide sequence anywhere'. Adds up to 14 further named oligos with sequences tested in human patient-derived motor neurons, against a human-in-vitro base of 116/2,393 rows (4.85%) of which only 13 identifiers carry a sequence. Table S4's observed mass and dual HPLC retention times are analytical identity data for a phase-2 human molecule, against 0/592 identity_confirmation and 3 real purity values project-wide. HTT-956/957/958/1526/1533 all share ONE base sequence and differ only in backbone chemistry and stereochemistry - the clearest possible case for a modification-position column, and an immediate leakage group.
- extract: mmc1.pdf: Table S1 (p.6) all 15 sequences; Table S3 (p.8) Sequence-and-chemistry, Tm, IC50; Table S4 (p.9) predicted/observed mass and both HPLC retention times; Table S5 (p.10) means +/- sem and P values. Then main-text Table 1 (BACHD cortex/striatum t1/2, IC50, Kout) and Figure 2D/2E dose-response in patient-derived motor neurons.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved without credentials. Licence statement inside the JATS: 'This is an open access article under the CC BY-NC-ND license (http://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/).' Under this repo's own rights table CC BY-NC-ND maps to summary_stat, NOT republishable in bulk - so sequences and chemistry can be recorded as facts but the tables must not be reproduced verbatim in a CC-BY release. No price displayed anywhere.
- worth: HIGH — Four hit types at once, the file is already in hand, and it resolves a gap the repo documented and failed to close. The one thing I could NOT verify: Table S3's 'Sequence and chemistry' cells render as graphics and extracted as empty text, so the per-position chemistry needs a visual read of p.8 - do not assume pdftotext will yield it.

**Kim, Woo, de Gusmao, Zhao et al., A framework for individualized splice-switching oligonucleotide therapy - Supplementary Table 13 'ASO sequences and chemistry'**  
- identifier: `doi:10.1038/s41586-023-06277-0; PMID 37438524; PMCID PMC10371869; file 41586_2023_6277_MOESM4_ESM.xlsx`
- gap closed: Gives CNS001 atipeksen its sequence (ATATAAGCATCACAAAGTACCT, 22 nt) from a primary document, retiring the repo's explicit 'treat as unverified' note on that row. Adds ~87 further ASOs with sequence, length, target variant and purpose. Most important: the caption is the only true PER-POSITION modification notation I found anywhere this round - it resolves PS linkage position, 5-methyl-C position and DNA-vs-2'MOE position for the ASO-tox control, i.e. exactly the three things the task says are unlocated for 100% of records. It is also a ready-made notation convention for the modification-position column that does not yet exist.
- extract: 41586_2023_6277_MOESM4_ESM.xlsx, sheet 'S Table 13' (89 rows, all columns) plus its caption string; then sheet 'S Table 14' (ASO mis-splicing rescue in patient fibroblasts, the human in-vitro outcome). Direct URL verified working: https://static-content.springer.com/esm/art%3A10.1038%2Fs41586-023-06277-0/MediaObjects/41586_2023_6277_MOESM4_ESM.xlsx
- access: OPEN — Downloaded with no credentials. Licence block in the JATS: 'Open Access This article is licensed under a Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International License' - CC BY, i.e. republishable with attribution under the repo's own rights table. No price displayed. Note the PMC mirror of the same file (pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/instance/10371869/bin/...) returned text/html 21,319 B, i.e. a wall page - use the Springer host.
- worth: HIGH — Entirely absent from the repo (0 concordance hits for the DOI, the PMCID and the sequence). CC BY, so bulk-republishable, which is rare for a sequence table here. It is the one lead that both raises the sequence count and supplies genuine per-position notation. Caveat to carry: fibroblasts and whole blood are human but NOT neuronal, so it does not by itself close the human-neuronal part of G3.

**EMA CHMP assessment report, Wainzua (eplontersen) - section 2.2 Quality aspects, Active Substance**  
- identifier: `EMA/CHMP/515905/2024; file wainzua-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf`
- gap closed: First real purity_method and identity_confirmation values available for CNS007 eplontersen, a molecule that already HAS a sequence (who_inn_nomenclature) - so one row goes from sequence-only to sequence + purity method + three orthogonal identity methods. Against 0/592 on all three characterization columns. The galactosamine absolute configuration is per-residue conjugate chemistry, addressing the 'conjugate positions unlocated' half of the modification-position gap. The platelet counts are raw continuous values where the repo holds curator-derived binaries.
- extract: Section 2.2.2 Active Substance, Characterisation and Specification sub-sections (pp.18-20) for purity_method, identity_confirmation and the diastereomeric-composition method; section 2.2.3 Finished Medicinal Product specification (around p.24) including the justification for omitting purity from release testing; and the clinical-safety platelet paragraph around p.98 (txt line 6159) for the numeric platelet series.
- access: OPEN — Fetched directly from ema.europa.eu with no credentials, HTTP 200. EMA assessment reports are treated as public_domain in this repo's own rights table (same class as the Qalsody EPAR already held). No price or access requirement displayed.
- worth: HIGH — H3 is the top-ranked gap and this is a clean, quotable, public-domain fill for a molecule already carrying a sequence. Zero concordance hits for EMA/CHMP/515905/2024 or 'Wainzua' - entirely new. Pairs with lead 5 to make eplontersen the endpoint's first fully characterized record.

**EMA CHMP assessment report, Spinraza (nusinersen) - section 2.2.2 Active Substance (Quality section, currently unmined)**  
- identifier: `EMA/289068/2017; file spinraza-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf-0`
- gap closed: Supplies purity_method 'purity (Full Length n) by IP-HPLC-UV-MS' and a named identity battery for CNS012 nusinersen - the single most-cited molecule in the corpus. 'Full Length n' is the actual purity attribute name for a therapeutic oligo and is worth adopting as the controlled vocabulary for the purity_method column. The 2^17-diastereoisomer statement independently corroborates the repo's ps_count=17 for CNS012.
- extract: Section 2.2.2 Active Substance: the Characterisation sub-section (p.12) and the Specification sub-section (pp.17-18); then section 2.2.3 finished-product specification (p.26). Do not re-extract the nonclinical sections - those 5 rows are already cited.
- access: OPEN — Fetched from ema.europa.eu, no credentials, HTTP 200. Public-domain class per the repo's own rights table. No price displayed.
- worth: HIGH — Highest-ranked gap, biggest molecule. BUT flag honestly: EMA/289068/2017 is ALREADY a cited source_ref on this branch (5 animal_invivo rows, 274 concordance hits). What is new is that the Quality section was never mined - purity is still 0/592 - and no PDF of the document exists under toxicity/sources/cns/. Propose it as 'mine section 2.2.2 of a document we already cite', not as a new source.

**PMDA Review Report, Qalsody Intrathecal Injection 100 mg (tofersen) - section 2 Quality**  
- identifier: `pmda.go.jp file 000277928.pdf (Report on the Deliberation Results, Qalsody, tofersen, Biogen Japan, application 21 May 2024)`
- gap closed: purity_method and identity_confirmation for CNS013 tofersen, which already has a patent-derived sequence. Independently corroborates the repo's ps_count=15 (2^15 diastereomers) and adds the count of 2'-MOE sugars (10 of 20) - a constraint on modification position that the repo does not currently record. Opens an entire source class: there are zero PMDA documents of any kind in this endpoint ('PMDA'/'pmda' = 0 concordance hits), and the task explicitly flags Japanese regulatory grey literature as under-searched.
- extract: Sections 2.1.1 Characterization, 2.1.3 Control of drug substance, Table 1 (drug substance control strategy), and 2.2.3 Control of drug product - pp.2-4 of the PDF.
- access: OPEN — Fetched from pmda.go.jp, no credentials, HTTP 200. The document carries PMDA's standard notice that the English translation 'is intended to serve as reference material made available for the convenience of users' and that the Japanese original takes precedence - record that caveat, and cite the Japanese original as the authority. No price displayed.
- worth: HIGH — Second mandatory field, new molecule covered, and a new source class with roughly a dozen more oligo review reports behind it (Onpattro, Amvuttra, Tegsedi, Viltepso and others). Also a clean cross-check: 2^15 independently confirms a number the repo derived from a patent.

**PMDA Review Report, Spinraza Intrathecal injection 12 mg (nusinersen sodium) - section 2 Data Relating to Quality**  
- identifier: `pmda.go.jp file 000228334.pdf (Report on the Deliberation Results, Spinraza, Nusinersen Sodium (JAN), Biogen Japan, application 7 December 2016)`
- gap closed: A second, methodologically independent purity_method and identity_confirmation record for CNS012 nusinersen (PMDA uses LC-MS and ICP-OES wording where EMA uses IP-HPLC-UV-MS). Two regulators agreeing is what turns a single extracted value into a defensible one, and it also adds XRPD and IR to the identity battery, which the EMA report does not list.
- extract: Sections 2.1.1 Characterization, 2.1.3 Control of drug substance, 2.2.3 Control of drug product - pp.6-7 of the PDF.
- access: OPEN — Fetched from pmda.go.jp, no credentials, HTTP 200. Same PMDA translation caveat as lead 5. Numeric acceptance criteria and impurity identities are masked with asterisk runs in the published text (e.g. 'Impurity a ****** impurity') - so the METHODS are recoverable and the NUMBERS are not. State that rather than recording a blank.
- worth: MEDIUM — Lower than lead 5 only because EMA/289068/2017 already covers the same molecule more fully. Its real value is corroboration plus the extra identity techniques. Honest limit: no numeric purity criterion is obtainable.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted RESULTS module for NCT04494256 (BIIB105 / ION-541, intrathecal ATXN2 ASO, ALSpire)**  
- identifier: `NCT04494256`
- gap closed: H5 directly: plasma NfL ratio-to-baseline and CSF/serum PK are raw continuous values by dose group, against a corpus that holds curator-derived binaries. NfL is the exact biomarker chronic-neurotoxicity.next-steps.md names as the one assay bridging in-vitro, NHP and clinical scales. The dose-graded CSF-protein series with clean denominators is also the shape H4 requires - it becomes a usable clean negative at the 5 mg and 20 mg cohorts (0 serious AEs in 6 and 6) the moment BIIB105 has a sequence.
- extract: From the API: resultsSection.outcomeMeasuresModule - the NfL ratio-to-baseline measure and the CSF/serum concentration measures with their dispersion values; and resultsSection.adverseEventsModule otherEvents/seriousEvents with every eventGroup denominator. Endpoint: https://clinicaltrials.gov/api/v2/studies/NCT04494256
- access: OPEN — Public US government data, retrieved over the open API without credentials, HTTP 200. public_domain under the repo's own rights table.
- worth: HIGH — Flag precisely: NCT04494256 is already cited, but only as registration metadata in CNS005's design_source ('metadata only; no sequence'); it is absent from the 29 NCT numbers cited by cns_measurements.csv, so the results module is unmined. Hard blocker to state plainly: CNS005 has no sequence, and I could not find one (see lead 9), so these rows attach to an unsequenced molecule until that is solved.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted RESULTS module for NCT04136184 (NEURO-TTRansform, eplontersen vs inotersen) - adverse-event module, for H4 only**  
- identifier: `NCT04136184`
- gap closed: The project has ZERO clean clinical negatives and the lead scientist is blocking a classifier on it. This record has every element H4 names: exact sequence (CNS007 eplontersen, who_inn_nomenclature, already held), human exposure, 66-week duration, EXPLICIT platelet monitoring with numbers, explicit outcome (1/144 thrombocytopenia on eplontersen), and traceable denominators (144/24/20). The within-trial comparator is inotersen, the known platelet-toxic oligo, at 4/24 - so the negative is anchored against a positive in the same protocol rather than against absence of reporting. mNIS+7 and serum TTR percent change are raw continuous nervous-system outcomes (H5).
- extract: resultsSection.adverseEventsModule: the three eventGroups with deaths/serious/other denominators, and the stats blocks for 'Thrombocytopenia' and 'Platelet count decreased'; plus resultsSection.outcomeMeasuresModule entries for mNIS+7 at weeks 35 and 66 with dispersion. Pair with the Wainzua EPAR platelet paragraph in lead 3 for the continuous platelet series.
- access: OPEN — Public US government data over the open API, HTTP 200, no credentials. public_domain.
- worth: HIGH — Must be proposed honestly: the repo FETCHED this trial and deliberately declined it, recording 'not mined: ... eplontersen NCT04136184 (fetched; its only CNS-coded serious events were single syncope/vertigo/cerebral-haemorrhage terms with no placebo arm - it is an open-label active-controlled study)'. That rejection was about CNS-coded serious events and it was right. H4 asks a different question - platelet/organ monitoring with an explicit outcome and a denominator - and on that question this trial is the strongest candidate I found. The 'no placebo arm' objection is also what makes it work here: an active comparator that IS platelet-toxic is a better control for a negative than placebo. Caveat to carry: eplontersen is subcutaneous and the neuropathy is peripheral, so this is nervous-system, not CNS.

**Vanden Broeck et al. (per JATS), To scramble or not: Steric blocking 2'-MOE/PS control antisense oligonucleotides across cellular models - supplementary set**  
- identifier: `doi:10.1016/j.omtn.2026.103051; PMID 42733805; PMCID PMC13571576`
- gap closed: Supplies a designed, sequence-defined set of 2'-MOE/PS control ASOs characterized for toxicity across human cellular models - the repo holds four control/scramble ASOs (CNS569, CNS571, CNS575, CNS579) with no sequences at all. mmc12/mmc13 give transcriptome- and proteome-level values for NUSINERSEN itself, a molecule the corpus already holds, as raw per-gene and per-protein numbers rather than a curator binary (H5). The deliberate species contrast (human vs NIH/3T3) speaks to the next-steps.md warning about overstating human in-vitro evidence.
- extract: mmc1.pdf Tables S1-S3 first - that is where the cASO sequences, GC content and 2'-MOE/PS design live. Then mmc12.xlsx (Table S15) and mmc13.xlsx (Table S16) for the nusinersen DEG/DEP values, and mmc7.xlsx (Table S10) for the human retinal-organoid DEGs. Use the supplementaryFiles?includeInlineImage=false route proven in lead 1.
- access: OPEN — Full text retrieved without credentials, HTTP 200. Licence read from inside the article: CC BY-NC-ND, which maps to summary_stat in this repo's rights table - facts extractable, tables not bulk-reproducible. I did not download the 15 supplementary files, so their individual retrievability is unconfirmed; no price was displayed anywhere.
- worth: MEDIUM — Zero concordance hits, so genuinely new, and it is primary data with a large structured supplement. Ranked below the top five because the cell panel is mostly non-neuronal (HEK293T, HeLa, retinal organoids) and the licence blocks republication, and because I verified the supplement's CAPTIONS but not its CONTENTS - the sequence table in mmc1.pdf is inferred from the caption 'Tables S1-S3', not seen.

**FDA Product Quality Review(s), NDA 215887 (QALSODY / tofersen)**  
- identifier: `215887Orig1s000ChemR.pdf (accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2023/)`
- gap closed: Would be a third independent regulator on tofersen characterization - but on inspection it does NOT close the gap, because the 23 pages are an executive summary with the drug-substance specification withheld as (b)(4) trade secret.
- extract: Nothing worth a Rocksteady session on its own. If pulled, take only the NDA Executive Summary product-identity block (p.1) and the paragraph on extractable impurities. The equivalent FDA chemistry review for NDA 209531 (nusinersen) did not surface in search; I located only 209531orig1s000medr.pdf, the medical review.
- access: OPEN — Downloaded from accessdata.fda.gov with no credentials, HTTP 200; US government work, public domain. Redactions are marked '(b) (4)', a FOIA trade-secret exemption, not a paywall - no price was displayed.
- worth: LOW — Reported so the next session does not spend a round rediscovering it. The FDA route is the WEAKEST of the three regulators for this gap and EMA and PMDA are both stronger: EMA prints the full specification attribute list and PMDA prints the methods. Useful negative result: on oligo CMC, do EMA and PMDA before FDA.

**Safety and toxicity assessment of antisense oligonucleotides in brain-relevant human cellular models - Tables 1 and 2 as a source-harvesting map**  
- identifier: `doi:10.1016/j.omtn.2026.103062; PMID 42724647; PMCID PMC13560553`
- gap closed: Does not close a gap itself. Its Tables 1 and 2 are a curated inventory of the human 2D and 3D in-vitro platforms actually used for ASO toxicity, with citations - i.e. a ready-made candidate list for the 4.85% human-in-vitro gap, assembled by domain specialists. Tables 3-8 are assay and dose-range methodology for the human iPSC panel that next-steps.md Gap 1 asks to be generated, including the gymnotic-delivery point the repo makes.
- extract: Tables 1 and 2 - harvest the cited primary references, screen each for an inline or supplementary sequence table, and only then queue the ones that pass. Tables 3, 4 and 8 for assay and IC50 dose-range conventions.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved without credentials, HTTP 200, CC BY per the licence statement inside the article. No price displayed.
- worth: MEDIUM — Explicitly a REVIEW with no sequences, so under this endpoint's own rule ('Reviews were used to find primary sources and to cross-check them, not as the cited source for a value') it must never be a source_ref. Ranked as a discovery instrument. Already appears once on the branch, as a hit in chronic-neurotoxicity.search-log-2026-10-02.csv, never ingested.

**US12188020B2, Methods for reducing Ataxin-2 expression (Ionis) - electronic sequence listing**  
- identifier: `US12188020B2`
- gap closed: Was pursued as the route to CNS005 BIIB105/ION-541's missing sequence. It does not get there. The patent carries ATXN2 ASO sequences, but no compound-code to SEQ ID mapping for ION-541 or BIIB105, which is exactly the hazard CNS005's own note already records: 'none of them mentions this compound's development code, so no compound-code -> SEQ ID mapping is available locally and any sequence assignment would be a guess.'
- extract: Only if someone wants the Isis No. 708137 CMAP data from Examples 3-4. Do NOT attempt to map BIIB105 to a SEQ ID from this document - that is precisely the kind of inference that produced the project's earlier bad attributions.
- access: OPEN — Google Patents record retrieved with no credentials. US patents are public domain, consistent with the 16 patents already held under toxicity/sources/cns/fpo/. No price displayed. I did not retrieve the ST25 sequence-listing text file itself, so its contents are unverified.
- worth: LOW — Reported as a negative result so the next session does not repeat it. BIIB105's sequence remains unfound after the patent route, the WHO INN route (the repo already searched Recommended Lists 70-95 and Proposed Lists 108-135) and this round's search. It should be escalated as a named unresolved item rather than quietly re-attempted.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- BIIB105 / ION-541 sequence: NOT FOUND. Patent route (US12188020B2) carries ATXN2 sequences but no compound-code to SEQ ID mapping; WHO INN was already exhausted by the repo across Recommended Lists 70-95 and Proposed Lists 108-135. CNS005 therefore stays at sequence TBD, which blocks lead 7 from being a clean H4. Recommend escalating this as a named unresolved item rather than re-running the same three routes.
- Per-position chemistry in Wave Table S3: the 'Sequence and chemistry' column of mmc1.pdf p.8 renders as graphics and extracted as empty text. The table EXISTS and is named; its contents need a visual read. I have not seen the per-position notation itself and am not claiming it.
- The 13 other supplementary files of PMC13571576 (mmc1.pdf and mmc2-14.xlsx) were NOT downloaded. I verified their captions from the article XML only. The cASO sequence table is inferred from the caption 'Tables S1-S3', not seen.
- No numeric purity acceptance criterion was obtained from ANY regulator. EMA and PMDA publish the specification attribute list and the analytical methods but withhold the limits; PMDA masks impurity identities with asterisk runs; FDA redacts as '(b) (4)'. So purity_method and identity_confirmation are fillable from these leads and purity_pct is probably NOT fillable from any public regulatory source. That is a finding, and the purity_pct column may need to stay empty with the reason recorded rather than chased further.
- I did not look for PMDA review reports for Onpattro (patisiran), Amvuttra (vutrisiran), Tegsedi (inotersen) or Viltepso (viltolarsen), all of which are plausibly available and all of which correspond to molecules in this corpus. The PMDA route is proven; it is simply unexhausted.
- Not searched at all this round: EudraCT, WHO ICTRP, jRCT, Health Canada Summary Basis of Decision (a Qalsody SBD and Regulatory Decision Summary did surface in search results at dhpp.hpfb-dgpsa.ca but I did not open them), pharmacopoeial monographs (USP, Ph. Eur., JP), doctoral theses, Figshare, Dryad, OSF, GEO and ArrayExpress. BioStudies was not re-queried with the construct-alias strategy the repo's own log identifies as the fix for its 28,486-hit query.
- Nothing was purchased, no subscription taken, no credential used, no author or sponsor contacted. No price was displayed to me at any point, so no lead is tagged PAYWALLED_VERIFIED. The one access gate I did observe was Zenodo record 10.5281/zenodo.7783848 ('A Framework for Individualized Splice-Switching Oligonucleotide Therapy', Kim/Woo/de Gusmao/Zhao/Yu, deposited 2023-07-10), whose API response carries "access": "restricted" with an empty files list and no licence - a request-access gate, not a price. I did not propose it as a lead because its underlying Nature article (lead 2) is CC BY and I already downloaded the sequence table from there.

### hydrocephalus (toxicity/hydrocephalus on origin/claude/hydrocephalus-toxicity-oligos-t172zv) — 10 leads

**Gaps targeted:** Read from the branch itself, not from the brief. (1) data/oligos.csv holds 53 roster entries; only 13 carry any sequence text and only 6 of those appear in human rows. purity_pct is NOT_REPORTED for 50/53 - the only three real values (90-97%) sit on HYD-OLG-0019/0020/0021, the rat Gai2 ICV/ex-vivo ODNs. (2) data/modifications.csv holds 256 per-position rows covering exactly 13 oligo_ids (0015, 0019-0021, 0026, 0032, 0039-0044, 0049) and every one is derived either from a WHO INN chemical-name parse (basis = position_resolved_from_WHO_INN_chemical_name, sources WHO_INN_List_74/75/77/81/83/85) or from the two animal papers. So the modification map is INN-driven, and the INN route has been used for only 6 INNs. (3) The intrathecal_lumbar compounds with NO sequence and NO chemistry are exactly the on-route priority set: HYD-OLG-0005 BIIB080, 0006 BIIB105, 0047 valeriasen, 0051 WVE-003, 0052 WVE-120101, 0053 WVE-120102. (4) README section 2 states "0 rows" for human laboratory/ex-vivo evidence and that workbook sheet 3_human_lab_evidence is present and empty; the only in vitro/in vivo pair in the release is animal (study_type in_vitro = 2 rows, animal). (5) data/_ctgov_outcome_measureme

**WHO Drug Information, Recommended INN List 94 - entry 'diranersen' (= BIIB080, the intrathecal tau ASO already in the roster with no sequence)**  
- identifier: `WHO Recommended INN List 94, entry diranersenum/diranersen; https://cdn.who.int/media/docs/default-source/international-nonproprietary-names-(inn)/rl94.pdf (HTTP 200, 4,264,479 bytes)`
- gap closed: HYD-OLG-0005 BIIB080 is route=intrathecal_lumbar with sequence_5to3_asprinted=NOT_REPORTED and modification_pattern=NOT_REPORTED, and contributes zero rows to the 256-row modifications.csv. This single entry yields the full base sequence AND per-position sugar/base-methylation/linkage chemistry for one of the six intrathecal roster compounds that currently have none, moving human sequence coverage from 6/41 to 7/41 and adding ~20 per-position rows. It is the highest-route-relevance of all the unresolved compounds (intrathecal lumbar, tau, Alzheimer's, same administration route as the nusinersen/tofersen/tominersen hydrocephalus signal).
- extract: rl94.pdf, the 'diranersen / diranersenum' entry (English chemical name block beginning 'all-P-ambo-2'-O-(2-methoxyethyl)-5-methyl-P-thiocytidylyl-...') plus its molecular formula line. Feed it to the existing scripts/parse_inn_sequences.py, which already refuses to emit a sequence that disagrees with the label formula. Then link to NCT05399888 / NCT03186989 for the human exposure side.
- access: OPEN — Freely downloadable from cdn.who.int with no login (HTTP 200, 4,264,479 bytes, retrieved 2026-10-03). Licence: the repository's existing WHO_INN_List_* rows record 'WHO publication; reuse terms not established in this session' - I did not re-establish WHO reuse terms, so carry that same wording rather than upgrading it.
- worth: HIGH — H2 is a mandatory Phase 2 field, the compound is already in the roster, the route is the endpoint's priority route, the parser already exists in the repo, and acquisition cost is one HTTP GET. Nothing else I found closes a named intrathecal chemistry gap this cheaply.

**Unparsed oligonucleotide entries inside the five WHO INN list PDFs the project ALREADY cites (Lists 75, 77, 81, 83, 85)**

Run scripts/parse_inn_sequences.py over them and let its label-formula cross-check gate each emission. Expect roughly 20-25 per-position rows per compound.

Licence wording should stay as the repository's existing 'WHO publication; reuse terms not established in this session'.

**Preclinical evaluation of stereopure antisense oligonucleotides for allele-selective lowering of mutant HTT (Iwamoto et al., the WVE-003 primary paper)**  
- identifier: `PMID 39027419 / PMCID PMC11255113 / DOI 10.1016/j.omtn.2024.102246, Molecular Therapy Nucleic Acids 2024`
- gap closed: This is the strongest single answer to the endpoint's headline gap: ZERO human in-vitro and ZERO human ex-vivo rows, no human-to-animal bridge at all. It supplies human-system rows (HD patient iPSC-derived motor neurons, homozygous and heterozygous SNP3 donors) for named molecules WITH their sequences and per-linkage stereochemistry (Table S1), raw continuous IC50 values (Table S3, 3.14 / 1.40 / 4.26 uM) rather than curator binaries, and analytical identity/characterisation methods (LC-HRMS + HPLC, Tables S7/S8) against a project-wide total of 3 real purity values in 3,034 rows. It also builds the bridge the Challenge asks for, because the same molecules are then run in BACHD and Hu97/18 mice IN THE SAME PAPER, and because the comparator HTT-2723 is explicitly 'designed based on tominersen' - tominersen being the one compound in this dataset with an observed human hydrocephalus signal. It additionally provides the sequence context for HYD-OLG-0051 WVE-003 (intrathecal_lumbar, sequence NOT_REPORTED), whose clinical study NCT05032196 is already a held source.
- extract: From the supplementary bundle (Document S1, Figures S1-S5 and Tables S1-S10): Table S1 = sequence AND chemistry of every oligonucleotide, with the ^ Rp/Sp per-linkage annotation and PN (phosphoryl guanidine) linkage positions - this is the H2/H3 payload; Table S3 = the iPSC-neuron dose-response and IC50 values (H5); Tables S7 and S8 = the two HPLC gradient methods with column part numbers 186009485 and 186003949 (H3 analytical identity). From the article body, Figure 3D/3E for the human iPSC motor-neuron dose-response curves. Named constructs to roster: HTT-1526, HTT-1533, HTT-956, HTT-2723 (tominersen surrogate), HTT-1233, APO-424, APO-543, APO-544, APO-545, APO-1920, APO-1921, plus the chemically matched NTC.
- access: OPEN — Europe PMC reports isOpenAccess Y, licence 'cc by-nc-nd'. Full text XML and the supplementary bundle both returned HTTP 200 from the Europe PMC REST API with no login. Note the ND/NC terms: the repository already handles exactly this case with redistribution=summary_stat_only (Choroid_plexus_siSPAK_LNP_2025_NatCommun, CC BY-NC-ND 4.0), so the same handling applies.
- worth: HIGH — It is the only source I verified that hits four of the six types at once, it is the only route I found to the endpoint's first human in-vitro rows, and the human/animal pair sits inside one paper so the bridge needs no cross-source assumption. Ranked below the two INN leads only because the brief ranks H2/H3 field completion above everything and the INN leads close named chemistry gaps with certainty; this one requires a curator decision on whether HTT-knockdown readouts are endpoint-admissible rows.

**Variant-selective stereopure oligonucleotides protect against pathologies associated with C9orf72-repeat expansion in preclinical models**  
- identifier: `PMID 33558503 / PMCID PMC7870851 / DOI 10.1038/s41467-021-21112-8, Nature Communications 2021`
- gap closed: H3 is the project's worst field: 41/41 human compounds have no purity or characterisation value and the only 3 real values sit on rat-only constructs. This paper carries LC-HRMS plus reversed-phase HPLC characterisation traces for every oligonucleotide in a CNS ASO panel (Supplementary Fig. 10), with the full analytical method written out (column, particle size, dimensions, HFIP/TEA buffer). Supplementary Table 1 adds sequence plus chemistry for the whole panel (H2/H1), the Source data file supplies raw continuous values instead of binaries (H5), and the CC BY licence makes it redistributable without restriction - which is the H6 property the project needs, since its only sequence-bearing source today is CC BY-NC-ND and flagged summary_stat_only. Human system: C9-ALS patient iPSC-derived neurons, closing part of the zero-human-in-vitro gap. Programme relevance: these are the preclinical constructs behind WVE-004, dosed intrathecally in humans in FOCUS-C9 / NCT04931862, which the repository's own backlog lists but no held source covers.
- extract: Supplementary Table 1 - sequence and chemistry of all oligonucleotides (the H2/H1 payload). Supplementary Fig. 10 - the LC-HRMS and reversed-phase HPLC characterisation per oligonucleotide (the H3 payload; record the method verbatim: Poroshell 120 EC-C18, 2.7 um, 2.1 x 50 mm, Agilent; Buffer A 200 mM HFIP + 8 mM TEA). The 'Source data file' - per-point raw values behind the figures, including the C9-ALS iPSC-derived neuron dose-responses (H5). Also Supplementary Fig. 1a-c for the Cos-7 luciferase primary screen if non-human in-vitro comparators are wanted.
- access: OPEN — Europe PMC reports isOpenAccess Y and licence 'cc by'. Full text XML returned HTTP 200 from the Europe PMC REST API with no login. I did not successfully download the supplementary bundle itself (my Europe PMC supplementaryFiles call for this PMCID returned an empty body within the timeout), so the supplement should be pulled from the Nature Communications article page or re-tried against Europe PMC.
- worth: HIGH — The only lead I verified that states, in its own Methods, that it characterised every construct by LC-HRMS and HPLC, under a CC BY licence, with a source-data file. That combination serves H3, H2, H5 and H6 simultaneously and is the single best answer to 'we have 3 purity values in 3,034 rows'.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results for GENERATION HD1 (tominersen, NCT03761849): the ventricle-volume outcome table, the per-arm hydrocephalus SAE row, and the two posted large documents**  
- identifier: `NCT03761849, results posted (hasResults = true); posted documents Prot_000.pdf (2021-04-02) and SAP_001.pdf (2022-05-12)`
- gap closed: H5: data/_ctgov_outcome_measurements.csv holds 21 rows total, 18 from NCT02519036 (n_at_risk 3-12) and 2 from NCT03342053 - and ZERO from this 899-participant trial, whose posted results carry ventricle volume in mL by arm. That is roughly a 10-fold increase in continuous ventricular-volume evidence for the endpoint, in the only placebo-controlled setting available. H4: the Q16W arm is a clean clinical negative in the exact shape the brief specifies - exact sequence held (HYD-OLG-0044 tominersen, CUCAGTAACATTGACACCAC, 5-10-5, 20 per-position rows already in modifications.csv), human exposure, stated dose and dosing interval and duration to week 69, explicit prespecified organ monitoring (structural MRI brain/ventricle volume as a registered secondary outcome, with the schedule in the posted protocol), explicit outcome (0 hydrocephalus), and a traceable denominator of 264. The 2/263 Q8W arm is the matched positive. It also closes the 'numerical dose absent in 1332/1332 human rows' finding for these arms, and it contradicts the current register row, which marks this trial NON_SYSTEMATIC_ASSESSMENT although ventricular volume was a registered, measured secondary endpoint.
- extract: (1) resultsSection.outcomeMeasuresModule, the outcome measure titled 'Change From Baseline in Whole and Regional Brain Volumes...' - every class whose title contains 'Ventricle Volume (mL)', all timepoints not just Week 13, with each class's per-group measurement values, spread and denominators. (2) The serious-AE rows 'Hydrocephalus', 'Meningitis aseptic', 'Intracranial hypotension' with their six per-group numerator/denominator pairs (these may partly duplicate the 24 AE rows already held from this NCT - de-duplicate against data/_ctgov_measurements.csv before adding). (3) The two posted large documents, https://cdn.clinicaltrials.gov/large-docs/49/NCT03761849/Prot_000.pdf and SAP_001.pdf, for the prespecified MRI acquisition schedule and the hydrocephalus monitoring / dose-interruption rules - this is the document that converts 'absence from an AE table' into 'systematically monitored', which is the H4 requirement the project has never satisfied.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved from the ClinicalTrials.gov API v2 over plain HTTPS, HTTP 200, no login. US Government work / public domain, same as the 150-plus NCT rows already in data/sources.csv.
- worth: HIGH — The brief calls H4 the highest-value find available and says there are zero in the whole project. This is the closest verified instance I can see anywhere for this endpoint, because the sequence is already held, the monitoring was registered, and the denominators are posted. Flagged honestly: the NCT is already a source_id, so this is an unextracted table inside a held source, not a new acquisition - but the table genuinely is not in data/.

**WO2017015555A1 - Oligonucleotide compositions and methods thereof (Wave Life Sciences; names WVE120101 and the HTT SNPs)**  
- identifier: `WO2017015555A1, applicant Wave Life Sciences Pte Ltd, priority 2015-07-22, published 2017-01-26`
- gap closed: HYD-OLG-0052 WVE-120101 and HYD-OLG-0053 WVE-120102 are both route=intrathecal_lumbar with sequence NOT_REPORTED and modification_pattern NOT_REPORTED, and their trials (NCT03225833, NCT03225846) are already held sources contributing human outcome rows with no molecule behind them. This patent is the only document I retrieved that names WVE120101 by its clinical code alongside sequence tables, so it is the realistic route to the sequence and the wing-core-wing 2'-OMe / phosphorothioate stereochemistry for the two PRECISION-HD compounds. The repository's own completeness critics flag that 'Patent literature was never searched' and the backlog holds only two patents, neither from Wave.

Specifically search the tables for the identifiers WVE120101, WV-1092, WV-2603, WV-2595 and for the rs362331 and rs362307 target sections. Note that the family likely includes a US equivalent with a cleaner printed sequence listing; resolve the family before extracting, so the citation names the document actually read.
- access: OPEN — The Google Patents page for WO2017015555A1 was retrieved and read with no login or payment; it offers a PDF download. WIPO-published international applications are public documents. I did not observe any price or access requirement.
- worth: HIGH — Patents are the named under-searched class here and are already proven productive on this project. This one is verified to exist, verified to name a roster compound by clinical code, and verified to reference sequence tables. Marked HIGH for H2 relevance but with the honest caveat that I have not seen inside the tables, so the per-position stereochemistry is expected, not confirmed.

**Preclinical evaluation of WVE-004, an investigational stereopure oligonucleotide for the treatment of C9orf72-associated ALS/FTD**  
- identifier: `PMID 35592494 / PMCID PMC9092894 / DOI 10.1016/j.omtn.2022.04.007, Molecular Therapy Nucleic Acids 2022`
- gap closed: Supplies human-system in-vitro rows for an intrathecally administered, human-dosed CNS ASO (WVE-004, FOCUS-C9 / NCT04931862, which the repository's backlog identifies but no held source covers): patient iPSC-derived ALS motor neurons, free-uptake exposure, concentration series to 10 uM, with IC50 values - raw continuous human-cell values against the endpoint's 0 human in-vitro rows. Pairs with the Nature Communications 2021 paper above, which does disclose the panel's sequences and chemistry, to give the human-to-animal bridge for the same programme.
- extract: Document S1 (Figures S1-S5) plus the article's Figure 1/2 panels for the iPSC-neuron dose-response curves and the 9 IC50 values with their units and replicate counts. Check Document S2 for whether any oligonucleotide sequence or chemistry table is present at all - if it is not, record WVE-004's sequence as genuinely undisclosed in this source rather than leaving the field ambiguous, and look to the Nature Communications 2021 Supplementary Table 1 for the sibling constructs instead.
- access: OPEN — Europe PMC reports isOpenAccess Y, licence 'cc by-nc-nd'; full text XML returned HTTP 200 from the Europe PMC REST API with no login. NC/ND terms, so handle as redistribution=summary_stat_only like the existing CC BY-NC-ND source in the repository.
- worth: MEDIUM — MEDIUM not HIGH precisely because I could not verify sequence disclosure, and H1 requires the sequence. It still earns a place because it is verified human-system in-vitro data with continuous readouts for an intrathecal human-dosed ASO, which the endpoint has none of.

**Health Canada Summary Basis of Decision for Spinraza (nusinersen), with its Post-Authorization Activity Table**  
- identifier: `Health Canada Drug and Health Products Portal resource SBD00361; DIN 02465663, 2.4 mg/mL nusinersen solution, intrathecal administration; page updated 2025-06-16`
- gap closed: Partially addresses the 41/41-no-purity-or-characterisation gap from a regulator the repository has never used - no Health Canada source of any kind is in data/sources.csv or notes/source_backlog.csv, which lists only FDA, EMA, MHRA and PMDA. The '7.3 Quality Basis for Decision' section is the Canadian analogue of the EPAR quality section. Its PAAT also supplies the dated safety-supplement timeline that the repository's own completeness critics asked for ('no time-resolved label history'), with Canadian control numbers and decision dates as a third jurisdiction alongside the US SPL and EU SmPC.
- extract: Section 7.3 'Quality Basis for Decision' in full, plus sections 7.1/7.2 (non-clinical and clinical basis) for the Canadian adjudication wording on intrathecal administration risks, plus the complete Post-Authorization Activity Table rows (activity/submission type, control number, date submitted, decision and date, summary of activities). Record honestly whether any NUMERIC purity or impurity limit appears - the wording I read is narrative ('meet the approved specifications') and may carry no number, in which case this contributes an analytical-identity/characterisation statement rather than a purity value.
- access: OPEN — curl GET returned HTTP 200, 92,039 bytes, no login, on 2026-10-03. Government of Canada publication. Note: WebFetch on the same URL returned HTTP 403, so use a direct GET. I did not read a licence statement on the page, so record the terms as unverified rather than asserting Crown open licence.

Still worth a Rocksteady hour: it is openly retrievable, it is a jurisdiction the project has entirely missed, and it doubles as the dated label-history source the critics requested.

**TGA Australian Public Assessment Report (AusPAR) for nusinersen (Spinraza), Biogen Australia, August 2018, with its separate Clinical Evaluation Report**

- gap closed: The AusPAR Quality findings section is one of the few public places that prints drug-substance purity and impurity specifications and analytical identity methods for an approved oligonucleotide, which is the 41/41 gap. The companion Clinical Evaluation Report carries the Australian evaluator's per-trial adverse-event tables with denominators, which is the H4 shape. No TGA source appears anywhere in data/sources.csv or notes/source_backlog.csv, and the backlog's critics name regulatory assessment reports as the single highest-value missing modality - this is a fourth jurisdiction they did not consider.
- extract: From auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813.pdf: the 'Quality findings' section in full - the drug substance subsection (specification table, purity and related-substances limits, analytical methods such as anion-exchange or capillary gel electrophoresis and mass spectrometry), the drug product subsection, and 'Quality summary and conclusions'. From auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813-cer.pdf: the evaluator's safety tables with per-arm denominators and any discussion of hydrocephalus, communicating hydrocephalus or ventricular events. Also record the TGA copyright/licence statement shown on the page, since Commonwealth material is often CC BY but I did not verify it here.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — I could not open it from this environment. curl to the PDF failed twice: 'HTTP/2 stream 1 was not closed cleanly: INTERNAL_ERROR' and then, over HTTP/1.1, 'Empty reply from server'. WebFetch on both the PDF and on https://www.tga.gov.au/resources/auspar/auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium returned HTTP 503 Service Unavailable. No price, purchase page or access requirement was displayed at any point - this is a technical block from this network, NOT evidence of a paywall, and must not be recorded as one.
- worth: MEDIUM — MEDIUM: the content class is exactly what H3 needs and the document is verified to exist with exact filenames, but I could not open it, so neither the purity numbers nor the licence are confirmed. A Rocksteady session on a different network may well get straight in.

**Impact of guanidine-containing backbone linkages on stereopure antisense oligonucleotides in the CNS**  
- identifier: `PMID 35106589 / PMCID PMC9177980 / DOI 10.1093/nar/gkac037, Nucleic Acids Research 2022`
- gap closed: This is the methods paper the WVE-003 and WVE-004 papers both cite for their PN (phosphoryl guanidine) backbone chemistry and Rp/Sp stereochemistry conventions. It is the document that lets a curator translate the '^ Rp = red / Sp = blue' notation in those papers into unambiguous per-position linkage rows, which the repository's modifications.csv schema requires (linkage_3prime, with stereochemistry where specified). Without it, the stereopure constructs can be entered with a sequence but not with defensible per-linkage chemistry.
- extract: The oligonucleotide table(s) giving per-position sugar, base and linkage chemistry with Rp/Sp and PN assignments for the CNS ASO series, plus the notation key, as the controlled vocabulary for entering stereopure constructs into data/modifications.csv.
- access: OPEN — Europe PMC reports isOpenAccess Y, licence 'cc by-nc'. I did not fetch the article body or supplement, so I have not confirmed the supplement is openly downloadable.
- worth: LOW — LOW on its own - it is enabling infrastructure for the two Wave leads above rather than a source of endpoint rows - but cheap, open, and it removes the main obstacle to recording stereopure chemistry correctly instead of as NOT_REPORTED.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- FDA Chemistry / Product Quality reviews, which were my primary H3 target. accessdata.fda.gov blocks this environment: the known-good 209531Orig1s000PharmR.pdf returned 'HTTP/2 302, location: /apology_objects/abuse-detection-apology.html' from AkamaiGHost, and every probed filename then returned 404 with a 420-byte body. The 209531Orig1s000TOC.html table of contents is JavaScript-rendered and WebFetch saw only 'Loading...' placeholders. So I could neither confirm nor rule out that a Chemistry/Product Quality review exists for NDA 209531 or NDA 215887, and I am NOT proposing one. This is a technical block, not a paywall - there is ~17 FDA-approved-oligo worth of CMC material behind it and it is worth one retry from a different network.
- Google Patents bulk search (xhr/query returned HTTP 503 with a Google 'Sorry...' interstitial) and PatentsView (api.patentsview.org 301; search.patentsview.org refused by the egress proxy, 'connect_rejected (organization policy)'). I therefore could not run a systematic patent sweep. The only patent I verified is WO2017015555A1, reached through a domain-restricted web search. Ionis/Biogen patent families for BIIB105 (ATXN2) and the Wave family member printing a clean US sequence listing were not reached.
- The contents of the WO2017015555A1 sequence tables. I confirmed the patent names WVE120101 and references Tables N1A, N2A, N3A, N4A and 8 as containing oligonucleotide sequences, but the retrieved excerpt did not include those tables, so the per-linkage Sp/Rp stereochemistry inside them is expected, not seen.
- TGA. Every route failed: curl to the AusPAR PDF gave 'HTTP/2 stream 1 was not closed cleanly: INTERNAL_ERROR' then, over HTTP/1.1, 'Empty reply from server'; WebFetch on the PDF and on the AusPAR landing page both returned HTTP 503. No price or access requirement was ever displayed, so the access status is unknown, not paywalled, and the licence is unverified.
- HYD-OLG-0047 valeriasen. It is route=intrathecal_lumbar with no sequence, and I could not place it. It does not appear in any of Recommended INN Lists 60-94, which I grepped exhaustively. A targeted web search for the name returned nothing about the molecule. I did not search the WHO PROPOSED INN lists (plNNN.pdf), which is where it most likely sits, nor the WHO MedNet INN database. This remains open and is a clean next step.
- HYD-OLG-0006 BIIB105. ClinicalTrials.gov NCT04494256 lists the intervention simply as 'BIIB105' with no otherNames, so I could not resolve it to an INN and therefore could not reach its chemistry. Its results ARE posted (hasResults true) and the NCT is already a held source, so its AE table may already be extracted; I did not check that.
- No human choroid plexus or human ependymal oligonucleotide study was found, despite five targeted searches. The HIBCPP human choroid-plexus-papilloma line does appear with siRNA, but only in manganese/zinc transporter work (ZIP8/ZIP14, e.g. PMC7464652, PMC9352541) that reports no oligonucleotide sequences and no CSF-dynamics or ventricular endpoint; I am not proposing those. 'Choroid plexus organoid' plus ASO/siRNA returned no primary study at all. If a human choroid plexus ASO/siRNA paper exists, I did not find it, and the human in-vitro bridge I am proposing instead is iPSC-derived neurons, not choroid plexus epithelium - a curator should decide whether that satisfies the challenge priority.
- PMID 42724647 / PMC13560553, 'Safety and toxicity assessment of antisense oligonucleotides in brain-relevant human cellular models' (Mol Ther Nucleic Acids 2026, CC BY). I retrieved and read it in full and am DELIBERATELY NOT proposing it: it is a methods review. Its eight tables are generic platform/assay comparisons with no per-oligo sequences, no chemistry, no purity values and no measurements, so it supplies none of H1-H6. It is a useful navigation aid for finding human in-vitro ASO toxicity primary studies and nothing more - recorded here rather than dressed up as a lead.
- Not searched at all this round, and each could plausibly carry something: EudraCT and the EU CTIS, jRCT and other Japanese registries, PMDA (the backlog already holds the RMP, package insert and a new-indication review report, but not the original-approval review report with its quality section), WHO ICTRP, pharmacopoeial monographs (USP/Ph.Eur./JP oligonucleotide monographs - I never checked whether any exists), GEO, SRA, PRIDE, ProteomeXchange, BioStudies/ArrayExpress, Dryad, OSF, Figshare, and doctoral theses. I ran only two Zenodo queries and both returned nothing on-point.
- Licence verification for the WHO INN lists. The repository records 'reuse terms not established in this session' for its existing WHO_INN_List_* rows and I did not establish them either, so my INN leads carry that same unresolved wording rather than an asserted open licence.
- The GENERATION HD1 posted ventricle-volume table beyond Week 13. I read the Week 13 class values (1.537, 3.785, 2.011 mL) and confirmed sibling caudate and whole-brain classes, but I did not enumerate every timepoint, every group mapping or the spread/denominator fields, and I did not open Prot_000.pdf or SAP_001.pdf to confirm what the MRI monitoring schedule actually says. The H4 claim therefore rests on the registered secondary outcome plus the posted AE denominators, and needs the protocol read to be fully adjudicated.

### XCUT-regulatory-CMC — 17 leads

**Gaps targeted:** 1. PURITY IS EMPTY AND THE REPO ASSERTS IT IS UNOBTAINABLE. toxicity/kidney/data/oligos.csv: 65 rows, purity_pct = "TBD" on 65/65, purity_method = "TBD" on 65/65. The generator toxicity/kidney/scripts/add_identity_characterization.py stamps every notes field with "purity_not_published_by_any_source_verified_not_assumed" and its docstring says "Labels and trial papers do not publish per-batch purity either... this cannot be closed by further curation." That was tested against in-repo patents and labels ONLY - never against EMA assessment-report quality sections, PMDA review reports or FDA Product Quality Reviews. It is too strong: I retrieved purity METHODS for 11 held drugs and surviving numeric purity/endotoxin LIMITS for two.  2. SAME HOLE ON PEER BRANCHES. coagulopathy branch oligos.csv: 218 rows, purity_pct = "NOT_REPORTED" on 218/218, purity_method "NOT_REPORTED" on ~205/218 (exceptions are vendor "HPLC grade" strings and PAGE, no numbers). BEEBOP_RESEARCH_ROUND_2026-10-02.md records 0/65 kidney, 0/34 thrombocytopenia, 0/38 coagulopathy, 0/21 nervous, 0/41 hydrocephalus purity values.  3. TWO HELD MOLECULES HAVE NO SEQUENCE AND NO CHEMISTRY. COG-OLG120 imetelstat and COG-OLG12

- gap closed: The single best-populated purity record available for any held oligo. Fills purity_method, identity_confirmation AND two numeric characterization values (NMT 0.5% unidentified / NMT 1.0% unqualified impurity peaks; 100 EU/ml endotoxin) for OLG003 givosiran, currently purity_pct=TBD / purity_method=TBD. Also supplies the 0.10% impurity identification threshold, i.e. the implied full-length purity floor. Directly falsifies the add_identity_characterization.py claim that no source publishes purity.
- extract: Section 2.2.2 Active Substance specification paragraph (PDF p.18) and section 2.2.3 Finished Medicinal Product specification + acceptance-criteria paragraphs (PDF pp.21-23), plus the CQA table on PDF p.20 which states verbatim "Purity Critical Decreased purity will lead to increased ... impurities. It may result in off-target effects and have impact to safety." Pull as three fields: purity_method, identity_confirmation, endotoxin_limit.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200 from www.ema.europa.eu, 2,547,006 bytes. Licence verbatim from the document footer: "Reproduction is authorised provided the source is acknowledged."
- worth: HIGH — Only document retrieved this session that supplies purity method, identity method, a numeric impurity limit AND a numeric endotoxin limit for a molecule the project already holds.

**Rytelo (imetelstat) - EMA CHMP assessment report; section 2.2.2 general information (structure, pp.13-15) and specification (p.17)**  
- identifier: `EMA/13310/2025, Procedure EMEA/H/C/006105/0000, 12 December 2024, 116 pp.`
- gap closed: COG-OLG120 imetelstat is held with an EMPTY sequence field and identity_confirmation = NOT_REPORTED. This document gives the complete 13-base sequence (TAGGGTTAGACAA), the exact per-position linkage chemistry (12 x N3'->P5'-thio-phosphoramidate plus one 5'-terminal phosphorothioate), the conjugate (palmitoyl via aminoglycerol linker), the molecular formula and the full specification method list. Closes sequence + modification positions + purity method + identity method for one held molecule in one pass.
- extract: PDF pp.14-15 (chemical name, formula, stereochemistry paragraph, Figure 1 molecular structure) and the drug-substance/finished-product specification paragraph on PDF p.17. Note also p.17: "There appears to be an overall trend towards higher purity with later process versions" - batch-level purity is discussed but the values are deleted.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved, 2,964,174 bytes, 116 pp. EMA footer licence: "Reproduction is authorised provided the source is acknowledged."
- worth: HIGH — Takes a held row from zero chemistry to full per-position chemistry. Imetelstat's N3'->P5'-thio-phosphoramidate backbone exists nowhere else in the dataset's chemistry vocabulary, so this row is also a chemistry-class outlier worth having right.

**Tryngolza (olezarsen) - EMA CHMP assessment report; clinical safety AE tables (pp.140-176) and section 2.2 quality (pp.13-19)**  
- identifier: `EMA/CHMP/216788/2025, Procedure EMEA/H/C/006477/0000, 24 July 2025, 257 pp.`
- gap closed: The strongest H4 candidate I found, and the project currently has ZERO. Olezarsen is the APOC3 ASO in the same target class and same sponsor as volanesorsen, which is a KNOWN thrombocytopenia positive already in the dataset (OLG005). This report gives the matched negative: exact human exposure across 5 studies, pre-specified platelet stopping rules and a platelet-count eligibility floor (explicit monitoring), AE tables with per-arm n (%) so denominators are recoverable, placebo comparator columns, and an explicit negative outcome statement. It simultaneously serves H5 (Figure 12 mean platelet counts and percent change over time, continuous values where the project holds curator binaries) and H3 (purity by IP-HPLC-UV-MS, NMT 1.0% unspecified oligonucleotide impurities) for COG-OLG121 / COG-OLG217, which are purity_pct = NOT_REPORTED with an empty sequence field.
- extract: (1) The TEAE-by-arm table starting PDF p.153 and repeated at pp.158 and 162 - take the column header row with the group Ns first, it is on the page before the body rows; do not take the percentages without it. (2) The platelet narrative on pp.144-147 and 169-176, including Figure 12 on p.176. (3) The stopping-rule and eligibility text on pp.76-77. (4) The active substance specification paragraph on p.14 and the finished-product specification on p.19. Flag that pp.140 and 147 compare against volanesorsen explicitly - that is the sequence-matched positive control.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200 from www.ema.europa.eu/en/documents/assessment-report/tryngolza-epar-public-assessment-report_en.pdf, 6,472,720 bytes, 257 pp. EMA licence: "Reproduction is authorised provided the source is acknowledged."
- worth: HIGH — H4 is named as the highest-value find available and there are zero in the project. This is a sequence-matched clean negative against a held positive, in an openly reusable document, with the monitoring and denominators the H4 definition requires. The same PDF also closes purity method and sequence for two held rows.

**Spinraza (nusinersen) - EMA CHMP assessment report; section 2.2 Quality, active substance specification (PDF p.15) and finished product (p.18)**  
- identifier: `EMA/289068/2017, Procedure EMEA/H/C/004312/0000, 21 April 2017, 117 pp.`
- gap closed: This is the literal Phase 2 field, named as such by a regulator: "purity (Full Length n)" with its method, IP-HPLC-UV-MS, for OLG004 nusinersen (and COG-OLG054), currently purity_method = TBD / NOT_REPORTED. Identity confirmation is given at two levels - most abundant mass by IP-HPLC-UV-MS and full sequencing by IP-HPLC-TOF-MS - which lets identity_confirmation carry an actual analytical method instead of the design_source heuristic the repo currently uses. Note for the curator: the numeric acceptance criteria themselves are removed - the document says limits were "revised by the applicant" without printing them. Record purity_method and identity_confirmation; leave purity_pct explicitly absent with this document as the evidence that the value is withheld rather than unmeasured.

- worth: HIGH — Nusinersen is held on at least three branches. The phrase "purity (Full Length n)" is the exact attribute the challenge asks for, and this is the cleanest regulator-printed instance of it.

**US 11,312,963 B2 - "Compositions and methods for inhibiting TIGIT gene expression" (Synerk Inc); the full four-field CMC boilerplate with real numbers**  
- identifier: `US11312963B2, granted 26 April 2022, assignee Synerk Inc`
- gap closed: The only document I retrieved that states all four requested fields with numbers in one place: numeric full-length purity (95-98%), the purity method (ion-exchange HPLC), the identity method (MALDI-TOF MS), and a numeric endotoxin level (<=0.1 EU/mg). Its primary value is as the PROOF AND THE SEARCH KEY: the phrase "purity of full-length oligonucleotides" co-occurring with "endotoxin" is a reusable Google Patents query that surfaces this CMC boilerplate across patent families, which is how numeric purity can be recovered at scale for research-grade constructs. It also gives the repo its first worked example of what a filled purity row looks like.
- extract: The Materials/Methods example paragraph carrying the three quoted sentences, plus the SEQ ID listing. CRITICAL CAVEAT, and the reason this must not be ingested naively: the 95-98% and <=0.1 EU/mg figures are PANEL-LEVEL statements covering all oligonucleotides in the examples, not per-construct values. RESEARCH_SOURCE_COMPENDIUM.md already rules that "one group-level characterization cannot be spread across constructs". Record it as a panel-level range with the panel as the unit, or the dataset gains 52 fabricated per-oligo values.
- access: OPEN — Full text served by Google Patents without any paywall or login; also listed at image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/11312963. US patent text, no price or access requirement observed.
- worth: HIGH — Numerically the richest single hit, and the search key it yields is worth more than the document. Downgraded from a per-row win because the figures are panel-level and the sequences lack per-position chemistry.

**FDA Product Quality Review(s) / Chemistry Review(s) in Drug Approval Packages - retrieved for four oligo NDAs and found to be numerically redacted**  
- identifier: `NDA 211172Orig1s000 PRODUCT QUALITY REVIEW(S) (inotersen); NDA 212154Orig1s000 PRODUCT QUALITY REVIEW(S) (viltolarsen); NDA 203568Orig1s000 CHEMISTRY REVIEW(S) (mipomersen); NDA 217388Orig1s000 INTEGRATED REVIEW (eplontersen)`
- gap closed: Partially closes the endotoxin and identity METHOD fields (USP <85>, LAL kinetic chromogenic, orthogonal identity tests) and names the registration batch numbers 017G16A / 018G16A / 019G16A for inotersen, which is real tested-material provenance. It does NOT close purity_pct for any drug. Reporting this is the point: SOURCES_TO_ACQUIRE.md section C sends a Rocksteady to Drugs@FDA for nine NDAs, and the chemistry half of that trip returns no numbers. Spend the day on EMA and PMDA instead.

From 212154: "orthogonal identity tests, assay, related substances, pH, residual solvents, bioburden and bacterial endotoxins". Record the (b)(4) redaction explicitly as the reason purity_pct is absent - that converts an unexplained blank into a sourced, documented withholding, which is what the challenge's missingness requirement actually asks for.
- access: OPEN — Files present locally and converted. The repository's own retrieval note at complement-activation/research-staging/working/REGISTER_regulatory_draft.md states verbatim: "accessdata.fda.gov returns 404 to a default user agent and 200 to a browser user agent. Not a paywall. A session that does not know this will wrongly log FDA reviews as unavailable." Document URLs of this form confirmed in search results, e.g. accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2024/217388Orig1s000IntegratedR.pdf and .../nda/2018/211172Orig1s000Approv.pdf. US Government work; no price or access requirement observed.

**AusPAR - Australian Public Assessment Report for nusinersen (heptadecasodium), Spinraza, Biogen Australia**  
- identifier: `TGA AusPAR, file auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813.pdf, August 2018; a companion Clinical Evaluation Report exists as auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813-cer.docx`

AusPAR Quality findings sections are structured around drug substance specification and analytical procedures. UNVERIFIED whether numeric acceptance criteria survive TGA redaction - that is the whole question and I could not answer it.
- extract: The "Quality findings" section - drug substance specification, analytical procedures, and any numeric acceptance criteria or endotoxin limit. Then compare against the EMA (EMA/289068/2017 p.15) and PMDA (000228334) nusinersen specifications; three regulators on one molecule is the cleanest available test of whether purity numbers survive anywhere in the public record.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — Three retrieval attempts all failed on transport, not on payment: curl over HTTP/2 returned "curl: (92) HTTP/2 stream 1 was not closed cleanly: INTERNAL_ERROR"; curl --http1.1 returned "curl: (52) Empty reply from server"; WebFetch returned "HTTP 503 Service Unavailable". No price, purchase page or access requirement was displayed. This is a blocked transfer from this environment, NOT evidence of a paywall.
- worth: MEDIUM — Honest MEDIUM. The document is confirmed to exist and the class is unsearched by this project, but I could not open it, so its purity content is a hypothesis. Worth one retry from a session with different network egress; not worth more than that.

**SwissPAR - Swiss Public Assessment Report, WAINZUA (eplontersen), Swissmedic**  
- identifier: `Swissmedic SwissPAR, authorisation 69332, WAINZUA, dated 09.05.2025, 48 pp.`
- gap closed: Thin. It does NOT close purity for eplontersen (OLG018, COG-OLG218). What it does give, in one citable regulator sentence, is the backbone distinction between eplontersen (mixed phosphorothioate AND phosphodiester) and inotersen (phosphorothioate only) despite an identical base sequence - a worked example of the leakage hazard RESEARCH_SOURCE_COMPENDIUM.md warns about, and directly relevant because OLG001 and OLG018 are exactly that pair in the kidney table, with ps_count 19 recorded for inotersen.
- extract: Section 4.1 Drug substance (PDF pp.6-7) and the eplontersen/inotersen backbone comparison on PDF p.8. Reported mainly so the next session does not repeat the search: SwissPARs summarise that specifications exist without naming the methods, so the class is NOT a purity source. I also retrieved the Tegsedi SwissPAR summary (2 pp., zero purity/HPLC/endotoxin occurrences), which confirms the pattern.
- access: OPEN — HTTP 200 from www.swissmedic.ch, 2,095,414 bytes, 48 pp. of English text.
- worth: LOW — Deliberately LOW and reported as a negative result on a whole source class, plus one genuinely useful chemistry sentence. A verified dead end documented is cheaper than the same dead end walked twice.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- TGA AusPAR could not be opened from this environment. Three independent routes failed on transport: curl HTTP/2 gave "stream 1 was not closed cleanly: INTERNAL_ERROR", curl --http1.1 gave "Empty reply from server", WebFetch gave HTTP 503. No price or access requirement was shown. The nusinersen AusPAR's Quality findings content is therefore UNVERIFIED - I am claiming the document exists, not what is in it.
- Rivfloza (nedosiran), Wainua (eplontersen) and Vitravene (fomivirsen) returned HTTP 404 at the standard EMA EPAR assessment-report URL path. I did not establish whether these products have an EU marketing authorisation under a different brand name, no EPAR, or an EPAR at a non-standard path. Do not record these as non-existent on my evidence.
- No individual USP or Ph. Eur. monograph for any named oligonucleotide was located. A retrieved search result additionally reported that no purity limits are stated in the Ph. Eur. monograph "Substances for pharmaceutical use" for synthetic oligonucleotides. I did not read either pharmacopoeia directly and did not verify that statement against the primary text, so this is a negative lead, not a settled finding - but it is enough to deprioritise the pharmacopoeial route that my brief listed as a target.
- Health Canada Summary Basis of Decision and Regulatory Decision Summary documents surfaced in search results for Tegsedi (SBD00422, RDS00509) and Onpattro (SBD00446, RDS00567) on dhpp.hpfb-dgpsa.ca. I did not open any of them. This is a fifth regulator class, completely unsearched by this project, and it is the most obvious remaining lead for a numeric purity specification after EMA and PMDA.
- ClinicalTrials.gov, EudraCT, WHO ICTRP and jRCT posted-results sections were not searched at all this session. My brief names them as the natural H4 source class and I spent the session on the CMC lane instead. The olezarsen H4 lead above comes from a regulator's assessment of those trials, not from a registry results table, so the registry route remains open and unduplicated.
- PMDA Japanese-language full review reports (審査報告書) were not searched. The English translations I read have numeric specification cells replaced by asterisk runs; whether the Japanese originals are redacted identically is unknown and untested, and it is the single cheapest remaining test of whether any regulator publishes the number.
- No numeric full-length purity percentage was found for ANY approved oligonucleotide therapeutic. State this plainly to the lead scientist rather than letting it be inferred. All three major regulators strip the value by a different mechanism - FDA under FOIA exemption (b)(4), EMA as "information of a commercially confidential nature deleted", PMDA by asterisk substitution. The two numeric exceptions I did verify are Givlaari's impurity-peak and endotoxin limits (NMT 0.5% / NMT 1.0% / 100 EU/ml) and Tryngolza's NMT 1.0% unspecified oligonucleotide impurities, which are acceptance LIMITS, not measured batch purity; and PMDA's nusinersen 4.1-6.7%, which belongs to deliberately impurity-enriched toxicology batches and is not a product release value. The realistic Phase 2 outcome for approved drugs is a fully populated, source-cited purity_method and identity_confirmation plus a documented reason purity_pct is withheld - which satisfies the "record missingness explicitly" requirement far better than the current unexplained TBD. Numeric purity at scale will have to come from research-grade constructs via patent and paper methods sections, and the US11312963B2 search key above is the entry point I verified for that.
- I did not pursue H1 at all. Every molecule I checked as a possible new entrant - olezarsen, imetelstat, donidalorsen, eplontersen - turned out to be already held on the coagulopathy branch. My endpoint produced no new molecule count, and I would rather say so than pad the list.
- The regulatory PDFs I read sit in this session's scratchpad, not in the repository. Nothing has been added to the repo and no row was changed. Several were already on disk from a sibling session; I verified each document's own header and page count before quoting it rather than trusting the filename, because the project has already been damaged once by a PDF whose label did not match its contents.

Separately, toxicity/cross-cutting.md §2 already records that PADP.md:103-104 claims the dataset "has already been released under an irrevocable CC-BY 4.0 license" — on the branch where a LICENSE does exist, but under a kidney-specific title. (3) THE ACTUAL EXPOSURE IS A COLUMN, NOT A CLASS. Every one of the 2,685 coa
