**European Medicines Agency Legal notice — the reuse term that reclassifies every EMA-derived row in the project**  
- identifier: `https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/about-us/legal-notice ; corroborated by the notice printed on EPAR page 1 itself, e.g. Onpattro EMA/554262/2018 and Qalsody EMA/276404/2024`
- gap closed: Directly reclassifies 64 coagulopathy measurement rows now blocked or unresolved: COG-S076 Waylivra EPAR 17, COG-S078 Waylivra Annex I SmPC 2, COG-S079 Tegsedi EPAR 11, COG-S082 Dawnzera EPAR 21, COG-S083 Amvuttra EPAR 4 (all tagged publisher_restricted) and COG-S091 Kynamro EMA/305826/2013 2 (tagged unresolved). Separately it replaces a wrong legal basis on 216 thrombocytopenia rows: rights_audit.csv justifies eight EMA sources with "US Government work; no copyright attaches" (Waylivra EPAR 117, Tegsedi EPAR 81, Waylivra SmPC 9, Oxlumo 3, Qalsody 3, Spinraza 1, Onpattro 1, Amvuttra 1). The conclusion survives; the stated basis does not, and a reviewer reading that cell will not trust the rest of the audit.
- extract: No data pull. Two file edits. (a) toxicity/coagulopathy/data/sources.csv: set redistribution on COG-S076, S078, S079, S082, S083, S091 to an EMA-attribution class, and propagate to the matching rows of data/measurements.csv. Hold COG-S077 (Waylivra EU-RMP v3.0, CTD Module 1.8.2) restricted — an EU-RMP is applicant-authored, so the third-party exception in the legal notice plausibly bites; say so in the basis cell rather than guessing. (b) thrombocytopenia/curation/rights/rights_audit.csv: replace the basis string on the eight EMA rows with the EMA notice and its URL. Also add the notice as the licence basis in whatever LICENSE file the three unlicensed branches get.
- access: OPEN — EMA legal notice, quoted above: reproduction and distribution permitted for non-commercial AND commercial purposes with EMA acknowledged as source; third-party content excepted. Licence is a bespoke agency term, not a Creative Commons licence — record it as such and do not relabel it CC BY.
- worth: HIGH — Highest eligibility-per-effort item I found. Costs no acquisition and no new extraction, converts 64 blocked rows, repairs the legal basis under 216 more, and resolves a four-way contradiction in which the same publisher is classed publisher_restricted, unresolved, public_domain and CC_BY inside one project. It also unlocks leads 2 and 3 below, because it is what makes the EPAR Quality sections redistributable rather than merely readable.

**EMA Assessment report, Givlaari (givosiran) — Quality section 2.2, active-substance impurity characterisation and the 0.10% identification threshold**  
- identifier: `EMA/CHMP/70703/2020, Procedure No. EMEA/H/C/004775/0000, CHMP assessment report adopted 30 January 2020, 101 pages`
- gap closed: H3, the top-ranked mandatory field, where the project has 3 real values in 3,034 oligo rows. This gives, for givosiran: a published numeric impurity identification threshold (0.10% peak-area percent), the two orthogonal analytical methods used to establish identity (2-D denaturing AX-HPLC fractionation then IPRP-HPLC-MS; plus denaturing IPRP-HPLC-MS), the statement that impurities above that threshold were identified by structure, sequence or mass, and Full Length Product named as a critical quality attribute. It is the real-value answer to the dead end already recorded in characterization_regulatory_cmc.json, where both the FDA Chemistry Review and the inotersen EPAR were found to name parameters and publish no numbers. And because of lead 1 it arrives redistributable, so it serves H6 at the same time.
- extract: Section 2.2 "Quality aspects" — specifically 2.2.2 Active Substance, the impurity-characterisation paragraph on p.17, the abbreviation table on p.4 for the FLP/CQA definitions, and the specification discussion around p.24. Do not extract the commercially confidential deletions. File the 0.10% figure as an identification/reporting threshold, NOT as a purity specification or an assay limit — it is the threshold above which peaks were characterised, and mislabelling it would repeat exactly the kind of error this project has already been damaged by twice.
- access: OPEN — EMA document; licence as per lead 1 (reproduction and distribution for commercial and non-commercial purposes with EMA acknowledged). PDF retrieved and read in full with no payment, login or access condition encountered.
- worth: HIGH — H3 is the project's worst-populated mandatory field and this is a published number plus named methods for a held molecule, under a verified reuse term. `git grep` for EMA/CHMP/70703 and EMEA/H/C/004775 returns NOT HELD in amazing-galileo, coagulopathy, thrombocytopenia, CNS and k394sz; the only copy anywhere is a text dump in the hydrocephalus branch that was mined for adverse events, not chemistry. Actionable in one sitting.

**EMA Assessment reports already in the repository whose Quality sections were never mined — Onpattro (patisiran) and Qalsody (tofersen)**  
- identifier: `Onpattro: EMA/554262/2018, EMEA/H/C/004699/0000, adopted 26 July 2018, 188 pp. Qalsody: EMA/276404/2024, EMEA/H/C/006072, adopted 22 February 2024, 181 pp.`
- gap closed: H3 as characterisation and as batch provenance. Onpattro supplies a usable, citable oligonucleotide impurity taxonomy (N-x internal deletion species with N-1 named as most common; truncated/terminal-deletion failure sequences; the synthesis steps that generate them) plus the regulator-imposed basis on which duplex purity, sodium content and pH specifications were set (±3SD, TI approach rejected) — that is a controlled vocabulary the project can key an impurity field to instead of inventing one. Qalsody supplies batch-level provenance in exactly the shape characterization_regulatory_cmc.json said was missing: 13 active-substance batches split 9 non-clinical-tox/clinical/stability/process-development versus 4 process-validation/commercial/stability, plus purity/impurity data on 5 clinical or pilot batches. Honest limit: Qalsody states that the data were provided and that specifications were met in all batches; it does NOT reproduce the values or the lot identifiers. So this closes the batch-count and categorisation part of lot traceability and does not close the numeric-purity part.
- extract: Onpattro EMA/554262/2018: section 2.2 Quality aspects, the impurity-class discussion on pp.21-22 and the specification-setting paragraph on p.31. Qalsody EMA/276404/2024: section 2.4.1 Active Substance, the impurity classification on p.16 and the batch-analysis paragraph on p.17 (ending "...met for all attributes in all batches"). Record purity_pct as absent-with-reason, exactly as the thrombocytopenia CMC file already does for inotersen; do not let a batch count become a purity value.
- access: OPEN — Both documents retrieved and read with no payment, login or access condition. Reuse per lead 1; the Qalsody copy carries "© European Medicines Agency, 2024. Reproduction is authorised provided the source is acknowledged." on its own first page.
- worth: HIGH — Both documents are already in the project — Onpattro yielded ONE row out of 188 pages in thrombocytopenia, Qalsody is held by coagulopathy as COG-S098 and by thrombocytopenia for 3 rows. The acquisition cost is zero and the H3 content is sitting in sections nobody has opened. Lower than lead 2 only because Givlaari carries an actual number and these carry taxonomy and provenance.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, fitusiran ATLAS programme — public-domain replacement for the five CC BY-NC-ND fitusiran papers that coagulopathy depends on**  
- identifier: `NCT03417245 (ATLAS-A/B), NCT03417102 (ATLAS-INH), NCT03549871 (ATLAS-PPX). Retrieved via ClinicalTrials.gov API v2.`
- gap closed: H6 for the single largest restricted cluster in coagulopathy: COG-S027 (29 rows), S028 (29), S029 (14), S030 (18), S031 (24) are all fitusiran clinical papers tagged CC_BY_NC_ND — 114 rows that cannot ship. The registry carries the same class of outcome (thrombosis and haemorrhage event counts, antithrombin-regimen arms) as a US Government work. It also plugs a structural hole: coagulopathy holds ZERO ClinicalTrials.gov sources across 100 source rows, while thrombocytopenia already mines the registry for nine mipomersen trials, five oblimersen and fifteen PMO trials — the same open-licence seam, worked on one branch and untouched on another. H4-shaped because every cell carries an explicit denominator and a stated collection window. Honest caveat on H4: these are positive-signal arms, not a clean clinical negative, and the registry posts no sequence — so they do not by themselves make an H4. Honest caveat on H5: I checked the outcomeMeasuresModule for NCT03417245 and all 12 outcome measures are bleeding rates, quality-of-life scores and TEAE counts — there is no posted antithrombin or platelet continuous series, so treat H5 as partial (event counts with denominators, not raw continuous values).
- extract: resultsSection.adverseEventsModule for each of the three NCTs, taking organSystem, term, numAffected and numAtRisk per eventGroup, plus the frequencyThreshold and timeFrame fields so the ascertainment level is recorded. Pull it from the API, not by scraping the HTML. Then map each registry cell against the paper-derived rows it corroborates and mark the paper rows as citable-from-open-source rather than deleting them.
- access: OPEN — Retrieved over the public ClinicalTrials.gov v2 REST API with no key, login or payment. The project already treats ClinicalTrials.gov as public domain (NLM) in thrombocytopenia's acquisition_candidates.csv; I did not separately re-verify the NLM terms-of-use page in this pass, so carry that basis forward from the existing register rather than from me.
- worth: HIGH — 114 non-shippable rows get an open corroborating source, and it also imports a licence-clean practice from one branch to another that lacks it entirely. Verified down to individual numerators and denominators, which is unusual for a lead at proposal stage.

**ClinicalTrials.gov posted results, APPROACH volanesorsen trials — public-domain platelet rows with denominators, replacing closed-access clinical thrombocytopenia evidence**  
- identifier: `NCT02211209 (APPROACH, placebo-controlled) and NCT02658175 (APPROACH open-label extension). Retrieved via ClinicalTrials.gov API v2.`
- gap closed: H6 against the closed-access volanesorsen clinical paper that thrombocytopenia depends on for 47 rows (rights_audit.csv: PMID 31390500, doi:10.1056/NEJMoa1715944, "Volanesorsen and Triglyceride Levels in Familial Chylomicronemia Syndrome", rights_tier C_closed_access, no declared licence). The registry gives the same trial's platelet signal as a US Government work with both denominators intact and the ascertainment threshold stated. Volanesorsen is the marketed oligo with the most severe clinical platelet signal, so this is the restricted row-block with the highest scientific weight per row. H4-shaped on four of the six H4 criteria — human exposure, explicit platelet monitoring, explicit outcome, traceable denominator (33 and 33) — and short of H4 on the other two, because the registry posts no sequence and the dose/duration must come from the protocol section. Say that plainly; do not log it as the project's first H4.
- extract: resultsSection.adverseEventsModule for NCT02211209 and NCT02658175: the four platelet/haemorrhage terms above with numAffected/numAtRisk per arm, plus frequencyThreshold and timeFrame. Also pull protocolSection.armsInterventionsModule and the dosing text so the dose and duration fields can be populated from the same public-domain record. Cross-key to the existing 47 rows rather than replacing them.
- access: OPEN — Public ClinicalTrials.gov v2 REST API, no key or login. Public-domain basis carried forward from the project's own register, not independently re-verified by me in this pass.
- worth: HIGH — The restricted block it replaces is small (47 rows) but it is the clinically heaviest platelet evidence in the project, and an 11/33 versus 1/33 contrast with a stated 5% ascertainment threshold is directly usable. Cheap: one API call per trial. `git grep` shows NCT02211209 mentioned in four branches but the only registry sources in thrombocytopenia's rights audit are NCT00138658 and NCT00327340, so the posted results are not ingested.

**Europe PMC REST core API as the licence adjudicator for every PMCID the project holds — and one named misclassification it surfaces**  
- identifier: `https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/search?query=PMCID:<id>&resultType=core&format=json — fields isOpenAccess, license, authMan, inEPMC, inPMC`
- gap closed: Gives the rights audit a machine-checkable basis instead of a judgement call, and does two concrete things. First it CONFIRMS that publisher_restricted is correct for the four author-manuscript records: authMan Y plus isOpenAccess N means in PMC but outside the Open Access Subset, which is exactly why "it is in PMC" is not a licence. That retires a recurring argument. Second it FLAGS a misclassification: COG-S007 (PMC8808741, Soule et al., aptamer targeting factor V/Va, 6 oligos and 21 measurements) is tagged publisher_restricted in coagulopathy sources.csv, but Europe PMC returns isOpenAccess Y and authMan N for it — so 21 rows and 6 oligos may be shippable and are currently being withheld. Note honestly: the license field comes back null, so this is a reclassification CANDIDATE, not a decided reclassification; the JATS <permissions> block has to be read before the tag is changed. The coagulopathy branch has already been bitten by exactly this — COG-S001's licence cell records a source whose ali:license_ref says CC BY-SA 4.0 while the printed license-p in the same block says CC BY-NC-ND.
- extract: Run the core query over every PMCID in toxicity/coagulopathy/data/sources.csv and in thrombocytopenia/curation/rights/rights_audit.csv, and store isOpenAccess, license, authMan, inEPMC and inPMC as new columns beside the existing is_open_access and in_pmc fields (which are blank for 30 and 46 of the 70 thrombocytopenia sources). Then resolve every disagreement by reading that article's JATS <permissions> block, and for the ones that come back isOpenAccess Y with license null, read the block before changing any tag. Start with PMC8808741.
- access: OPEN — Public EMBL-EBI REST endpoint, no key, no login, no rate-limit error encountered across seven sequential queries.
- worth: MEDIUM — It is infrastructure rather than evidence, so it ranks below the leads that add H3 content or move whole row-blocks. But it is cheap, it hardens an audit that a Phase 2 reviewer will read closely, and it already produced one specific 21-row candidate. Marked MEDIUM and not HIGH because its concrete yield so far is one source, and because its main effect is to confirm existing tags rather than change them.

**PMDA Review Report, Viltepso (viltolarsen) — Japanese regulatory CMC with the chemical structure, the full base sequence and per-position base indexing**  
- identifier: `https://www.pmda.go.jp/files/000237467.pdf — Report on the Deliberation Results, 6 March 2020 (MHLW Pharmaceutical Evaluation Division) bound with the PMDA Review Report of 19 February 2020; Viltepso Intravenous Infusion 250 mg, viltolarsen (JAN), applicant Nippon Shinyaku Co., Ltd., application 26 September 2019; 81 pages, official English translation`
- gap closed: H2 and H3 for a molecule the project holds thinly: viltolarsen appears in thrombocytopenia only via "FDA NDA 212154 Orig1s000 Pharmacology/Toxicology Review (VILTEPSO)" with ONE row. Page 2 gives the complete nucleobase sequence (CCTCCGGTTCTGAAGGTGTTC, 21-mer) together with an explicit per-position indexing convention B(n) keyed to the drawn morpholino structure — that is modification position data in the form H2 asks for, from a regulator, for a Japan-origin phosphorodiamidate morpholino. For H3 it supplies the full drug-substance specification parameter list and the analytical method behind each one: identification by UV/VIS, IR and MS; related substances by HPLC; residual solvents by GC; water content; bacterial endotoxins; microbial limit; assay by HPLC. HARD LIMIT, stated plainly: the numeric acceptance criteria are masked with asterisk runs ("********", "******"), the same confidentiality redaction the project already documented for the FDA (b)(4) limits. This closes the analytical-identity and modification-position half of the gap and does NOT deliver a purity number.
- extract: Two targets in https://www.pmda.go.jp/files/000237467.pdf. (a) Page 2, the Chemical Structure figure plus its B(n) legend and the Base sequence line — transcribe the sequence and the position convention into the oligos/modifications tables. (b) Section 2, "Data Relating to Quality and Outline of the Review Conducted by PMDA", starting p.3, in particular the drug-substance specification paragraph on p.7 — record each specification parameter and its analytical method, and record the acceptance criteria as redacted-at-source with the asterisk masking quoted, exactly the convention characterization_regulatory_cmc.json already uses for the FDA (b)(4) limits.
- access: OPEN — PDF retrieved and read in full with no payment, login or access condition. BUT NOT OPENLY LICENSED: the PMDA English site footer states "Copyright © Pharmaceuticals and Medical Devices Agency, All Rights Reserved." I did not fetch the full PMDA Site Policy page (/english/0013.html), so a more permissive clause may exist that I have not seen. Treat this as freely readable, extractable as fact, NOT redistributable — so it is an H2/H3 source and must NOT be filed as an H6 open-licence replacement. Also note the document's own disclaimer: "This English translation ... is intended to serve as reference material ... In the event of any inconsistency between the Japanese original and this English translation, the Japanese original shall take precedence."
- worth: MEDIUM — It serves the two highest-ranked hit types for a thinly-held Japan-origin molecule, and it opens a regulator class the project has never touched — `git grep` finds no PMDA document number in any of the six branches. Held to MEDIUM rather than HIGH for two honest reasons: the purity numbers are masked, and the licence is All Rights Reserved, so it cannot do the H6 job this endpoint exists to do. If it is ingested, the extracted facts go in and the document does not get redistributed.

**TGA Australian Public Assessment Reports for oligonucleotide therapeutics — a real content seam that is NOT an open licence, and the counter-example the project needs**  
- identifier: `Six AusPARs located by filename and sponsor metadata: auspar-nusinersen-heptadecasodium-180813.pdf (Spinraza, Biogen Australia, Aug 2018); auspar-onpattro-230824.pdf (patisiran, Alnylam Australia, Aug 2023); auspar-amvuttra-240912.docx (vutrisiran, Medison Pharma Australia, Aug/Sep 2024); auspar-givlaari-240628.pdf (givosiran as sodium, Medison Pharma Australia, Jun 2024); auspar-inclisiran-220420.pdf (Leqvio, Novartis Australia, Mar/Apr 2022); auspar-zirdawny-260814.pdf (donidalorsen as sodium, Otsuka Australia, Aug 2026). All under https://www.tga.gov.au/sites/default/files/`
- gap closed: Two things, and the second matters more than the first. (a) Potentially H3/H2 for six oligos including donidalorsen, on which coagulopathy currently depends on the EMA Dawnzera report (COG-S082, 21 rows) tagged publisher_restricted, and vutrisiran (COG-S083, 4 rows). Unproven until a body is retrieved. (b) The correction this cross-cutting endpoint most needs: the project is about to generalise "regulator document equals government work equals public domain". It does not. The thrombocytopenia rights audit already applies "US Government work; no copyright attaches" to eight EMA documents (216 rows). On the same reasoning an AusPAR would be filed as public domain — and the TGA term expressly forbids re-transmission, distribution and commercialisation without prior written approval. EMA happens to permit commercial reuse with attribution, so that conclusion survives its wrong basis; TGA would not survive, and PMDA is All Rights Reserved. The rule to write into the licence policy: the US-Government-work rule reaches US federal agencies only; every other regulator needs its own term read and quoted, per agency.
- extract: Do NOT ingest an AusPAR as an open-licence source. Two separate actions. First, as policy: add to whatever LICENSE and rights-basis documentation the three unlicensed branches get a per-regulator licence table with the quoted term and its URL for each of FDA (US Government work), EMA (legal notice, commercial and non-commercial reuse with attribution), TGA (Copyright Act 1968, no re-transmission or distribution without written approval), PMDA (All Rights Reserved) — and stop using one basis string for all of them. Second, if the AusPAR content is wanted: retry the six URLs from a session that can reach tga.gov.au, target the "Quality findings" section, and treat any extracted number as a fact extracted from a restricted document, the same handling the 17 C_closed_access thrombocytopenia sources already get.
- access: ACCESS_UNKNOWN — No price, purchase page or paid-access requirement was observed anywhere — this is a retrieval failure, not a paywall, and must not be logged as one. What I observed was repeated HTTP 503 from WebFetch and two distinct curl transport failures. What I DID observe is a redistribution restriction, which is a different thing from a price: "You are not permitted to re-transmit, distribute or commercialise the material without prior written approval."
- worth: MEDIUM — The documents themselves are unverified in content and are not redistributable, so as a data source this is speculative and ranks low. It earns MEDIUM entirely on the licence correction it forces, which protects 216 already-extracted rows from resting on a basis a Phase 2 eligibility reviewer could puncture in one sentence, and which stops the next scout importing AusPARs as public domain.

**Not reached / could not verify:**

- TGA AusPAR document bodies. Existence verified from a retrieved listing of six specific filenames with sponsor and date metadata, but every attempt to open one failed: WebFetch returned HTTP 503 twice, curl failed with an HTTP/2 stream INTERNAL_ERROR and then an empty reply over HTTP/1.1. I have therefore NOT seen a Quality findings section in any AusPAR and make no claim that one contains specification data. No price or purchase page was observed, so this is a retrieval failure and must not be recorded as a paywall.
- Zenodo, Figshare, Dryad and OSF. The Zenodo REST records search returned no usable hit count, and I did not search Figshare, Dryad or OSF at all. So the repository-deposit class named as under-searched in my brief is genuinely unsearched by me. No deposit is proposed, and nothing should be inferred about whether one exists.
- Health Canada. A Regulatory Decision Summary for Spinraza (RDS00268, dhpp.hpfb-dgpsa.ca) appeared in a search result listing, but I did not fetch it and I did not verify Canada's Open Government Licence terms or whether the Summary Basis of Decision series carries CMC content. Unverified lead, not proposed.
- Pharmacopoeial monographs (USP, Ph. Eur., JP). Not searched. I observed no price and no access requirement for any of them, so nothing at all is claimed about their cost or availability.
- PMDA reuse terms are only partly established. I retrieved the English site footer, 'Copyright (c) Pharmaceuticals and Medical Devices Agency, All Rights Reserved', but did NOT fetch the full PMDA Site Policy at /english/0013.html. A more permissive clause may exist there that I have not seen, so the viltolarsen lead is tagged open-to-read but not-redistributable on the evidence I actually have, and that should be rechecked before the conclusion is relied on.
- NLM / ClinicalTrials.gov terms of use not independently re-verified. I retrieved trial records and AE tables myself, but the public-domain basis in my two registry leads is carried forward from the project's own existing register rather than from a terms page I read in this pass. Worth one fetch to close properly.
- NCATS GSRS not queried by me. I only established that one GSRS record already exists in the repository (hydrocephalus, trabedersen, UNII 98OYR854NY). I make no claim about GSRS coverage of other oligos, its per-position modification detail, or its licence. It remains a plausible H2 seam that someone should actually test.
- Preprints and doctoral theses not searched in this pass, despite being named as under-searched and usually openly licensed. Same for non-English material other than PMDA's own English translations.
- Row-level reconciliation of the thrombocytopenia figures. rights_audit.csv sums to 1,959 rows across 70 sources and shows 30 sources in the legacy summary_stat class, but I did not reconcile that against the 984-row summary_stat figure in my brief, nor against thrombocytopenia/data/*.csv. The licence distribution I report is what rights_audit.csv itself says; the 984 figure I did not reproduce and cannot confirm.
- Open-licence replacements for the two largest CC BY-NC blocks in thrombocytopenia remain unfound: Crooke 2017 NAT (PMC5467133, 387 rows, cc by-nc confirmed by Europe PMC) and Lundberg Slingsby in Haematologica (PMC8804562, 157 rows, cc by-nc confirmed). Both are human clinical or human ex-vivo platelet data with no public-domain equivalent that I could verify. These are the two row-blocks a further pass should attack, and I did not close them.
- No numeric purity VALUE was found under any licence. Every regulatory route I checked publishes parameters, methods, impurity taxonomies, batch counts or an identification threshold, and withholds the acceptance criteria: FDA Chemistry Review (b)(4) per the project's own file, PMDA masked with asterisk runs, EMA EPARs naming attributes without limits. The Givlaari 0.10% figure is an impurity IDENTIFICATION threshold, not a purity specification, and must not be recorded as one. The project's 3-values-in-3,034-rows H3 gap is not closed by anything in this report.
